# LEDGER — source identifications and factual claims

*Every source identification and dated fact in a chunk has a row here (CONVENTIONS §11–12). A chunk
with an open `to-verify` or a `[verify]` tag is not publishable.*

**Status:** `verified` (checked against a text; how and when) · `editor` (the Society editor's own
identification, carried over) · `to-verify` (from memory or secondhand; not yet checked) ·
`untraced` (looked for, not found; the note says so).

| V-13 | dialogus-01 n7 | "Augustine" on the king as vicar of the Godhead (also *Off. reg.* 13) | verified (corrected) | Ps.-Augustine (Ambrosiaster), *Quaestiones Veteris et Novi Testamenti*, local PL TEI 7317.xml (PL 35). q. 91: *Rex enim adoratur in terris quasi vicarius Dei* (PL 35:2284). q. 106: *habens imperium Dei quasi vicarius ejus; quia omnis rex Dei habet imaginem* (PL 35:2319). q. 35: *Dei enim imaginem habet rex, sicut et episcopus Christi* (PL 35:2234). This is exactly the *De officio regis* 13 form (king = image of God, bishop = image of Christ). q. 106 passed into Gratian under Augustine's name: C.33 q.5 c.13 *Vir est caput mulieris*, "Idem in Quaestionibus Veteris et Novi Testamenti … c. 106" (PL 187:1654A, TEI 21354.xml). It is also in Ivo, *Decretum* (PL 161:603D). That circulation explains *sepe meminit Augustinus*. Read 2026-09-29. |

| D02-1 | dialogus-02 n1 | The eagle, gazing at the sun, as a medieval figure of contemplation and of John the Evangelist | verified | Augustine, *Tract. in Ioh.* 36.5 (PL 35:1666, TEI 7323.xml): *Restat aquila: ipse est Joannes, sublimium praedicator, et lucis internae atque aeternae fixis oculis contemplator*, followed by the eaglets held up *radiis solis*. See also 40.1, *aquilae comparatum: nulla quippe avis volare altius perhibetur* (PL 35:1686). Read 2026-09-29. |
| D02-2 | dialogus-02 n3 | Augustine reads the Psalms' *montes* as the great men of the church (apostles, preachers): *En. in Ps.* 71.5, 120.4 | verified | *En. in Ps.* 71.5 (PL 36:903–904, TEI 7329.xml): *Excellenti ergo sanctitate eminentes in Ecclesia, montes sunt; qui idonei sunt et alios docere*. *En. in Ps.* 120.4 (PL 37:1607, TEI 21385.xml): *Jam illuminati sunt montes … Jam ortus est sol justitiae, jam ab Apostolis praedicatum est Evangelium … ipsi montes non a se lucent … Montes possunt intelligi, homines magni*. This matches the Dialogus's *montes* receiving influence from the *sol*. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D02-3 | dialogus-02 n5 | John 10:38 at 5.11 | editor | Pollard's margin reference *John x. 38* (plate n36); text checked with bible.py |
| D03-1 | dialogus-03 n2 | "First commandment of the second table" = Honour thy father and mother; the Augustinian division counts it fourth and Wyclif follows it; Pollard's "fifth" is the Protestant count | verified | Augustine, *Quaest. in Hept.* II.71 (PL 34:620–621, TEI 7312.xml) gives both divisions (4+6, *quorum primum est, Honora patrem et matrem*; or 3+7) and prefers 3+7: *Mihi tamen videtur congruentius accipi tria illa, et ista septem*. Wyclif, *De mandatis divinis* c. 15 (ed. Loserth, p. 152; OCR latinworks17 ll. 13852–13890) speaks of the *tria mandata prime tabule* and notes *Augustinus ponit septem mandata in secunda tabula*. Also c. 12: *iniuriando matri ecclesie contra primum mandatum secunde tabule*, the same application as the Dialogus. Pollard's side-note at p. 6 reads "The fifth commandment" (latinworks22 l. 1515). Read 2026-09-29. |

| D05-1 | dialogus-05 n7 | *Pauperes … sacerdotes* anticipates the "poor priests", the preachers associated with Wyclif | verified (corrected) | Pollard, intro pp. xiv–xv (latinworks22 ll. 485–540) quotes this very sentence (p. 10, l. 23 sqq.) and discusses it as a reference to "measures against the 'Poor Priests'". He concludes that the Poor Priests "must have both begun their work, and met with resistance" earlier than supposed. His chronology dates their activity from 1378 (p. xiii n.). His index, s.v. "Poor Priests (pauperes et fideles sacerdotes), persecuted, 10, 23". So the passage *refers to* the poor priests, and "anticipates" is wrong. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D05-2 | dialogus-05 n7 | In canon law the secular arm carried out the church's sentences on heretics | verified | Lucius III, *Ad abolendam* (Verona 1184), PL 201:1298C–1299A (ep. 171, TEI 11606.xml). A convicted cleric *saecularis relinquatur arbitrio potestatis, animadversione debita puniendus*; a layman *saecularis judicis arbitrio relinquatur, debitam recepturus … ultionem*; relapsed heretics *saeculari judicio … decernimus relinquendos*. The X 5.7.9 number was not checked (no Liber Extra locally); the note does not cite it. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D07-1 | 07 / n4 | *pertinaciter*: pertinacity makes error heresy in the canonists' definition (Gratian C.24 q.3 c.31, from Augustine) | verified (corrected) | Gratian, PL 187:1306C–1307B (TEI 21354.xml). The "pertinacity" text is **c.29** (Augustine, ep. 43, inscribed "epist. CLXII"): those who defend a false opinion *nulla pertinaci animositate* … *nequaquam sunt inter haereticos deputandi*. **c.31** (Augustine, inscribed *contra Manichaeos*) gives the positive form: *si correpti … resistunt contumaciter … haeretici fiunt*. The ledger reference should read "C.24 q.3 cc.29, 31". Read 2026-09-29. |
| D08-1 | 08 / n2 | *patrimonium crucifixi* a current name for church goods held by the clergy as stewards of the poor | verified | Phrase attested widely in PL (16 files). Examples: ps.-Bernard, *Ad pastores in synodo* (PL 184:1092B), *abutimini eleemosynis pauperum? De patrimonio Crucifixi non facitis codices…*; Joannes de Deo (canonist), *Poenitentiale* excerpts (PL 99:1107A), clergy who give to hunters and jesters *quam pauperibus de patrimonio Crucifixi … contra ecclesiasticas sanctiones*; Peter of Blois, *Compendium in Job* (PL 207:799A); John of Salisbury, *Epistolae* (PL 199). Read 2026-09-29. |
| D08-2 | 08 / n5 | the "three hundred years" run to the Constantine/Sylvester endowment, treated elsewhere as the poison entering the church | verified | Dialogus p. 16 (`text/dialogus/p016.la.txt`): *lex Christi … per trecentos annos tantummodo observata*. Wyclif, *De potestate pape* c. 9 (ed. Loserth, p. 198; OCR latinworks19 ll. 14524–14540): *hostis antiquus facta per Constantinum hac publica largicione ecclesiis legitur publice in aere proclamasse 'hodie infusum est venenum in ecclesia'* (MS margin: *venenum infusum est in ecclesiam Dei*). The next lines speak of a heretic pope *tercius post Silvestrum*. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D09-1 | 09 / n9 | chief lord's leave for a grant compared with mortmain (Statute of Mortmain 1279) | verified | *Statutum de viris religiosis*, 7 Edw. I (1279), Statutes of the Realm translation via Wikisource "Mortmain Act 1279". The recital: religious men "should not enter into the Fees of any without Licence and Will of the chief Lord, of whom such Fees be holden immediately". On alienation into mortmain, "it shall be lawful to us and other chief Lords of the Fee immediate, to enter into the Land so aliened, within a Year". Read 2026-09-29. |
| D10-1 | 10 / n6 | Augustine: "Si Christus hoc asserit ve illi qui non credit" (20.17) | verified (corrected) | Augustine, *En. in Ps.* 66.1 (PL 36:802, TEI 7329.xml; the TEI lacks a col. 802 anchor, and the passage stands immediately before `<pb n="0803"/>`): *Si ego dico, nemo credat: si Christus dicit, vae qui non credit*. Wyclif quotes freely (*asserit*, *ve illi*). Read 2026-09-29. |
| D11-1 | 11 / n6 | *duo pape … electi* = the double election of 1378 (Urban VI / Clement VII), start of the Great Schism | verified | Dialogus p. 22: *si duo pape ex seminacione sathane electi fuerint … ex tali scismate*. Pollard's chronology, intro p. xiii n. 1 (latinworks22 ll. 455–463): "Protests of Cardinals against Urban's election, July — … Clement VII elected Anti-Pope, Sept. 20". Pollard dates the Dialogus by this "fresh scandal" (pp. xvi–xvii). Nuance: Clement was elected at Fondi and settled at Avignon only in 1379. "Clement VII at Avignon" names his seat and is acceptable. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D12-1 | 12 / n1 (now n2) | *litere fraternitatum* = friars' letters granting lay benefactors a share in the order's prayers | verified | Dialogus p. 23: *literis fraternitatum de suffragio spiritali*. Pollard, index s.v. "Fraternity, letters of (promising their purchasers the prayers of the brother[hood])" and intro p. [x] ("letters of bishops and of fraternity"); latinworks22 ll. 206–208, 9305. Friars: the Dialogus p. 26 (*literas fraternitatum*, sold by those "who do not know whether they are … foreknown") is in the anti-friar context of ch. 13 n7. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D12-2 | 12 / n4 (now n5) | Boethius, "whatever opinion anyone holds is faith to him" | untraced | Searched Boethius in local PL 64 (TEI 7781 *De diff. top.*, 7778 *In Top. Cic.*) for *fides*/*opinio*. Nearest candidate: the definition *Argumentum est ratio rei dubiae faciens fidem* (*De diff. top.* I, PL 64:1174C; *In Top. Cic.* I, PL 64:1156D), where *fides* is the conviction any probable argument produces. *De diff. top.* I (PL 64:1180–81, TEI l. 529ff.) speaks of *fides* resting *in opinione hominum*. Not found as Wyclif's wording. Next: Aquinas / Lombard-commentators on *credere* taken *large* "secundum Boethium" (e.g. Aquinas, *In III Sent.* d.23; *De veritate* q.14 a.1–2); Wyclif's own logic works on *credere*. The note already says "unidentified", so no text change is needed. |
| D12-3 | 12 / n5 (now n6) | the "childish principle" (grant, deny, doubt) = the respondent's rule in *de obligationibus* | verified | Dialogus p. 24: *famosum puerile principium quod omnis proposicio proposita sit concedenda, neganda, vel dubitanda*. Standard reference: Uckelman, Maat & Rybalko, "The Art of Doubting in *Obligationes Parisienses*" (ILLC preprint 2008, eprints.illc.uva.nl/id/document/791), on "the tripartite answering scheme (concede, deny, doubt)" of obligational disputation, with the Latin rules for *dubitatio*. SEP "Medieval Theories of Obligationes" treats grant/deny as the main responses and doubt as a third. Burley's own text was not read (Green's edition is not online); if a primary is wanted, Burley, *De obligationibus*, ed. Green, or Spade's translation. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D13-1 | 13 / n3 | Wyclif dates the loosing of Satan (Apoc. 20:7) to c. 1000 | verified | Wyclif, *De solutione Satanae* c. 1 (Polemical Works, ed. Buddensieg, II p. 395; OCR latinworks29 ll. 988–990): *quod erit post mille annos ad literam post ascensionem domini, tunc sathanas est solutus*. On p. 392 he discusses the friars' "first millennium / second millennium" argument. Dialogus p. 25: *nec in alicujus pape sapiencia antequam satanas est solutus*. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D13-2 | 13 / n6 | a bishop's indulgence limited to 40 days (Lateran IV c. 62) | verified | Lateran IV c. 62 *Cum ex eo* (Schroeder trans., Fordham Medieval Sourcebook, sourcebooks.fordham.edu/basis/lateran4.asp): "on the occasion of the dedication of a church an indulgence of not more than one year be granted … and on the anniversary of the dedication the remission granted for penances enjoined is not to exceed forty days. We command also that in each case this number of days be made the rule in issuing letters of indulgences". Dialogus p. 25: *literis episcopalibus concedentibus 40 dies venie*. (The one-year allowance at a church's dedication is the exception.) Read 2026-09-29. |
| D14-1 | 14 / n7 | Corpus Christi office, 1st nocturn, lesson 2: accidents without a subject | editor | Pollard margin "In primo nocturno, Lectio II."; wording to-verify against the breviary |
| D14-2 | 14 / n8 | Aug., *De serm. Dom. in monte* I.20.65: the apocryphal story of Thomas, the servant and the dog | verified | Augustine, *De sermone Domini in monte* I.20.65 (cap. XX begins at §62), PL 34:1262–1263 (TEI 7307.xml): *in illis libris … ubi scriptum est apostolum Thomam imprecatum cuidam, a quo palma percussus esset … cujus a leone occisi … discerptam manum canis intulit mensis in quibus convivabatur Apostolus. Cui scripturae licet nobis non credere; non est enim in catholico canone*. This matches the note. Augustine tells the same story in *Contra Adimantum* 17.2 (PL 42:158, TEI 21356.xml). Read 2026-09-29. |
| D15-1 | 15 / n8 | Cyprian "si solus Christus" = *Ad Caecilium* §14 (Ep. 63), cited through Gratian D.8 (B, B¹, C "decimo capitulo") | verified | Cyprian, Ep. 63 *Ad Caecilium* §14, PL 4:385A (TEI 130.xml; the heading reads "EPISTOLA LXIII … AD CAECILIUM", with Erasmus's numbering II, 3): *Quare, si solus Christus audiendus est, non debemus attendere…*. Gratian, D.8 **c.9** *Dei veritatem, non hominum consuetudinem sequi oportet*, "Idem lib. II, ep. 3, ad Caecilium", PL 187:48B (TEI 21354.xml). In PL 187 the text before c.9 is an unnumbered excerpt, *Idem in epistola ad Jubaianum*. A count that treats it as a separate chapter makes *Si solus* the tenth, which explains the MSS' *decimo capitulo* and Pollard's supplement [*decimo*]. The note gives no canon number, so no change is needed. If one is wanted: D.8 c.9 (PL 187:48B). Read 2026-09-29. |
| D15-2 | 15 / n10 | Possidius, *Vita Augustini*: Augustine willing to give Hippo's possessions back to its citizens | verified (corrected) | Possidius, *Vita Augustini* c. 23, PL 32:53 (TEI 7297.xml): *paratum se esse illis cedere … Sed nunquam id laici suscipere voluerunt*. Wyclif tells the story again in the Epilogue, Pollard p. 88 (latinworks22 ll. 6878–6886), where Pollard's margin reads "Possidius Vita Aug. c 23". The note has no chapter; add "23". Read 2026-09-29. |
| D15-3 | 15 / n10 | Jerome refusing possessions, life outside the cloister, clerical estates | untraced | Pollard gives no reference at p. 31 (latinworks22 ll. 3370–3380). *extraclaustral-* occurs nowhere else in the Wyclif OCR. The nearest Jerome texts, read in PL 22 (TEI 7132.xml): Ep. 52.5 to Nepotian, where the cleric whose portion is the Lord *nihil extra Dominum habere potest … si possessiones … Dominus pars ejus fieri non dignabitur* (PL 22:531); and Ep. 58.5 to Paulinus, *Sin autem cupis esse … Monachus, id est, solus, quid facis in urbibus?* (PL 22:582–583). No text found in which Jerome himself refuses possessions. The claim is probably Wyclif's own summary of these letters. Next: Wyclif, *De civili dominio* and *De ecclesia* on Jerome; Gratian C.12 q.1 (Jerome excerpts). The note should say it is untraced (see the D15-2 correction). |
| D17-1 | 17 / n1 | "fourth commandment" = honour father and mother (Exod. 20:12) in the Augustinian count | verified | Augustine, *Quaest. in Hept.* II.71, PL 34:620–621 (TEI 7312.xml), gives both divisions and prefers 3 + 7 (*Mihi tamen videtur congruentius accipi tria illa, et ista septem*). On that count *Honora patrem et matrem*, the first of the second table, is the fourth. Wyclif follows it: *De mandatis divinis* c. 22 opens *redeundum est ad primum mandatum secunde tabule, quod est: Honora patrem tuum et matrem tuam* (latinworks17 ll. 25112–25115). The Dialogus text reads *ex quarto mandato decalogi* (p. 33). Exod. 20:12 checked with bible.py. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D19-1 | 19 / n1 | *quatuor sectas cleri* = endowed secular clergy, monks, canons, friars | verified | Wyclif, *De quattuor sectis novellis* c. 1 (Polemical Works I, ed. Buddensieg, p. 243; latinworks28 ll. 18745–18790): *Sacerdos autem cesareus … intelliguntur … dotati presbiteri, quicunque seculares clerici … dotati*. The MS marginal note there reads *Secte quatuor: Sacerdos cesareus, Monachus, Canonicus, Frater*. See also *De Christo et suo adversario Antichristo* c. 2 (Pol. Works II p. 656; latinworks29 ll. 12879–12887): *Sunt autem iste secte quatuor, ut sepe dictum est, clerus cesareus, monachi, canonici atque fratres*. That text says the Caesarean clergy's *patronus* is the pope, which fits the Dialogus's *curie romane* (p. 37). Read 2026-09-29. |
| D19-2 | 19 / n1 | *quatuor sectas fratrum* = Franciscans, Dominicans, Carmelites, Austin friars | verified | Pollard, index (latinworks22 ll. 9339–9342): "Friars, the four sects of (Carmelites, Augustinians, Jacobites or Dominicans, and Franciscans), 37, 27; 48, 6". Here 37, 27 is this very passage. His index s.v. CAIM (ll. 8912–8920) quotes Wyclif, *Trialogus* IV.33: *C. Carmelitas, A. Augustinenses, I. Iacobitas et M. Minores significat*. Buddensieg quotes the same passage (latinworks28 ll. 8550–8556). Read 2026-09-29. |
| D20-1 | 20 / n1 | black canons = Augustinian, white = Premonstratensian; *boni homines* = the English Bonshommes | verified | Pollard gives nothing. *Catholic Encyclopedia*, "Canons and Canonesses Regular" (newadvent.org/cathen/03288a.htm): "AUGUSTINIAN CANONS, BLACK CANONS"; "The Premonstratensians wear a white habit". VCH Derbs. II, Beauchief (british-history.ac.uk/vch/derbs/vol2/pp63-69): "for Premonstratensian or White Canons". VCH Bucks. I, Ashridge (british-history.ac.uk/vch/bucks/vol1/pp386-390): "The College of Bonhommes at Ashridge, was founded in 1283 … It was the only house of this order in England, except the small college at Edington in Wiltshire". The brethren were professed to the rule of St Augustine. VCH Wilts. III, Edington (…/vch/wilts/vol3/pp320-324): converted into a house of Bonhommes in March 1358. Fetched 2026-09-29. |
| D20-2 | 20 / n5 | *istas 8 species* = the eight kinds of religious listed at 39.4–8 | verified | Pollard pp. 39–40 (latinworks22 ll. 3918–3992). The list at 39.4–8 runs: *canonici regulares, nigri* (1) *et albi* (2); *fratres hospitales* (3); *monachi cartusienses* (4); *fratres boni homines* (5); *sanctimoniales* (6); *sororisse* (7); *cum anacoritis et ceteris inclusis* (8). That makes exactly eight if black and white canons count separately and anchorites and other recluses count as one class, as *ceteris* suggests. The note's hedge ("does not count out to eight unambiguously") is fair but could be replaced by this count. p. 40 l. 1: *per istas 8 species*. Read 2026-09-29. |

The same point recurs at p. 3 (*has tres partes … Media autem pars que est domini temporales*). p. 40 (*sectam unicam militantem et ipsam habere tres partes*) refers back to it. Read 2026-09-29. |

Works II p. 656; latinworks29 ll. 12883–12885): *Secunda secta dicitur esse monachi … cuius patronus dicitur esse Benedictus, et regula, quam ex eius sentencia beatus Gregorius compilavit*. So the identification is Wyclif's own, and "presumably" can go. Read 2026-09-29. |

| D23-4 | 23 / n11 | Gratian C.3 q.7, *Si quis in gravibus* | verified (corrected) | Gratian, C.3 q.7 **c.5** *Gravatus criminibus judicare non valet*, inscribed "Gregorius praefationis in Job", PL 187:694A (TEI 21354.xml; Causa III at l. 27157, q. VII at l. 28185). Incipit: *In gravibus peccatis quis positus, dum suis premitur, aliena non diluit. § 1. Cuncti enim liquido novimus, quia, quum is displicet, qui ad intercedendum mittitur, irati animus proculdubio ad deteriora provocatur*. This is the same Gregory sentence the Dialogus has just quoted (relevant to D23-3). The canon's incipit is *In gravibus*; Wyclif's *si quis* is loose. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D23-5 | 23 / n12 | Augustine, *En. in Ps.* 148.2 (paraphrase + *vinolencia*) | verified (corrected) | The quotation is **146.2**, not 148.2. *En. in Ps.* 146.2, PL 37:1899–1900 (TEI 21385.xml; the heading "IN PSALMUM CXLVI" at l. 61342) reads: *et cibus tuus et potus tuus laudat Deum: si vero modum naturae debitum immoderatione voracitatis excedas, et vinolentia te ingurgites; quantaslibet laudes Dei lingua tua sonet, vita blasphemat*. The preceding sentences give the paraphrase (praise by works; *opere tuo laudas Deum*). *En. in Ps.* 148.2 (PL 37:1938) makes the same general point (*Non cesset bene vivere, et semper laudat Deum … lingua tua tacet, vita tua clamat*) but has no *vinolentia* sentence. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D23-6 | 23 / n13 | "four sects" = Wyclif's usual count of the orders | verified (corrected) | Pollard indexes this passage (*plures oratores de hiis sectis 4or*, p. 48 l. 6) under "Friars, the four sects of (Carmelites, Augustinians, Jacobites or Dominicans, and Franciscans), 37, 27; 48, 6" (latinworks22 ll. 9339–9342). Wyclif's usual "four sects" (*ut sepe dictum est*) are the Caesarean clergy, monks, canons and friars (*De Christo et suo adversario Antichristo* c. 2; *De quattuor sectis novellis* c. 1; see D19-1). The note's "count of the orders" is ambiguous between the two readings; name both. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D24-1 | 24 / n5 | *dotacione ecclesie presumpta per cesarem* (49.17) = Constantine's endowment | verified | Dialogus p. 49: *cum hoc nomen papa sit terminus extra fidem scripture, videtur quod in dotacione ecclesie presumpta per cesarem est inventum*. p. 72 uses the same verb of Constantine: *dicitur constantinum anglicum istam vesaniam diabolicam presumpsisse*. See also p. 18 *stulta donacio* (D04-1). Pollard's intro p. xiv (latinworks22 ll. 648–654) discusses p. 49 and notes that the Pope's pre-eminence "is traced to the interference of Constantine (pp. 82, 83)". His index, s.v. "Constantine, Donation of, 63, 12; 72, 14". Read 2026-09-29. |
| D26-1 | 26 / n2 | Falsehood's eucharistic "heresy" (53.19–20) = the controversy in which Wyclif's teaching was condemned at Oxford, 1381 | verified (corrected) | Pollard, intro p. vi (latinworks22 ll. 121–125): Mendacium "is content to use such very Wyclifite language as *ut patet de heresi in eucharistia et aliis similibus que in fine temporum diabolus spissius seminavit*". So Pollard reads the "heresy" as the doctrine Wyclif calls heresy: the host as *accidens … sine subjecto*, which Truth names at 54.23–25 (*ut reserant nove secte*). Pollard pp. xix–xx (ll. 721–803) calls p. 53 l. 19 an "unimportant reference" and dates the *Dialogus* to 1379. It therefore predates the Oxford condemnation by Chancellor William Berton's council. Pollard p. xix n. 1 even doubts the *Fasciculi zizaniorum* date of summer 1381 for the twelve theses, noting the Confessio heading altered to 1380. So the note cannot be right that Falsehood refers to the condemnation of 1381. At most, read in character, he refers to Wyclif's teaching, already in dispute. Replacement text is below. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D29-1 | 29 / n1 | *in manum mortuam* = mortmain; land to the church only by licence (Statute of Mortmain 1279) | verified | *Statutum de viris religiosis*, 7 Edw. I (1279), Statutes of the Realm translation (Wikisource "Mortmain Act 1279 (unsourced)"). It recites that religious men "should not enter into the Fees of any without Licence and Will of the chief Lord". It enacts that no one "buy or sell any Lands or Tenements … whereby such Lands or Tenements may any wise come into Mortmain", under pain of forfeiture. The statute forbids alienation outright; licensing was the practice that followed. That practice is the subject of P. Brand, "The Mortmain Licensing System, 1280–1307" (title and abstract seen, not read). "Only by licence" is therefore right in substance. Fetched 2026-09-29. |
| D30-1 | 30 / n6 | *castra caynitica* = "Caim's castles", CAIM the initials of the four mendicant orders | verified | Pollard, index s.v. "Castra caynitica, of the friars' houses … 65, 11" (latinworks22 ll. 8903–8920). He suggests the true reading is *Caimitica* and quotes Wyclif, *Trialogus* IV.33: *Alii autem fingunt quod in Caym fuerunt istae sectae quattuor inchoatae. Et in testimonium istorum quattuor litterae huius nominis Caim inchoant hos quattuor ordines … ita quod C. Carmelitas, A. Augustinenses, I. Iacobitas et M. Minores significat*. Pollard's side-note 65.12 reads "'Caim's Castles'". *Trialogus* (ed. Lechler) itself was not read; the passage is Pollard's verbatim quotation. Optional improvement: the note could add "(Wyclif, *Trialogus* IV.33, quoted by Pollard, index s.v. *Castra*)". Read 2026-09-29. |
| D30-2 | 30 / n20 | an English king seized a bishop's goods for keeping royal office instead of residing | untraced | Pollard does not identify it. His side-note 68.14 says only "according to the precedent of an English king", and his index has only "Confiscation of Endowments, 68, 14". Searched all 33 Wyclif Society OCR volumes for *confiscass-/confiscavit* (only this passage) and for Winchester/Wykeham (nothing relevant). Wykeham (temporalities seized Nov. 1376; convocation of Feb. 1377 resisted) fits *cum cleri repugnancia* but not the stated ground, non-residence while in royal office. Stratford (1340–1) fits neither well. Next: Wyclif's *De officio regis* and *De civili dominio* on "Caesarean clergy"; ODNB Wykeham; Holmes, *The Good Parliament*. The note already says "not identified", so no change is needed. |
| D30-3 | 30 / n22 | John XXII, *Cum inter nonnullos* (1323), against the absolute poverty of Christ | verified | Latin text, Franciscan Archive (franciscan-archive.org/bullarium/qinn-l.html): *assertionem huiusmodi pertinacem … erroneam fore censendam et haereticam*, i.e. the assertion that Christ and the apostles had nothing *in speciali* or *in communi*. Dated *II Idus Novembris* 1323 (12 Nov.). Extrav. Ioann. XXII tit. 14 c. 4/5 *De verborum significatione*. Read 2026-09-29. |

| D31-2 | 31 / n12 | "Augustine": king = vicar of the Godhead, priest = vicar of Christ's humanity | verified (corrected) | Same source as V-13 (ch. 01 n7), confirmed against the ch. 31 wording. Ambrosiaster, *Quaest. VNT*, local TEI 7317.xml (PL 35). q. 35: *Dei enim imaginem habet rex, sicut et episcopus Christi* (PL 35:2234). q. 106: *habens imperium Dei quasi vicarius ejus; quia omnis rex Dei habet imaginem* (PL 35:2319). q. 91: *Rex enim adoratur in terris quasi vicarius Dei* (PL 35:2284). Gratian C.33 q.5 c.13 carries q. 106 under Augustine's name (V-13). Ch. 01 (Pollard p. 4, latinworks22 ll. 1212–1222) gives the same pairing: *sepe meminit augustinus quomodo rex est vicarius deitatis. Sacerdos autem … vicarius humanitatis domini jhu Christi*. Ambrosiaster pairs king/God with bishop/Christ; the "humanity" is Wyclif's sharpening. Remove "probably" and cite as in ch. 01 n7. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D31-3 | 31 / n14 | English Constantine = Constantine the Great, British-born in legend | verified | Geoffrey of Monmouth, *Historia regum Britanniae* V.6–7 (Giles trans., *Six Old English Chronicles*, en.wikisource "Geoffrey's British History/Book 5"). After Coel's death Constantius became king and "married the daughter of Coel, whose name was Helena"; their son Constantine was born of this marriage. Constantius "died at York, and bestowed the kingdom upon his son". V.8: Constantine takes Rome and the empire. Pollard's index lists 72.14 (this line) under "Constantine, Donation of". Latin text not read; the translation was. Read 2026-09-29. |
| D31-4 | 31 / n15 | "half the empire" = the Donation of Constantine | verified | *Constitutum Constantini* in Gratian D.96 c.14 (Palea), PL 187:464A–B (TEI 21354.xml): *tam palatium nostrum … quam Romanam urbem, et omnes Italiae seu occidentalium regionum provincias … Silvestro universali Papae contradimus atque relinquimus*. § 7: the emperor moves his own rule *in orientalibus … regionibus* to Byzantium. So the West went to the pope and the East stayed with the emperor: half the empire. Also D.96 c.13: *coronam, et omnem regiam dignitatem in urbe Romana, et in Italia, et in partibus occidentalibus Apostolico concessit*. Pollard, index s.v. "Constantine, Donation of, … 72, 14" (the same passage, 72.14–22). Read 2026-09-29. |
| D31-5 | 31 / n16 | King John's tribute of 1,000 marks, withheld from 1366 | verified (corrected) | 1,000 marks is correct: John's concession, 15 May 1213 (Henderson, *Select Historical Documents* pp. 430–431, via Fordham Medieval Sourcebook source/john1a.asp). The Roman church is to receive "yearly a thousand marks sterling … seven hundred, namely, for the kingdom of England, and three hundred for the kingdom of Ireland". "Withheld from 1366" is wrong: payment had already lapsed about 1333. In 1366 Urban V demanded the arrears ("thirty-three years in arrear") and Parliament refused and repudiated the tribute (*Catholic Encyclopedia*, "Edward III", newadvent.org/cathen/05321a.htm). Rot. Parl. ii (40 Edw. III) not read; if a primary is wanted, read it there. Pollard, index s.v. "Edward III, detention of pope's tribute in reign of, 72, 29". Read 2026-09-29. |

| D35-2 | 35 / n4 | *patrimonium crucifixi* plays on "patrimony of St Peter" | verified (corrected) | The papal lands were called "patrimony of St Peter": Paschal II, *Ep.*, PL 163:423C (TEI 10794.xml), *patrimonium beati Petri, quae potuit occupavit*; also the Alexandrine cardinals to Frederick I (TEI 11591.xml). *Patrimonium crucifixi* is not Wyclif's coinage, though. D08-1 found it current for church goods held for the poor (ps.-Bernard PL 184:1092B, Peter of Blois PL 207:799A). Wyclif's point (83.3–5) is that papal lordship is called *patrimonium crucifixi* "as if by hereditary right". The note's "turns the name … onto the crucified Christ" credits him with the substitution. Reword. Read 2026-09-29. |
| DEA-1 | epil-a / n8 | the devil sent Pilate's wife's dream | verified | Paschasius Radbertus, *Expositio in Matthaeum* XII, PL 120:937D–938A (TEI 9114.xml): *nonnulli dicunt hanc visionem a diabolo immissam, et ab eo ipsam flagellatam, sentiens jam forte passione Christi se superari, ac mundum salvari. Idcirco egerit arte sua ut Christus liberaretur per mulierem*. It then gives the alternative view, *Alii vero dicunt bonitate Dei factum fuisse*. This is exactly Wyclif's reading, the devil fearing the Passion would save the faithful. Read 2026-09-29. |
| DEA-2 | epil-a / n10 | the king revokes grants made in his minority | untraced | The minority rule was not traced to a text. The context is **prescription** (*sic non potest currere efficax et salubris prescripcio coram deo. Quia ut dicunt de privilegiis regis Anglie, quod sit semper iuxta etatem potens iustificare iniuriam prius factam*). That points first to the royal privilege that time does not run against the king: Bracton, *De legibus*, ed. Woodbine–Thorne ii. 58 (amesfoundation.law.harvard.edu, Latin v2 p. 58), *nec in primo casu nec in isto currit tempus contra regem* (royal franchises such as wreck and treasure). *Iuxta etatem* stays obscure: "at every age"? or the nonage rule? Next: Henry III's 1227 declaration voiding charters issued in his minority (Roger of Wendover s.a. 1227). A note revision is recommended (see below). Read 2026-09-29. |
| DEA-3 | epil-a / n12 | Aristotle, *Eth.* VI.2, Agathon | verified | *EN* VI.2, 1139b8–11 (Perseus canonical-greekLit tlg0086.tlg010.perseus-grc2): *τὸ δὲ γεγονὸς οὐκ ἐνδέχεται μὴ γενέσθαι· διὸ ὀρθῶς Ἀγάθων· μόνου γὰρ αὐτοῦ καὶ θεὸς στερίσκεται, ἀγένητα ποιεῖν ἅσσʼ ἂν ᾖ πεπραγμένα*. | DEA-4 | epil-a / n14–15 | Magna Carta c.1, coronation oath, excommunications, Becket | verified | (1) Magna Carta 1297 c. 1 (legislation.gov.uk aep/Edw1cc1929/25/9/section/I): "the Church of England shall be free, and shall have all her whole Rights and Liberties inviolable". (2) Coronation oath of 1308 (text via thehistoryofengland.co.uk, no edition named; standard text Statutes of the Realm i. 168): "preserve to God and Holy Church … entire peace" and the "liberties granted to the clergy and people by … the sainted Edward". (3) Excommunication of infringers: *Confirmatio Cartarum* 1297 c. 4 (constitution.org): "all archbishops and bishops shall pronounce the sentence of excommunication against all those that … do contrary to the foresaid charters", published twice yearly. The "beyond the sea" sentences (papal) were not separately traced. (4) Becket: Alexander III to Thomas, praising the constancy *quem pro libertate Ecclesiae tuae conservanda induisse dignosceris* (Ep. 772, PL 200:710C, TEI 11589.xml). His canonization bull *Redolet Anglia* (PL 200:901D–902A) speaks of his *martyrio*. Read 2026-09-29. |

| DEA-8 | epil-a / n32 | Averroes: truth self-consonant; truth rubbed, fragrant | untraced | The Aristotle cross-reference is confirmed: *EN* I.8, 1098b11–12, *τῷ μὲν γὰρ ἀληθεῖ πάντα συνᾴδει τὰ ὑπάρχοντα* (Perseus grc2). *APr.* I.32 (47a8) was not read; no text was reachable. No Averroes locus found. Wyclif uses the same dictum without naming Averroes: *veritas secundum philosophos testatur sibi ipsi omnibus modis* (*Sermones* I, latinworks30 l. 5982; also latinworks27 l. 18772, latinworks32 l. 38550), and Loserth gives no source. A web search reports a Wyclif *Trialogus* instance naming Averroes (not seen). The "rubbed truth grows fragrant" figure was not found in PL (searched *fricat-* near *redolet/fragr-/odor*). Next: Averroes, *In Ethic.* I (Latin, Juntine vol. 3), comment on 1098b11; *Trialogus* (Lechler). The note's "cf." wording already hedges it, so no change is needed. |

| E01h-5 | ecclesia-01-h / n11 | *Moralia* IV: patriarchs' virtues | verified | Wyclif's *IV Moralium cap. XXXII* is right. *Moralia* IV.32.63 (on Job 3:16, *abortivum absconditum*), PL 75:671C–672A. The Maurist page 132 is printed in the PL margin at 671B, so this is Loserth's "Opp. I, 132". Here Gregory lists the just from the world's beginning: Abel, Enoch, Noah, Abraham, Isaac, Jacob. Wyclif's virtue terms are not from this passage. Nine of his twelve come from the Preface 6.13 (PL 75:524B–C): *innocentiam* Abel, *munditiam* Enoch, *longanimitatem spei* Noah, *obedientiam* Abraham, *conjugalis vitae castimoniam* Isaac, *laboris tolerantiam* Jacob, *mansuetudinem* Moses, *fiduciam* Joshua, *patientiam* Job. David's humility and Elijah's zeal are in X.6.9 (PL 75:925A–B): *Haec David … humilitate commovit*, *Haec Eliam, quia fervoris zelo*. Joseph's chastity is in neither passage (the Preface has *bonae retributionis gratiam*). (close-out 2026-10-03) |

Scripture* (B *quinto*) | verified | Book 6 = *De veritate*, and its chs. 16–18 are the treatise on lying: A of *De ver.* has the rubric *materia de mendacio* at c. 16, and c. 18 opens *Sed faciendo finem in materia de mendacio*; c. 16 argues that God does not deceive (*quod fallat peccatores*). Wyclif cites the same *tractatus De Mendacio* in this work at 159 (08-a). The chapter number after *capitulo* is wanting in the print. X* | verified | John 10 itself, the chapter being expounded: 10:29 *Pater meus quod dedit mihi, maius omnibus est* (the "gift" Wyclif reads as the Spirit) and 10:30 *Ego et Pater unum sumus* (Vulgate checked). It is not this work's chapter 10 (204–27, the Westminster privilege): that chapter has no passage on the Spirit. The church's union with Christ by the Spirit is chapter 1 (3, 01-b: *amore spiritus sancti Christo in matrimonio perpetuo copulatur*). (close-out 2026-10-03) |

Wyclif's *dux et legifer* is an attested reading of this antiphon: Thomas Cisterciensis, *In Cant.* VI, quotes *O Emmanuel, dux et legifer noster, exspectatio gentium* (PL 206:616A). Cf. O-antiphon 2, *O Adonay, et dux domus Israel* (col. clv), and the Sarum Advent Lauds antiphon *Dominus legifer noster, Dominus rex noster* (Isa. 33:22; feria v, third week, col. cxxii). | E06c-4 | ecclesia-06-c / n4 | *terrena despicere et amare celestia*: an Advent postcommunion | verified | Sarum postcommunion, Second Sunday of Advent (Legg p. 17): *Refecti cibo potuque spiritualis alimonie supplices te domine deprecamur ut huius participacione misterii doceas nos terrena despicere et amare celestia*. The Gregorian has it *Ad complendum*, Hebd. III ante Natalem (PL 78:192A, *Repleti cibo*). The same Sunday's Sarum collect has *ut per eius aduentum* (Legg p. 17), which is Wyclif's *per adventum eius*. (close-out 2026-10-03) |
| E06c-5 | ecclesia-06-c / n7 | *ut canit ecclesia*: office of the dedication of a church | verified | Sarum, Dedication of a church. (1) Sequence *Jerusalem et Syon filie* (an option at Legg p. 203; text pp. 473–74): *Christus enim desponsat hodie matrem nostram … ecclesiam*; *sponsa sponsi letatur gracia a reginis laudis cum gloria felix dicta* (Cant. 6:8, Wyclif's *reginas … concubinas … adolescentulas*); *Huc uenit austri regina salomonis*; *Hec futura prefigura reserauit*. Other sequences offered: *Rex Salomon*, *Psallat ecclesia*. (2) Matins lessons iv–vi: the queen of Sheba coming to Solomon is the church of the gentiles coming to Christ, *verus pacificus*; with R. *Benedic, Domine, domum istam quam aedificavi* (Procter–Wordsworth I, cols. mcccclvi–lix). (3) Octave lessons on the temple's measures as figures of the church (col. mcccclxxxviii). This is a thematic identification; there is no verbal quotation. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

The ordinary gloss on 3.49.10 says *Inter alia* excepts *tantum duae personae* and C.17 q.4 *Sicut antiquitus* only the *publicus latro* (glossed Decretals 1588, II t. 2); Hostiensis, *Summa aurea* III, *De immunitate ecclesiarum*, likewise *duo genera tantum hominum excipiuntur*. Four results only if killers and maimers in church are counted apart. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

Nearest: X 2.19.7 (Innocent III, 1206), the ordinary's letters *quibus standum est, donec probetur contrarium*, close to Wyclif's second clause (the lower judge's judgment stands). The maxim is the glossators'; not traced to a text. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

Buddensieg II, 212.5–15): *cum in cruce suspensus esset, velum templi scissum est … Cristi, qui in despectum temporalium pauperem vitam vixit, et demum nude crucifixus est*, against clergy *qui plus inhiabant temporalibus quam spiritualibus*: the same veil, nakedness and contempt of temporal goods. The chapter number stays lost. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

| E10d-4 | ecclesia-10-d / n8 | the coronation-oath clause on doing justice | verified | 1308 coronation oath (Edward II, the form in use after him): "Sire, will you, so far as in you lies, cause justice to be rendered rightly, impartially, and wisely, in compassion and in truth?" Wyclif's *non negabit sed concedet iusticiam cuicunque* paraphrases it, in words nearer Magna Carta 1297 c. 29, "we will not deny or defer to any man either Justice or Right" (legislation.gov.uk). (close-out 2026-10-03) |
| E10d-5 | ecclesia-10-d / n12 | Wyclif's thesis condemned by Gregory XI (1377) | verified | The proposition *Si Deus est, domini temporales possunt legitime ac meritorie auferre bona fortune ab ecclesia delinquente* is art. 6 of the *Conclusiones* condemned in Gregory XI's bulls of May 1377 (Poole, *De civ. dom.* I p. 267 n. 12, citing Walsingham i. 354, 359). Wyclif defends it as no. VI in his *Libellus* to parliament (*FZ* p. 248, read). 2026-10-03. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

| E11a-5 | ecclesia-11-a / n6 | the bull "of Nicholas III" to Westminster | verified | The text Wyclif quotes, *Monachi … nulli omnino persone subdantur nisi regi*, is in a privilege of **Pope Nicholas II** (1059–61), addressed to Edward the Confessor and inserted in Edward's "Third Charter" to Westminster: Kemble IV, no. 825, pp. 183–184 (*Nicholaus episcopus … Eadwardo … monachorum, qui nulli omnino personae nisi regi subdantur*). eSawyer S 1041 says the charter is spurious (Osbert de Clare, before 1139). Nicholas III (1277–80) is impossible for a letter to Edward. Loserth does give a reference, on p. 206 (foot to 206.26: *Bullam Nicolai III. huic diplomati insertam invenies in Cod. dipl. l. c. pag. 183*), but he repeats the "III". 2026-10-03. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

III st. 5 c. 17 (1352), after the chapter on nontenure: *It is accorded, That such Process shall be made in a Writ of Debt, and Detinue of Chattels, and taking of Beasts, by Writ of Capias, and by Process of Exigend …, as is used in a Writ of Accompt* (*Statutes of the Realm* I, archive.org OCR). Thorne II, 382–83; Ames Foundation): fugitives to a church must come to the king's peace and stand trial, or abjure the realm before the coroner; if they refuse, "food be denied him … and he who supplies food to him be deemed the king's enemy". These are Wyclif's three choices. The forty-day term is not on those pages (p. 383 mentions forty days only for abjurors under the assize of Clarendon). (close-out 2026-10-03) |

| E12d-6 | ecclesia-12-d / n17 | the monk killed in 1378 | untraced | The slaying of 1378 is under *homicidia inibi perpetrata* (the objection). The *modo vero* list is set against those killings as worse pollutions by the church's own inhabitants: sanctuary men living there, *occisione religiosi intra ecclesiam*, and a graduate monk's head broken by a confrere. Reading *occisio religiosi* as the 1378 killing goes against that structure. Also, the second man killed in 1378 is called a sacrist or church servant (Westminster Abbey page), not plainly a monk. Neither incident found in Loserth or the abbey account. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

I, 339–40 (Poole's foot: *Summ. Theol. sec. sec. qu. lxxxvii. 1*). Wyclif concludes there: *iste sanctus nescit fundare ex Novo Testamento quod decime dande sunt clericis*, so tithes rest on the Old Testament, as 283 says. Henry: *Quodl.* VI q. 23 (1613 ed. I, 169–70), the question quoted at 317, on whether the pope may grant tithes from lay goods. It was read: Henry argues from papal jurisdiction (the priesthood and kingship of the Mosaic law) and *ratione delicti*, not from Old Testament tithes, and Henry is not named in *De civ. dom.* I c. 42 (OCR). The Thomas half is closed; the Henry half is the question Loserth points to, but it is not the Old Testament argument. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

With c. 24 (520–21, every sin of the foreknown counts as mortal in the third sense), it gives the foreknown man's continual damnable sin. | E19g-2 | ecclesia-19-g / n10 | *feretrum pape bellantis*: which pope | verified | The pope is **Gregory VI** (1045–46). Wyclif's cross-reference at 460.1–4, *ut tetigi XVII° cap. libri quarti*, is *De civ. dom.* II c. 17, p. 253: *Cestrensis … narrat quod Gregorius sextus …*. Gregory armed against those plundering St Peter's offerings and ordered his bier set before the closed church; *turbo repente adveniens portas ecclesie dirrupit et funus … intus impulit*. Margin p. 257: "Apology for Gregory VI"; *de illacione sui feretri intra ecclesiam*. Loserth's foot: Higden, *Polychron.* VI c. 22, RS vii 150. 2026-10-03. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

| E19g-4 | ecclesia-19-g / n13 | "ex dictis XXVII° capitulo libri V": cross-reference | verified | Book 5 = *De civ. dom.* III; but c. 27 (626–62: testaments, suffrages, purgatory) does not treat baptism or original sin (OCR searched, and Wyclif's own capitula). The matter is in c. 26, 615–17: baptism does not take away all sin (*restituit hominem ad graciam si non sit finaliter impenitens*), and the capitula of c. 26 include *baptismus non delet omnem culpam*. The book is right; the chapter number is off by one, or the manuscripts divide the chapters differently. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

This is Wyclif's *equivoce … alternatim*. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

(corpusthomisticum): *Altisiodorensis enim dixit, quod suffragia prosunt damnatis non quidem per diminutionem vel per interruptionem, sed per confortationem patientis; sicut si homo portaret grave onus*. Wyclif's d.46 q.1 follows Bonaventure's distinction (d.46, Opp. IV, 1019–21); Loserth's foot (496.1) gives *dist. XLVI, q. II … Opp. tom. X (Edit. Paris. 1660), pag. 707*. V. *pro valde* sets out the four kinds of men, and argues that alms help the *mediocriter malus* who died in mortal sin *ad mitiorem poenam*, by proportion (*centesima pars*), *& sic solvo illud quod obicitur de indulgentiis*: the view Hostiensis rejects. It says nothing of the pope dispensing *contra Deum*. The gloss's formula is *Papa dispensat contra Apostolum* (D.34 c.18 *Lector*, v. *fiat*; 1588, I t. 1). (close-out 2026-10-03) |
| E21b-9 | ecclesia-21-b / n9 | Augustine on Arius's punishment growing with his followers | untraced | The saying is not found in Augustine (prior pass: not in PL; web search nothing). Wyclif's immediate source is verified: Bonaventure, *In IV Sent.* d. 46 a. 1 q. 1, arg. 2, *Augustinus dicit quod poena Arii non est determinata, quia augetur secundum quod peccantes suo errore multiplicantur; sed Deus pronior est ad miserandum quam ad condemnandum*, and ad 2, *non est simile, quia remissio debet fundari super fundamentum caritatis* (Quaracchi text, magistersententiarum.com); Wyclif's 496 follows both nearly word for word, as does his third argument (*impossibile est multorum preces non exaudiri*). (close-out 2026-10-03) |

Here it is the doctor's (Hostiensis's) argument as Wyclif reports it. (close-out 2026-10-03) |

Same sermon. §2: *erigatur antenna, ut suspensa arbori crucem Christi figuret … Huic ergo antennae … simplex conversatio et pura confessio tanquam candentia vela religentur*. §4: *Si tu es, non miror quod solidam carnem super liquentis elementi terga suspendis … fac quod mirer*. Wyclif paraphrases it (Loserth 145, foot 9: "loosely"). It is spurious, since the Maurists printed it in their Appendix. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-6a.md`) |

| P08a-2 | potestate-pape-08-a / n3 | Augustine, Epp. 112 (PL 22, 916), 31 (al. 32), 41 (p. 87), 53 (al. 165) | verified | 2026-10-08. All the salutations and passages were found. **The note is wrong.** Ep. 112 belongs to *Jerome's* collection (PL 22, 916, *Domino vere sancto ac beatissimo Papae Augustino*). In Augustine's collection the letter is Ep. 75 (PL 33, 251). The other letters are in PL 33: Ep. 31.8, *Libros beatissimi papae Ambrosii …* (col. 125); Ep. 41, to Aurelius *consacerdoti papae*, from Alypius and Augustine (col. 158; Loserth gives only "Ibid. pag 87", so "Ep. 41" is ours, now confirmed); Ep. 53.2–3, the list *ab ipso Petro usque ad Anastasium* (col. 197). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-6b.md`) |
| P08a-3 | potestate-pape-08-a / n4 | Boniface's letters in the spurious appendix of Augustine, *Opera* II (App. p. 1; Ep. 14 al. 194, App. p. 4) | verified | 2026-10-08. PL 33 Appendix (TEI 7298), the spurious Boniface–Augustine letters. Ep. 2 (col. 1095): *Tuae Sanctitatis epistolam admonitu me terrificantem suscepi. Etenim, venerabilis papa, si torus ille legitimus pavidos habet accessus …*. Ep. 14 (col. 1098): *Ora igitur, venerabilis papa, ut talis de inimicis ultio saepe procedat.* This matches Loserth's App. pp. 1 and 4. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-6b.md`) |
| P08a-4 | potestate-pape-08-a / n5 | the "Archdeacon" is Guido de Baysio, *Rosarium* | untraced | Loserth gives only "Codd.: Archidiaconus; seq. lacuna". Searched the whole PL TEI for *Graeci vocant* / *presbyteros papas*: no relevant hit. Two web searches found no *Rosarium* text with the phrase. Loserth's other foot on *Archidiaconus* (at p. 33) is only "Archid. l.c.". Guido de Baysio is the canonists' standard *Archidiaconus*, but the passage itself is unfound. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-6b.md`) |
| P08a-5 | potestate-pape-08-a / n7 | FitzRalph, *Summa* bk 7: the reason from Rome as capital | verified | 2026-10-08. FitzRalph *Summa* VII.15 (Richard speaking), Paris 1512 fol. 48r col. a (scan p. 105). The chapter has all of the reasons Wyclif lists. (1) Rome, the city that ruled the whole world, is the fitting seat. (2) The nations subject to Rome would more readily receive the faith there. (3) "ab eodem fonte pro spiritualibus causis ac temporalibus … duplex rivulus regiminis emanaret", with the temporal power to strengthen (*roborari*) the spiritual. (5) The Lord let the blood of the most excellent martyrs be shed in that place above all others (cf. 171.24, the "fifth cause"). The chapter ends *romana ecclesia omnium christianorum sit caput*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-6b.md`) |

(see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-6b.md`) |

Bede, *HE* II.1 (thelatinlibrary): Gregory asks the pope to send preachers and offers himself, *quod dum perficere non posset … non tamen ciues Romani … potuere permittere*. So in Bede he never set out. *Iter arripuit* (three days on the road, then recalled) is in John the Deacon, *Vita Gregorii* I.22–24 (PL 75, 72A–C, TEI 8047). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-6a.md`) |

Gratian 21354, D.40 c.6, *ex dictis Bonifacii Martyris* (PL 187, 214D–215A): *a nemine est judicandus, nisi deprehendatur a fide devius*. The note is wrong that Loserth gives no reference: his foot to 159.5 reads "Cap. VI." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-6a.md`) |
| P08c-1 | potestate-pape-08-c / n7 | FitzRalph, *Summa* 7.18: two pontificates and two elections (codd. cap. XIX; Loserth "loosely quoted") | verified | 2026-10-08. FitzRalph *Summa* VII.18 (Richard), Paris 1512, fol. 50r col. b: *idem legittime potest eligi duplici electione in romanum et in summum: ex quo sunt due dignitates distincte quamvis annexe*. It goes on at fol. 50v col. a: *congruentius videtur michi ut expresse eligatur ad statum utrumque*. Wyclif's "naturally differing" comes closer to the interlocutor John at the end of VII.17 (fol. 50r col. a): *nulla videtur inter hos pontificatus annexio sed videntur penitus dispares*. Richard holds that the two are annexed by divine ordination. The print's *cap. XVIII* is right. The codices' XIX is wrong, since VII.19 is on Clement's succession. Loserth 175, foot 13: "Loosely quoted." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7a.md`) |
| P08d-1 | potestate-pape-08-d / n7 | Higden, *Polychronicon* 4.36 = Rolls Series V, 140 (codd. cap. XIV, corrected by Loserth) | verified (chapter corrected) | 2026-10-08. Higden, Rolls Series V, 140 (OCR): *Postquam vero Nicena synodus hoc contulit Romano pontifici privilegium, ut sicut Augustus prae ceteris regibus, ita Romanus pontifex prae ceteris praeemineat episcopis, et papa tanquam principalis pater vocaretur*. The page lies in **book IV, chapter 26** (*Capitulum vicesimum sextum* at V, 115; the next heading, ch. 27, is after p. 146), and it is the same chapter as P08a-11 (V, 130). The codices' *cap. XIV* is wrong, and so is Loserth's *XXXVI* (foot 177.25: "Codd.: cap. XIV. Correxi"). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7a.md`) |

| P08d-6 | potestate-pape-08-d / n16 | place of the *Quo vadis* story in FitzRalph | verified | 2026-10-08. Cui Petrus stupefactus dixit Domine quo vadis. Cui Christus respondit. Vado Romam iterum crucifigi … Petrus vero ex hoc dicto intelligens seipsum tanquam alterum Christum … ibi crucifigi debere*. This is Wyclif's *sub colore Christi consilii*, *in porta civitatis* and *Petrus expectans concepit*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7a.md`) |

Sermo 129.8, PL 38, 724, has no *quod Deus avertat*: *Simus sancti, Deus scit; simus iniqui, et hoc magis ipse scit … Si autem mali sumus, nec sic deserti estis*. Also, Augustine has *Ecce est alius qui veniet in nomine suo*, where Wyclif has *in nomine Christi* (cf. 08-g n6). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7a.md`) |

At 149.25–27 Wyclif adds *Nudis foliis verborum ut signis generacionis adultere innititur Antichristus* (P07e-4, untraced). Both match "not on bare words … the custom of Antichrist". Et statim cum baculo pastorali pupugit in latus sinistrum … non est permissum a curia ut canonizetur*. Higden names Innocent IV himself. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7a.md`) |
| P08g-7 | potestate-pape-08-g / n14 | Marcellinus = D.21 c.7 + ordinary gloss (Venice 1595, p. 91) | verified | 2026-10-08. Friedberg I (BD1141951), D.21 c.7 *Nunc autem divina*: *Marcellinus episcopus urbis Romae, qui postea insignis martir effectus est … collecto numerosorum episcoporum concilio … hoc idem Pontifex se egisse confessus est*. The canon was read. The ordinary gloss (Venice 1595, p. 91) was not seen, so *in se tulit sentenciam deposicionis* rests on Loserth. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7a.md`) |
| P08g-8 | potestate-pape-08-g / n18 | Sermo 130 (Opp. V, 637) / *De verbis Domini* 46 (V, 230) | verified (identification corrected) | 2026-10-08. The quotations are Sermo 129.9, PL 38, 725, the same sermon as Wyclif's *ubi supra*: *Quid tibi fecit Ecclesia, ut eam velis quodam modo decollare? … Qui decollare vult, et caput et corpus conatur occidere. Erubescunt negare Christum, et non erubescunt negare verba Christi … Nos non vidimus, et credimus: verba ipsius nobiscum sunt. Comparate vos Judaeis: illi contempserunt pendentem in ligno, vos contemnitis sedentem in coelo*. Loserth 191, foot 30: "Serm. CXXX De Verb. Joh. VI. Opp. tom. V, 637." His foot 31: "Sermo XLVI. Opp. tom. V, 230." Neither is right. *decollare* occurs nowhere else in PL 38, and Sermo 46 (*De pastoribus*) lacks the passage. Opp. V, 637 is probably the last page of Sermo 129 in the Maurist edition, where Sermo 130 begins. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7a.md`) |
| P08h-1 | potestate-pape-08-h / n3 | Aug. *De verbis Domini* s. 89, Opp. V, 803/4 | verified | 2026-10-08. The modern number is **Sermo 167** (on Eph. 5:15–16), §§2–3, PL 38, 910: *Quisquis me audis, et nondum vivis in Christo pie, incipe in Christo pie vivere, et probas quod dico … Caetera pericula quiescere possunt, pericula a falsis fratribus quiescere usque in finem saeculi non noverunt … Redimere tempus, hoc est, quando aliquis tibi infert litem, perde aliquid, ut Deo vaces, non litibus*. Loserth 192, foot 18: "Ibid. p. 803/4." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7a.md`) |
| P08h-2 | potestate-pape-08-h / n9 | *qui creavit te sine te …* = Augustine, Sermo 169 | verified | 2026-10-08. Sermo 169.13, PL 38, 923: *Qui ergo fecit te sine te, non te justificat sine te*. Wyclif reads *creavit* for *fecit*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7a.md`) |
| P08h-3 | potestate-pape-08-h / n11 | "that bishop" = the bishop's arbitration in Augustine's sermon | verified | 2026-10-08. Sermo 167.4, PL 38, 910–911. Augustine speaks as the bishop who hears the litigants' cases: *in causis vestris et in negotiis vestris, quando ad nos judicanda veniunt*. The calumniator says *dat mihi aliquid ut tempus redimat, quia episcopum audivit*. "That bishop" is therefore Augustine himself, judging disputes (the *episcopalis audientia*). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7a.md`) |
| P09a-1 | potestate-pape-09-a / n3 | *In apibus* = Decretum C.7 q.1 c.41, from Jerome Ep. 125 (Wyclif says Gregory) | verified | 2026-10-08. Friedberg I (BD1141951), C.7 q.1 c.41 *In una ecclesia duo prelati esse non debent*: *In apibus princeps unus est; grues unam secuntur ordine litterato; inperator unus, iudex unus prouinciae …* The inscription is *Idem ad Rusticum Monachum*, after c.40 of Gregory; the Roman edition reads *Item Hieronymus* (apparatus a–b). So Wyclif's "Gregorius" comes from the canon's inscription. Source: Jerome Ep. 125.15, PL 22, 1080 (TEI 7132): *grues unam sequuntur ordine litterato*. This supports Loserth's *literato* at 194.26. Loserth's foot 194.25: "Cap. XLI." Friedberg reads *archiepiscopi* where Wyclif has *archipresbiteri*, the Roman edition's reading (apparatus e). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09a-2 | potestate-pape-09-a / n7 | Augustine, *De verbis Domini* serm. 45: God alone justifies | verified | 2026-10-08. Wyclif's *De verbis Domini* 45 is *Sermo* 129. Loserth makes this identification for the same citation at 187.1 (P08f-2). Sermo 129.6, PL 38, 723 (TEI 7334): *Quid autem expavescimus in Antichristo, nisi quia nomen suum honoraturus est …? Quid aliud facit qui dicit: Ego justifico?* This matches Wyclif's "he counts the opposite as all too heretical". Loserth's foot at 195 gives no reference. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09a-3 | potestate-pape-09-a / n12 | the *quidam* behind cardinal < *cardo* / daughter of the horseleech | untraced | Loserth gives no source (196, foot: only "Codd.: dicunt"). Searched the whole Society OCR (ia-ocr and ia-ocr-alt) for *a cardine* and *sanguisug-*. The only parallel is Wyclif's own *De blasphemia* cap. 5 (ed. Dziewicki, p. 64; latinworks02): *filie sanguisuge sunt cardinales apostate. In illis enim, ut cardine, vertuntur valve duplicitatis late vie …*, followed by the acrostic CARdinalis = *CARior DIaboli NAtus …* introduced with *describunt quidam*. A web search for the etymology with *porte inferi* found nothing. PL was not searched (the *a cardine* pattern is too broad for the 8 GB rule). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09b-1 | potestate-pape-09-b / n2 | Higden, *Polychronicon*, Rolls V, 130 (Loserth: IV.26) | verified | 2026-10-08. Higden, Rolls Series V, 130 (scratchpad poly5.txt, OCR): *Ex tunc ecclesia Romana quanto plus coepit ditari plus inde saecularis adepta sollicitudinis et subjectionis quam spiritualis devotionis, plus exterioris assecuta pompositatis … hostis antiquus … Hodie infusum est venenum in ecclesia Dei; unde et Jeronimus scripsit in vitis patrum: Ecclesia ex quo crevit possessionibus decrevit virtutibus*. This is book IV, ch. 26 (see P08d-1). Loserth 198, foot: "Polychronicon Ranulphi Higden V, 130", and at 198.3: "Recte: libro IV, cap. XXVI". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09b-2 | potestate-pape-09-b / n3 | Jerome: the church grew in possessions, decreased in virtues = *Vita Malchi* 1 | verified | 2026-10-08. Jerome, *Vita Malchi* 1, PL 23, 53C (TEI 7161): *postquam ad Christianos principes venerit, potentia quidem et divitiis major, sed virtutibus minor facta sit*. Wyclif's form of the saying and his "in vitis patrum" are taken from Higden V, 130 (P09b-1). Loserth gives no foot. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09b-3 | potestate-pape-09-b / n7 | Liberius narrative: *Polychronicon* IV (Rolls V, 146, 148, 158; "nunc cap. XXVII") | verified | 2026-10-08. Higden V. The Julius entry is on p. 148: *a Constantino secundo passus est exsilium* (C.D. add *decennale*, Wyclif's *decennale*). The Marcus/Julius entries are on pp. 146–148, in ch. 26. The Liberius entry is on p. 158: *Liberius papa post Julium sedit annis novemdecim. Hic tribus annis exulat quia noluit favere Arianis. De cujus consilio clerus Romanus ordinavit interim Felicem*. It continues on p. 160 (*consilium celebravit … Ursacium et Valentem condemnans ejecit … Felix vero martyrizabatur*). The heading *Capitulum vicesimum septimum* comes before these pages, so the entry is in ch. 27. Loserth 199, foot: "V, 146, 148, 158" and "Nunc cap. XXVII". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09b-4 | potestate-pape-09-b / n7 | Liberius was exiled by Constantius II, not Constantine | verified | 2026-10-08. Higden V, 160: *Constantius imperator Liberium tanquam mitius agentem cum Arianis de exsilio revocavit*. At p. 158, apparatus n. 10, MSS C.D. read *Hic jussu Constantii*. Higden's own text has *Constantius*, so Wyclif's *Constantini* is his slip or his exemplar's. The exile of 355 under Constantius II is standard history; no further source was read. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09b-5 | potestate-pape-09-b / n10 | FitzRalph, *Summa* XI.5: presbyter = bishop (Loserth cf. *Trialogus* 276) | verified | 2026-10-08. FitzRalph, *Summa* XI.5, Paris 1512, fol. 84r col. b – 84v col. a (scan pp. 175–176, PDF p101-200 pp. 76–77, read at 60 dpi). It opens *Item nullus ambigit quin episcopi ipsis Christi apostolis in potestate ordinis successerunt, nec in scripturis euangelicis aut apostolicis invenitur aliqua distinctio inter episcopos et sacerdotes simplices qui presbyteri appellantur*. It then cites Titus 1:5–7, Acts 20 (Miletus, *maiores natu*), 1 Tim. 3:1, 5:17, 5:19 and 3 (deacons), and on 84v: *inter ordinem episcopalem et inter ordinem dyaconatus non est ordo medius … iste doctor maximus qui suum evangelium recepit a Christo ut ipse scribit ad Gal. 1*, followed by 1 Cor. 12 and Eph. 4. This is Wyclif's sequence at 199.25–200.12. Ch. 6 begins on fol. 84v. Loserth 199, foot: "Cf. Trialogum 276" (not read). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |

V." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09c-3 | potestate-pape-09-c / n6 | *Olim* is from Jerome, *In Titum* 1 | verified | 2026-10-08. Jerome, *In Titum* on 1:5, PL 26, 562C–563C (TEI 7189): *Idem est ergo presbyter qui et episcopus, et antequam diaboli instinctu studia in religione fierent …* and *Sicut ergo presbyteri sciunt se ex Ecclesiae consuetudine ei qui sibi praepositus fuerit, esse subjectos: ita episcopi noverint se magis consuetudine, quam dispositionis Dominicae veritate, presbyteris esse majores*. The Roman edition's apparatus places it "ad caput I. epistolae ad Titum". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09c-4 | potestate-pape-09-c / n8 | Augustine Serm. 137 (*Opp.* V, 669) = *De verb. Dom.* 49 | verified | 2026-10-08. Augustine, *Sermo* 137.12, PL 38, 761 (TEI 7334): *Si viderit mercenarius aliquem loqui prava, aut sentire secundum perniciem animae suae, aut aliquid facere sceleratum … et tamen quia videtur habere alicujus momenti personam in Ecclesia, unde si sperat commoda, mercenarius est … non illi dicit, Peccas; non illum objurgat, ne perdat commoda sua*. Loserth 202, foot 14: "Opp. tom. V, 669 (Serm. CXXXVII)". The awkward *unde si sperat* of 09-c n9 is Augustine's own. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09c-5 | potestate-pape-09-c / n13 | Lombard, *Sent.* IV d.18 c.2 ff., PL 192, 1106 | verified (column corrected) | 2026-10-08. Lombard, *Sent.* IV d.18, PL 192, 885–889 (TEI 11468). Col. 885, c.2 *De clavibus*, Ambrose: *Ecclesia hoc sibi vindicat, quae veros sacerdotes habet; haeresis vendicare non potest, quae veros sacerdotes non habet*. Cc. 4–6 (886–888) give Augustine, Ambrose and Jerome. Loserth 203, foot 17: "Opp. ed. Migne CXCII, 1106". That is wrong: col. 1106 is Bandinus on marriage (TEI 11457). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09c-6 | potestate-pape-09-c / n16 | Jerome, *In Matth.* III on 16:19, PL 26, 118 | verified | 2026-10-08. Jerome, *In Matth.* III on 16:19, PL 26, 118A (TEI 7191): *Istum locum episcopi et presbyteri non intelligentes, aliquid sibi de Pharisaeorum assumunt supercilio, ut vel damnent innocentes, vel solvere se noxios arbitrentur; cum apud Deum non sententia sacerdotum, sed reorum vita quaeratur. Legimus in Levitico …* Wyclif's wording follows Lombard's quotation (PL 192, 887: *Hunc, inquit, locum quidam non intelligentes, aliquid sumunt de supercilio Pharisaeorum*). Loserth 204, foot 2: "Hier. Comment. in Ev. Matth. Lib. III … Migne XXVI, 118." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09d-1 | potestate-pape-09-d / n1 | the "Master" is Lombard, *Sent.* IV d.18 (PL 192, 887); the gloss on Matt. 16:19 ascribed to Augustine; Wyclif's "cap. IX" | verified | 2026-10-08. Lombard, *Sent.* IV d.18 c.5, PL 192, 887 (TEI 11468): *Hinc August. ait, lib. de vera et falsa Poen., c. 10 … Dixit enim, Matth. 16: Quodcumque solveritis super terram … hoc est: Ego Deus, et omnis coelestis curiae ordines, et omnes sancti in gloria mea laudant vobiscum et confirmant quos ligatis et solvitis. Non dixit quos putatis ligare et solvere; sed in quos exercetis opus justitiae vel misericordiae. Aliter autem … opera vestra in peccatores non cognosco*. The true source is ps.-Augustine, *De vera et falsa poenitentia* 10.25, PL 40, 1122 (TEI 7359), on Matt. 18:18, which reads *omnes ordines coelestis militiae*, Wyclif's *milicie*. Loserth 204, foot 31: "Magister, i. e. Petrus Lombard. l. c. pag. 887." Wyclif's "cap. IX" matches neither text: §9 in PL's d.18 is *Quae sint interiores tenebrae*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09d-2 | potestate-pape-09-d / n4 | Augustine, *Tract. in Ioh.* 121 ("Sermone CXXI"): the church forgives sins | verified (locus corrected) | 2026-10-08. Augustine, *Tract. in Ioh.* 121.4, PL 35, 1958 (TEI 7323): *Ecclesiae charitas quae per Spiritum sanctum diffunditur in cordibus nostris, participum suorum peccata dimittit: eorum autem qui non sunt ejus participes, tenet*. Lombard (PL 192, 886–87) cites *tract. 121 super Joan.* just before quoting the same sentence, which he assigns to *De vera et falsa poen.*; this is probably Wyclif's route. Loserth 205, foot 24: "Opp. tom. V, 595". The Maurist tom. V page fits *Sermo* 121, read in PL 38, 678–80, which is on John 1:10–14 and does not have the sentence. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09d-3 | potestate-pape-09-d / n5 | Augustine "De verbis Domini Sermo 53", *Melior est virginalis sanctitas …* | verified (number corrected) | 2026-10-08. Augustine, *Sermo* 354.9 (*Ad continentes*), PL 39, 1567–68 (TEI 7342): *Etenim melior est virginalis sanctitas, quam pudicitia conjugalis … Junge illam virginali sanctitati … Si enim tu tenueris superbiam, et tua mater humilitatem, melior erit mater quam filia … non dubito praeferre humilem mulierem virgini superbae*. Loserth 205, foot 32: "St. Aug. Serm. CCCLV, Opp. tom. V, 1379. Loosely quoted." His number is off by one. Sermo 355 is *De vita et moribus clericorum*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09d-4 | potestate-pape-09-d / n7 | Augustine on the human race needing the humble heavenly physician | verified | 2026-10-08. Augustine, *Sermo* 142.2, PL 38, 778 (TEI 7334), on John 14:6: *Quae enim causa humilitatis Christi, nisi infirmitas tua? … haec res fecit ut veniret ad te tantus medicus … Sed quia tu ire non potuisti ad eum, ille venit ad te. Venit docens humilitatem, qua redeamus: quia superbia nos redire non sinebat ad vitam; et ipsa fecerat nos a vita recedere*. Loserth 206 gives no foot. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09d-5 | potestate-pape-09-d / n8 | "ut dicit Augustinus": a pope who seeks worldly glory | verified | 2026-10-08. No verbal match. The nearest is *Tract. in Ioh.* 29.8, PL 35, 1631–32 (TEI 7323), on John 7:18 citing John 5:43: *Significavit eos Antichristum suscepturos, qui gloriam nominis sui quaesiturus est, inflatus, non solidus … In Antichristo autem … gloriam suam quaesiturus est … Omnes ergo pertinentes ad corpus Christi, ne inducamur in laqueos Antichristi, non quaeramus gloriam nostram*. This has Wyclif's Antichrist seeking glory, John 5, and "is not of Christ's church". Cf. *Sermo* 129.6 (PL 38, 723), also on John 5:43. Searched the Augustine TEI files 7320–7339 (tractates and sermons) for Antichrist within 150 characters of *glori-*. Loserth gives no foot. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09d-6 | potestate-pape-09-d / n10 | the triad "qua vult ire … quo … ubi vult manere" on John 14:6 | verified | 2026-10-08. Augustine, *Sermo* 142.1, PL 38, 778 (TEI 7334): *Ego sum, inquit, via, et veritas, et vita. Tanquam diceret, Qua vis ire? Ego sum via. Quo vis ire? Ego sum veritas. Ubi vis permanere? Ego sum vita. Securi ergo ambulemus in via: sed insidias timeamus juxta viam … Juxta semitas scandala posuerunt mihi (Psal. CXXXIX, 6)*. Wyclif's next sentences (207.8–11) follow it. This supports the emendation *vita* for *cursiv*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09f-1 | potestate-pape-09-f / n2 | shooting stars, burning dragons, comets from a kindled exhalation: Aristotle, *Meteor.* I.4 | verified | 2026-10-08. Aristotle, *Meteor.* I.4 (MIT Classics, Webster tr., English): the dry "windy exhalation" rises to the top of the air and, set in motion, "catches fire … if it burns lengthwise only, we see what are called 'torches' and 'goats' and shooting-stars". Comets are treated at I.7 as the same kind of kindled gathering. *Draco ardens* is the medieval Latin name for the "torch/goat" class. The Bekker lines were not checked. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09f-2 | potestate-pape-09-f / n6 | Ps.-Chrysostom, *Opus imperfectum* on Matt. 20:27; Wyclif Hom. 28, Loserth "Recte Homilia XXXV" | verified | 2026-10-08. Ps.-Chrysostom, *Opus imperfectum in Matth.*, hom. 35 (on Matt. 20; PG 56, 822–32), read in the Manipulus florum project's transcription of PG 56 with Migne's column marks (manipulus-project.wlu.ca/OpusImperfectum.pdf). At col. 830: *saecularem quidem primatum desiderare, etsi ratio non est, vel causa est: quia etsi justum non est, vel utile est; primatum autem ecclesiasticum concupiscere, neque ratio est, neque causa … Quis enim sapiens ultro se subjicere festinat servituti, labori, dolori, et quod majus est, periculo tali, ut det rationem pro omni Ecclesia apud justum Judicem, nisi forte qui nec credit judicium Dei, nec timet uti abutens primatu suo ecclesiastico saeculariter, convertat eum in saecularem?* Loserth 211, foot 25: "Recte Homilia XXXV ex cap. XX." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09f-3 | potestate-pape-09-f / n8 | "Robertus … Urbanus" = Robert of Geneva (Clement VII) and Urban VI | verified | 2026-10-08. Loserth's introduction (latinworks19, as at P08f-1): "if Robert in Avignon, or Urban in Rome …". His side-note at 212.15 reads "The contention of the rival popes marks them as Antichrists". Robert of Geneva (Clement VII, Avignon) and Urban VI (Rome) were elected in 1378. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P09f-4 | potestate-pape-09-f / n9 | the two later "ubi supra" Chrysostom quotations come from the same homily | verified | 2026-10-08. Same homily 35, PG 56, 829–30 (as P09f-2). 212.18–25: *quicumque autem desiderat primatum in terra, inveniet confusionem in caelo: ut jam inter servos Christi non sit de primatu certamen, nec festinet unusquisque eorum, quomodo aliis major appareat, sed quomodo omnibus inferior videatur: quoniam non qui major fuerit in honore, ille est justior, sed qui justior fuerit, ille est major*. Wyclif has *qui inferior fuerit*. 213.4–8, col. 830: *principes autem Ecclesiae fiunt, ut serviant minoribus suis, et ministrent eis quaecumque acceperunt a Christo, ut suas utilitates negligant, et illorum procurent: ut, si opus fuerit, neque mori recusent pro salute inferiorum suorum*. Wyclif's *Ideo primatus in populo non est ab aliquo appetendus* (212.25) echoes the homily's *primatus in Christo nec ab aliquo appetendus est*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-7b.md`) |
| P08c-2 | potestate-pape-08-c / n2 | James son of Alphaeus = bishop of Jerusalem = "the brother of the Lord" (Gal. 1:19); Jerome, *De viris illustribus* 2 | verified | 2026-10-12, coordinator: Jerome, *De vir. ill.* 2 (PL 23, TEI 7156): *Jacobus, qui appellatur frater Domini (Galat. I, 19) … Mariae sororis matris Domini … filius, post passionem Domini statim ab Apostolis Hierosolymorum episcopus ordinatus* |
| P09g-1 | potestate-pape-09-g / n1 | Decretum D.79 c.9 *Si quis pecunia* | verified | 2026-10-08. Gratian D.79 c.9, Nicholas II, PL 187, 382C: *Si quis pecunia vel gratia humana, vel populari seu militari tumultu, sine concordi et canonica electione … fuerit apostolicae sedi inthronizatus, non Apostolicus, sed apostaticus habeatur*. Loserth 214, foot 6: "Decr. Prima pars, dist LXXVIIII, cap. IX." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |

Gratian D.40 c.2, PL 187, 214B, inscribed *Hieronymus ad Heliodorum*: *Non est facile stare loco Pauli et tenere gradum Petri jam cum Christo regnantium, quia hinc dicitur: non sanctorum filii sunt, qui tenent loca sanctorum, sed qui exercent opera eorum*. Loserth 215, foot 1: "Decr. Prima Pars dist. XL, cap. II." The canon does not read *Petri et Pauli*, so the note's claim that Loserth's emendation is "the canon's reading" is wrong. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P09g-3 | potestate-pape-09-g / n5 | FitzRalph, *Summa* 7.24: Constantine's bishop called pope | verified | 2026-10-08. FitzRalph *Summa* VII.24, Paris 1512, fol. 51v col. b. The interlocutor John is speaking: *quidam … conantur romanam ecclesiam probare precipue esse caput aliarum eo quod imperator constantinus circa annum domini 350 hoc censuit et fecerit promulgari et foca etiam imperator illud idem constituit circa annum domini .dc. sicut legitur in annalibus eorundem*. The print has "350" (with a gothic 3), not 301, and "head of the others", not "called pope". Loserth 215 gives no foot. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P09g-4 | potestate-pape-09-g / n6 | Higden, *Polychronicon* V, 416: Phocas grants Rome headship | verified | 2026-10-08. Loserth 215, foot 25: "Sic et in Polychronico V, 416; Hic impetravit ab Augusto Phoca, ut ecclesia beati Petri Romae caput foret omnium ecclesiarum in orbe." The Latin was read on Rolls Series V, 416 (Higden V.10) in wave 7a (P08d-3). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P09g-5 | potestate-pape-09-g / n8 | the "Tartary/Cambalek" sentence is FitzRalph's (*ut inquit*) | verified | 2026-10-08. FitzRalph *Summa* VII.24, Paris 1512, fol. 51v col. b, John speaking, in the same paragraph as P09g-3: *cur insuper imperator tartarie ecclesiam de cambalon aut de cathai caput omnium ecclesiarum constituere non posset*. So *ut inquit* is FitzRalph's dialogue. The print has *Cambalon* where Wyclif has *Cambalek*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P09g-6 | potestate-pape-09-g / n10 | Decretum C.24 q.1 c.25 *Quoniam vetus oriens* = Jerome, Ep. 15.1–2 | verified | 2026-10-08. Gratian C.24 q.1 c.25, *Item Hieronymus ad Damasum*, PL 187, 1276B–1277B: *Quoniam vetusto oriens … Fatiscat invidia Romani culminis, recedat ambitio. Cum successore piscatoris et discipulo crucis loquor … Quicunque tecum non colligit, spargit, hoc est, qui Christi non est antichristi est*. Jerome, Ep. 15.1–2, PL 22, 355–356, has *Facessat invidia* and the same close. Loserth 216, foot 16: "Cap. XXV." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P09g-7 | potestate-pape-09-g / n12 | Gregory, *Regula pastoralis* 1.2, "nemo amplius in ecclesia nocet" | verified | 2026-10-08. Gregory, *Regula pastoralis* 1.2, PL 77, 16A: *Nemo quippe amplius in Ecclesia nocet, quam qui perverse agens, nomen vel ordinem sanctitatis habet*. Loserth 217, foot 20: "Gregorii, De Cura Past. l. c." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P09g-8 | potestate-pape-09-g / n13 | Decretum C.16 q.7 c.3 *Pervenit ad nos*, "loosely quoted" | verified | 2026-10-08. Gratian C.16 q.7 c.3, a Palea inscribed *Item Gregorius VII*, PL 187, 1044A: *Pervenit ad nos fama sinistra, quod quidam episcoporum … decimas atque Christianorum oblationes conferant … laicalibus personis, militum videlicet, sive servitorum, vel (quod gravius est) etiam consanguineis … inter maximos haereticos et antichristos non minimus habeatur*. The same text is also C.1 q.3 c.13 (PL 187, 554B). Loserth 217, foot 23: "Cap. III loosely quoted." Wyclif's *monstruosa familia* has no counterpart in the canon. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P09g-9 | potestate-pape-09-g / n17 | "Archidiaconus" = Guido de Baysio, *Rosarium* on D.40 | untraced | Loserth 218 has no foot at line 14 (only "27. Cap. II."). Wyclif's *archidiaconus* also occurs at 165.10, where Loserth notes a lacuna. The Society editors elsewhere identify *Archidiaconus* as Guido de Baysio: latinworks07 ("Guido de Baisio, Rosarium (ed. Venet. 1577)"), latinworks04 and latinworks02. Loserth himself, at latinworks06, writes "Archidiaconi Rosarium mihi ad manum non est". The *Rosarium* on D.40 was not seen: archive.org has only manuscripts of it, and it is not in the PL. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |

Gratian D.22 c.2 (*Anacletus … epistola III*), PL 187, 123A–124A: *Sacrosancta Romana et apostolica ecclesia non ab apostolis, sed ab ipso Domino salvatore nostro primatum obtinuit … Secunda autem sedes apud Alexandriam … a Marco, ejus discipulo atque evangelista, consecrata est*. D.22 c.3, 124D–125A: *Constantinopolitanae civitatis episcopum habere oportet primatus honorem post Romanum episcopum, propterea quod sit nova Roma*. Loserth 218, foot 27: "Cap. II"; 219, foot 5: "dist. XXII. cap. III". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |

| P09h-3 | potestate-pape-09-h / n9 | Decretum D.40 cc.2, 3, 4, 12 | verified | 2026-10-08. Gratian D.40, PL 187, 213C–218A. c.2 *Non est facile*; c.3 *Nos, qui praesumus*; c.4 *Non loca vel ordines*; c.12 (ps.-Chrysostom, *Opus imperfectum* hom. 43) *Multi sacerdotes, et pauci sacerdotes; multi nomine, pauci opere*. Loserth 220, foot 26: "Cap. II, cap. III Nos qui presumus et IV Non loca"; 28: "Cap. XII." Wyclif's incipit *Multi sunt sacerdotes* is his own form. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
