LXXI (old number 38 in the margin): *Bonum est facultates cum dispensatione pauperibus erogare. Melius est pro intentione sequendi Dominum insimul donare, et absolutum sollicitudine cum Christo egere*. The same text circulates under Isidore's name (PL 83, 1241C). Loserth 223, foot 9: "Opp. tom. VIII, App. pag. 79, cap. XXXVIII." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10b-1 | potestate-pape-10-b / n2 | *Flores historiarum*, ed. Luard, II, 184 (Otto, 1226) | verified | 2026-10-08. *Flores historiarum*, ed. Luard, II, 184 (s.a. 1226): *magister Otto, domini Papae nuncius, litteras … recitavit, in quibus idem Papa allegavit scandalum sanctae Romanae ecclesiae et obprobrium vetustissimum, notam scilicet cupiditatis, quae radix dicitur omnium malorum … Sed quoniam scandali hujus et infamiae Romana paupertas causa est … Ad istud itaque scandalum penitus eradicandum*. The chronicle has *eradicandum*, which confirms Loserth's supplement (224.5, 10-b n3). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10b-2 | potestate-pape-10-b / n6 | Isidore, *Sententiae* 3.51 | verified | 2026-10-08. Isidore, *Sententiae* 3.51.4, under the old chapter heading *LIII. De disciplina principum in ecclesia* (The Latin Library text): *Principes saeculi nonnumquam intra ecclesiam potestatis adeptae culmina tenent, ut per eandem potestatem disciplinam ecclesiasticam muniant*. Elsewhere in this volume (latinworks19, OCR line 24654) Loserth gives the same canon "ex Isidori De Summo bono III, cap. LIII". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10b-3 | potestate-pape-10-b / n6 | Decretum C.23 q.5 c.20 *Principes saeculi* | verified | 2026-10-08. Gratian C.23 q.5 c.20, PL 187, 1223C–1224A, inscribed *Isidorus lib. III Sent. de summo bono, c. 55*, with the text above. Loserth 225, foot 3: "Decreti Secunda Pars, Causa XXIII, Quaest. V, cap. XX." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10b-4 | potestate-pape-10-b / n9 | Bernard, *De consideratione* 4, on the pope's upkeep | verified | 2026-10-08. Loserth gives no foot at 225.23. Wyclif's "libro IV ad Eugenium" is best matched by *De consideratione* 4.3.6, PL 182, 776A: *Petrus hic est, qui nescitur processisse aliquando vel gemmis ornatus, vel sericis; non tectus auro, non vectus equo albo, nec stipatus milite … Absque his tamen credidit satis posse impleri salutare mandatum … In his successisti, non Petro, sed Constantino*. That the office can be fulfilled without such wealth is Bernard's point. The "many hundreds of popes" are Wyclif's. Book IV, ch. 6 (the household) was also read, and it does not fit as well. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10b-5 | potestate-pape-10-b / n14 | Richard I's capture: *Flores historiarum* I, 107, 114; Matthew Paris II, 394 | verified (volume corrected) | 2026-10-08. Loserth 227, foot 7: "Rex Richardus captus a duce Austriae et venditus imperatori Henrico v. Flor. Hist. I, 107, 114 e Matth. Paris II, 394." Luard's *Flores* vol. I ends at 1066, so the reference is wrong. The passages are in **vol. II**. p. 107 has the side-heading *Rex Ricardus capitur a duce Austrie, et venditur imperatori*, and the text *a duce Austrie captus est*. p. 114 has *imperator, qui regem Ricardum emptum vendidit*. Matthew Paris, *Chron. maj.* II, 394: *Qualiter rex Ricardus a duce captus sit*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10b-6 | potestate-pape-10-b / n15 | Decretum D.96 c.14 *Constantinus* | verified | 2026-10-08. Gratian D.96 c.14 *Constantinus* (Palea), PL 187, 460B–462A. Loserth 232, foot 18: "Cap. XIV. Loosely quoted." (read on the plate, leaf n293). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10c-1 | potestate-pape-10-c / n1 | Decretum D.22 c.2 *Sacrosancta* | verified | 2026-10-08. Gratian D.22 c.2, PL 187, 123A–124A, as at P09h-1. The canon includes Rom. 1:9 (*proprio chirographo pollicetur*) and *Adhibita est etiam societas in eadem Romana urbe beatissimi apostoli Pauli*. Loserth 228, foot 16: "Cap. II." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10c-2 | potestate-pape-10-c / n1 | the canon is a Pseudo-Isidorian decretal under Anacletus' name | verified | 2026-10-08. The canon's inscription in Gratian (PL 187, 123A) is *Item Anacletus Servus Christi Jesu ad omnes episcopos, epistola III, c. 3*. The Anacletus letters belong to the Pseudo-Isidorian decretals, which is standard. Friedberg's apparatus was not opened. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10c-3 | potestate-pape-10-c / n6 | *Unam sanctam*, *Extrav. comm.* 1.8.1 | verified | 2026-10-08. Loserth 229, foot 29: "Extrav. Comm. De [maioritate et obedientia]". The text was read in Friedberg II (BD1141952), 1245–46, in wave 7a (P08g-4). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10c-4 | potestate-pape-10-c / n11 | Durham vacancy: Walsingham, *Ypodigma* p. 283, *Historia Anglicana* II, 382 | verified (page corrected) | 2026-10-08. This is the vacancy of 1345. Walsingham, *Historia Anglicana* II, 382 (Appendix (b), *Obiit Episcopus Dunelmensis*): *Hoc anno obiit Ricardus de Bury … cui successit Thomas de Hatfeld, Regis Secretarius, mediantibus Regis litteris ad Papam … contra suas litteras et vota procerum … Papa vero … quibusdam de Cardinalibus calumniantibus dictum Thomam fore levem et laicum, ita respondit: Vere, si Rex pro asino supplicasset, obtinuisset*. *Ypodigma*, ed. Riley, has the same text on **p. 284** (the page header 284 comes before it, and Riley's index gives "Hatfeld, Thomas de, Bishop of Durham … 284"), not p. 283. The pope is Clement VI (Loserth's index: "Clemens VI. papa 231"). Loserth 231, foot 2, as in the note. The *Studien* was not seen. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10c-5 | potestate-pape-10-c / n12 | Decretum C.11 q.3 c.63 *Privilegium* | verified | 2026-10-08. Gratian C.11 q.3 c.63, *Idem* [Gregorius] *in Regesto*, PL 187, 862A: *Privilegium omnino meretur amittere qui permissa sibi abutitur potestate*. Loserth 231, foot 18: "Cap. LXIII." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10c-6 | potestate-pape-10-c / n16 | Augustine on not being believed beyond scripture (Ep. 82; *De Trin.* 3 prooem.) | verified | 2026-10-08. Loserth gives no foot at 232.5. *De Trinitate* 3 prooem. 2, PL 42, 869: *Noli meis litteris quasi Scripturis canonicis inservire*. This is said of his own writings and is the exact parallel. Ep. 82.3, PL 33, 277: *solis eis Scripturarum libris qui jam canonici appellantur, didici hunc timorem honoremque deferre … Alios autem ita lego …*. There Augustine says it of other writers, not of his own. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10d-1 | potestate-pape-10-d / n1 | Decretum D.96 c.14 *Constantinus* | verified | 2026-10-08. Gratian D.96 c.14, PL 187, 460B–461B. Loserth 232, foot 18: "Cap. XIV. Loosely quoted." (plate). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10d-2 | potestate-pape-10-d / n1 | the Donation continues *tam … quam etiam super omnes … ecclesias* | verified | 2026-10-08. PL 187, 461B: *ut principatum teneat tam super quatuor praecipuas sedes, Alexandrinam, Antiochenam, Hierosolymitanam, Constantinopolitanam, quam etiam super omnes in universo orbe terrarum ecclesias Dei*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10d-3 | potestate-pape-10-d / n3 | Grosseteste, *Epistolae*, ed. Luard, p. 90 | verified | 2026-10-08. Grosseteste, *Epistolae*, ed. Luard, p. 90. The letter is to William Raleigh, 1236, standing between Luard's nos. XXII and XXIV, which agrees with Wyclif's *Epistola XXIII*; the number is garbled in the OCR. The OCR is damaged, but it reads that the princes of the church receive nothing of ecclesiastical power from any secular power, *sed immediate a Dei ordinatione*. Loserth 233, foot 4: "Roberti Grosseteste Epp. ed. Luard pag. 90." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10d-4 | potestate-pape-10-d / n4 | *Robertus Gilbonensis* = Robert of Geneva, elected 1378 as Clement VII | verified | 2026-10-08. Loserth's index (latinworks19): "Robertus antipapa (Clemens VII.) 212, 213, 233 …", and "Clemens VII. antipapa, v. Robertus Gebennensis". Walsingham *Hist. Angl.* index: the cardinals "elect a Pope of their own party, I. 382" (1378). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10d-5 | potestate-pape-10-d / n7 | Decretum D.22 c.4 *De Constantinopolitana* | verified | 2026-10-08. Read on the plate (archive.org latinworks19wycl, leaf n294, p. 233 foot): "27. Cap. IV." The OCR's "IX" is a misreading. The text is PL 187, 125A–B, as at P09h-2. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10d-6 | potestate-pape-10-d / n9 | Aristotle, *De caelo* 1.5, 271b: a small error at the beginning | verified | 2026-10-08. Aristotle, *De caelo* 1.5, 271b8–13 (MIT Classics, English): "the least initial deviation from the truth is multiplied later a thousandfold … that which was small at the start turns out a giant at the end." Wyclif's *error modicus in principio* is the scholastic form. The Greek and Bekker lines were not checked. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10d-7 | potestate-pape-10-d / n13 | Gregory, *Hom. in Evang.* 17 | verified | 2026-10-08. Gregory, *Hom. in Evang.* 17.17, PL 76, 1148B: *Ibi Petrus cum Judaea conversa, quam post se traxit, apparebit. Ibi Paulus conversum … mundum ducens*. Loserth 235, foot 13: "Gregor. l. c. Opp. (ed. Migne) II, 1148" (= PL 76). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-8.md`) |
| P10e-1 | potestate-pape-10-e / n8 | *concessio Cesaris* (237.6) = the Donation of Constantine | verified | 2026-10-08. Context read: 237.1–6 *non obstante concessione Cesaris quod quilibet papa habet potestatem iurisdicionis … super totam Yndiam, Yudeam*, the caesarean papacy this chapter has been arguing against, and 246 *pontificis Constantini* (10-g n24). The grant is the Donation of Constantine. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10f-1 | potestate-pape-10-f / n3 | *Si solus Christus* = Gratian D.8 c.9 (Augustine) | verified (attribution corrected) | 2026-10-08. Gratian D.8 c.9, PL 187, 48B: *Si solus Christus audiendus est, non debemus attendere, quid aliquis ante nos faciendum putaverit, sed quid … Christus prior fecerit*. The inscription is *Idem lib. II, ep. 3, ad Caecilium*, and *Idem* is Cyprian (c.8: *Item Cyprianus*), i.e. Cyprian *Ep.* 63. It is not Augustine. Loserth does give a foot at 237 ("Cap. IX"), so the note is wrong to say he gives none. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10f-2 | potestate-pape-10-f / n7 | the cause maxim (*posita causa …*) is "the philosophers'" | untraced | Wyclif writes *secundum philosophos* (238.33). Loserth gives no foot. The wording *qua posita … ponitur* is not in the local PL (phrase grep) and not elsewhere in the Society OCR. It is a scholastic commonplace with no single locus. The note claims nothing beyond Wyclif's own words. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10f-3 | potestate-pape-10-f / n10 | "Matth. V" Pharisees = the gift vowed to the temple, Matt. 15:5 | verified | 2026-10-08. Matt. 15:5 Vg *Munus, quodcumque est ex me, tibi proderit*. Wyclif makes the same point at 245.2 and cites it there as *ut patet Matth. XV* (latinworks19). So the *Matth. V* at 239.12 has lost an X. Loserth has no foot. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10f-4 | potestate-pape-10-f / n11 | Glossa ordinaria on C.24 q.1 c.1 | editor | Loserth 239, foot 19: "Cf. Glossam ord. (ed. Venet. 1595) I, p. 1295." The canon (C.24 q.1 c.1, Gelasius on Acacius: *in haeresim semel damnatam labitur*, PL 187, 1263) fits *Sacerdos hereticus … non excommunicat nisi se ipsum*. The gloss itself is not local. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10f-5 | potestate-pape-10-f / n11 | the second "ut dicit glossa" (celebrate secretly) is the same gloss | untraced | The second *ut dicit glossa* (239.30: excommunicated man may celebrate *saltem abscondite*) has no foot from Loserth. The Glossa ordinaria to the Decretum is not local, and Friedberg prints no gloss. The note's "refers to the same gloss" is therefore an assumption. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |

| P10f-7 | potestate-pape-10-f / n18 | Gregory struck Sabinian after death (John the Deacon, *Vita Gregorii*?) | verified | 2026-10-08. Wyclif at 241.1 says *ut recitatum est VIII cap.*; that passage follows Higden V, 412 (P08g-5). The story is also in Paul the Deacon, *Vita Gregorii* 29, PL 75, 58C–D: *apparuit ei in visu sanctus Gregorius tertio … quarto … eum in capite percussit, cujus dolore vexatus paulo post defunctus est*. Sigebert, *Chronica* a. 607, PL 160, 114B, has the same story with the marginal source *(V. Greg.)*. John the Deacon was not checked. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |

(see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10g-1 | potestate-pape-10-g / n12 | Augustine, *De verbis Domini* s. 53 = Loserth *Opp.* V, 1378 ("Aliquando Deus iratus dat …") | verified | 2026-10-08. Loserth's quoted words are in Augustine, *Sermo* 354.7 (*Ad continentes*), PL 39, 1567 (TEI 7342): *Aliquando Deus iratus dat quod petis, et Deus propitius negat quod petis*. Loserth 243, foot 27: "Opp. tom. V, 1378". That column fits his own 1379 for 354.9 (P09d-3). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10g-2 | potestate-pape-10-g / n12 | modern number for *De verbis Domini* 53 | verified | 2026-10-08. Modern number: *Sermo* 354.7, PL 39, 1567. Whether 354 was the old *De verbis Domini* 53 is not shown in the PL heading. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |

| P10h-1 | potestate-pape-10-h / n3 | Robert of Geneva (Clement VII, Fondi, Sept. 1378); Urban VI elected April 1378; Paris for Clement, England for Urban | verified | 2026-10-08. Walsingham, *Hist. Angl.* I, 369 (A.D. 1378): *Huic successit Bartholomaeus, Barensis Archiepiscopus … ut ipsi niterentur eum … Papatu privare*. I, 380–81: Parliament receives Urban's envoys, rejects the cardinals'. I, 391: the King of France forbids obedience to Urban. I, 393: *Clemens … Antipapa … in terra Comitis Fundorum*. The months (April, September) and the Fondi election itself are not in Walsingham. This is the same text Gratian gives under Jerome's name. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |

The sentence is § 1 of the same canon, C.24 q.1 c.14, PL 187, 1269B–C: *Item: § 1. Sancta Romana ecclesia, quae semper immaculata mansit, et Domino providente … in futuro manebit*. It is not in Pelagius' *Libellus*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10i-1 | potestate-pape-10-i / n2 | Johannes de Deo, Johannes Andreae, Johannes Monachus: 13th–14th-c. canonists | untraced | Loserth's index (latinworks19) lists only "Johannes a Deo 200" (*sic*, for 250), "Johannes Andreae 250", "Johannes Monachi 250". | P10i-2 | potestate-pape-10-i / n8 | Gregory = Gregory XI (Loserth); his 1377 bulls condemned the proposition that lords may take temporal goods from a delinquent church | verified | 2026-10-08. Loserth 252, foot 1: "i. e. Gregorius XI." Walsingham, *Hist. Angl.* I, 354, the bulls of Gregory XI dated *undecimo Kalendas Junii, anno septimo* (22 May 1377). The sixth condemned conclusion reads *Si Deus, domini temporales possunt legitime ac meritorie auferre bona fortunae ab Ecclesia delinquente*, and this matches Wyclif's *quod domini temporales possunt auferre temporalia ab ecclesia delinquente*. See also FZ 254–55 (Wyclif's explanation, concl. XVI–XVII). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |

Est decretum Nicolai papae de electione Romani pontificis." D.23 c.1, PL 187, 127B–129, *Nicolaus Papa II in Concilio Lateranensi*: *In nomine Domini … crebrisque tunsionibus*, with the privilege of *Henrici*. The year 1059 is not in the PL text. | P10i-5 | potestate-pape-10-i / n10 | 1044–46 (Benedict IX, Silvester III, Gregory VI; Sutri); Loserth's "Benedictus X" at 253.1 | verified | 2026-10-08. Sigebert, *Chronica* a. 1045–46, PL 160, 208C–209A: *Benedictus Simoniace papatu Romano invaso … alterum … secum papam consecrari fecit … tertius superducitur … rex Heinricus contra eos Romam vadit; et eis … depositis*. Loserth 253, foot 1: "Glossa ordinaria: Benedictus X." So the gloss numbers the restored Benedict "X"; he was Benedict IX. Sigebert does not name Sutri. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |

No foot from Loserth. Nothing matching in Walsingham I, 1378–79 (the *stipendiarii* of I, 395 are Arundel's expedition). Cf. 252.3, where Urban *mercede conduxit temporales dominos*. Richard's birth year was not checked in a source. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10j-2 | potestate-pape-10-j / n5 | the cardinals annulled Urban's election (Anagni, Aug. 1378) and elected Robert at Fondi | verified | 2026-10-08. Walsingham, *Hist. Angl.* I, 369: the cardinals *niterentur eum cum dedecore Papatu privare*. I, 393: Clement in the Count of Fondi's land. Anagni and the August date are not in Walsingham. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10j-3 | potestate-pape-10-j / n8 | Augustine, *De verbis Domini* s. 53 (Opera V, 1375) | verified (number corrected) | 2026-10-08. *Caput humile et membrum superbum absit …* is Augustine, *Sermo* 354.9, PL 39, 1568: *Caput humile, et membrum superbum? Absit. Non vult esse de corpore capitis humilis, qui amat superbiam*. Loserth 255, foot 11: "Opp. tom. V, 1375." Augustine, *Sermo* 138.5 (on John 10:11–16), PL 38, 765: *merito Christus in se habens omnes pastores bonos … Ego sum, unus sum, mecum omnes in unitate unum sunt*. Loserth 255, foot 15: "Ibid. pag. 671." Wyclif's "sermone L" is not the modern number. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10j-5 | potestate-pape-10-j / n13 | Grosseteste, *Epistolae*, ed. Luard, letter 36, p. 125 | verified | 2026-10-08. Grosseteste, *Epistolae*, ed. Luard, no. XXXVI, p. 125, to the cardinal Giles: *Sicut super mundi cardines mundus innititur eorumque fulcimine supportatur … sic super sanctae Romanae ecclesiae cardinales, orbis terrarum qui non commovebitur*. Loserth 256, foot 4: "p. 12?" (OCR). The page is 125. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10j-7 | potestate-pape-10-j / n17 | Grosseteste's sermon to Innocent IV at Lyon, 1250 | verified | 2026-10-08. Matthew Paris, *Chron. maj.* V, 96–98 (A.D. 1250): *Episcopus Lincolniensis R[obertus] transfretat* to the papal curia, where he addresses the pope. Loserth has no foot at 257.14. Wyclif cites the same *sermo ad papam* at *De civ. dom.* III (latinworks06): *papa gerit vicem domini Jesu Christi pauperrimi*. The sermon text itself (Brown, *Fasciculus* II) was not read. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10j-8 | potestate-pape-10-j / n19 | Grosseteste's appeal; Matthew Paris: after death he struck Innocent IV with his staff | verified | 2026-10-08. Higden VIII, 240–42 gives the appeal *ad tribunal Christi* and the staff (P08g-6; Wyclif's "ut dixi VIII capitulo"). Matthew Paris, *Chron. maj.* V, 429–30 (A.D. 1254): *apparuit ei idem episcopus … pungens ipsum in latere ictu impetuoso cuspide baculi … pastoralis*. V, 470–71: Innocent dies, *postquam episcopus Lincolniensis … cuspide baculi sui pastoralis ipsum in latere … pupugit*. Paris V, 389–93 has the refusal letter (*non obedio, contradico, et rebello*), not an appeal. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-9.md`) |
| P10k-1 | potestate-pape-10-k / n2 | Ps.-Chrysostom, *Opus imperfectum* hom. 28 (Opp. VI, 771) | verified (number corrected) | 2026-10-08. PG 56, 832: *Quicumque autem, Domino humilitati studente, jactantiae studet, non est imago Christi. Et qui, Domino paupertatem amante, divitiarum amator est in hoc seculo, repellit a se Christi similitudinem. Non est verus discipulus, qui non imitatur magistrum*. This closes Migne's hom. 35 (*ex capite xx*, on Matt. 20:20–28). Hom. 36 begins right after it. Wyclif's "XXVIII" is not Migne's number. Loserth 258, foot 4: "Opp. tom. VI, pag. 771". That is a Montfaucon/Gaume page, not checked. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10k-2 | potestate-pape-10-k / n3 | Wyclif's "Ep. XLIII" = Grosseteste Ep. 49 (Luard p. 145) | verified | 2026-10-08. Luard, Ep. XLIX, pp. 144–45, to Otto, cardinal-legate. On p. 145 (OCR damaged but legible in part): *Scio et veraciter … hanc esse potestatem … de omnibus beneficiis ecclesiasticis … possit ordinare. Scio … abutitur hac potestate*. Loserth 258, foot 12: "Est Epistola XLIX Roberti Grosseteste vid. Epp. pag. 145." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10k-3 | potestate-pape-10-k / n6 | Grosseteste Ep. 35 (Luard p. 123) | verified | 2026-10-08. Luard, Ep. XXXV, p. 123, to Gregory IX (*Sanctissimo patri … Gregorio*, dated "1236?"): *Etsi ex generali debito subjectionis, quo non solum populus Christianus, sed totum humanum genus obligatur et sine cujus solutione nullus salutem consequitur, debitor sim … plenitudinis obedientiae et reverentiae, honoris et timoris*. Wyclif drops *Etsi*. Loserth 258, foot 24: "Roberti Grosseteste, Epistolae, No. XXXV, pag. 123." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10k-4 | potestate-pape-10-k / n7 | the man Grosseteste flattered is Innocent IV; the posthumous blow | verified (corrected) | 2026-10-08. The letter Wyclif has just quoted (Ep. 35) is addressed to **Gregory IX**, not Innocent IV (Luard p. 123). So the pope flattered in that letter is Gregory. The punishing pope Wyclif means is presumably Innocent IV, but Wyclif treats the two as one man. The posthumous blow to Innocent is Matthew Paris, *Chron. maj.* V, 429–30, 470–71 (read for P10j-8). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10k-5 | potestate-pape-10-k / n9 | "decrees and chronicles" of ch. 8 = Phocas and the Donation | verified | 2026-10-08. Chapter 8 (08-d, 177–78): Higden IV, 36 (the Nicene privilege); Decretum D. 96 c. 14 *Constantinus* (Wyclif's "XLVI distinccione", sic), on the origin of the title *in dotacione ecclesie*; Higden V, 10, where Boniface IV obtains from Phocas *ut ecclesia beati Petri … Caput esset ecclesiarum*. Then: *Quotlibet tales fideles cronice et decreta testantur primatum Romani pontificis fuisse humanitus adinventum*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10k-6 | potestate-pape-10-k / n10 | *De cessatione legalium*: Grosseteste's treatise or Wyclif's own | verified | 2026-10-08. Wyclif has no work of this title. He names Grosseteste's treatise at *De ver. sacr. scr.* I, 135 (latinworks21): *racio … quam dat Lincolniensis de cessacione legalium*. He uses the same formula without an author, *ut patet in materia de cessacione legalium*, at *De officio regis* 208 (latinworks18), and *in De Cessacione Legalium* at *De simonia* 51 (latinworks20). Buddensieg's introduction (latinworks21) lists Grosseteste's *De Cessatione Legalium* (London 1632, partial). Loserth gives no foot at 259.20. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10k-7 | potestate-pape-10-k / n16 | "dixi alias" = *Trialogus* p. 413 | verified | 2026-10-08. Lechler, *Trialogus* (1869), p. 413, *Supplementum Trialogi* cap. 2: *Notandum tamen, quod aliqua creduntur explicite et aliqua implicite, sicut debent: explicite creduntur, quae in fide scripturae patule explicantur, implicite ergo creduntur quaecunque veritates sive quas explicite reputamus sive quas confuse credimus*. Loserth 260, foot 21: "E. g. Trial. p. 413." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10l-1 | potestate-pape-10-l / n5 | Grosseteste Ep. 37 (Luard p. 226), recited in *De civ. dom.* III p. 30 | verified | 2026-10-08. Luard p. 226: *ita injustae possessiones … ecclesias ipsas non augent, sed deturpant; et earum abscisio per justum judicium non est ecclesiarum imminutio, sed potius pulcrificatio quaedam*. Page 226 is in Luard's **Ep. LXXII** (to John Romanus, subdean of York, pp. 203–35). It is not Luard 37. *De civ. dom.* III, p. 30 (latinworks06), end of cap. 2, has the same words as *Lincolniensis Epistola LXXIII*. Loserth 262, foot 16: "De Civili Dominio III, p. 30, Rob. Linc. Epp. pag. 226." He gives no number. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10l-2 | potestate-pape-10-l / n5 | Wyclif's "book 5, ch. 2" = *De civ. dom.* III in the *Summa* | verified | 2026-10-08. The recital in *De civ. dom.* III is on p. 30, the last lines of *Capitulum secundum*. *Capitulum tercium* begins on the same page (latinworks06). This matches Wyclif's *in fine secundi cap. libri V*, with *Summa* book 5 = *De civ. dom.* III (WORKFLOW 8). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10l-3 | potestate-pape-10-l / n8 | *par in parem non habet imperium*, Roman/canon-law maxim | verified | 2026-10-08. Innocent III to the archbishop of Canterbury, *Prima collectio decretalium*, tit. IV (*De electione*), PL 216, 1195C: *pari post eum imo etiam eadem potestate functuris, cum non habeat imperium par in parem*. Also in PL 55 (TEI 7636, a modern dissertation): *Par in parem non habere imperium jura omnia asseverant*. Loserth gives no foot. The Roman-law locus (Digest) and the *Liber Extra* number were not checked. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10l-4 | potestate-pape-10-l / n17 | Decretum D.21 c.7 (*Nunc autem*) | verified | 2026-10-08. Loserth 264, foot 18: "Decreti Prima Pars, Dist. XXI, cap. VII." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P10l-5 | potestate-pape-10-l / n17 | Marcellinus, incense, synod of Sinuessa | verified | 2026-10-08. The incense and the council are in the canon itself (D. 21 c. 7, PL 187, 120D–121A: *collecto numerosorum concilio episcoporum … se egisse confessus est*). The canon names no place. *Vitae pontificum* (*Liber pontificalis*), PL 128, 1410C: *ad sacrificium ductus est, ut thurificaret: quod et fecit*. Sinuessa is not in the local PL in this sense (the grep finds only the town). The apocryphal *Gesta synodi Sinuessanae* (Mansi I) is not local. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |

| P10l-7 | potestate-pape-10-l / n20 | Bernard, *De consideratione*, on reproving popes | verified | 2026-10-08. Wyclif cites *ex libro beati Bernhardi ad papam Eugenium* as a whole. Loserth gives no foot. The work is addressed to Eugenius (PL 182, 727A, *papa beatissime Eugeni*) and reproves him openly, e.g. I.5.6, 733B: *Audi ergo quid redarguam, quid suadeam*, and II.7.15, 750C (*Monstruosa res*). Searches of 21366 for *corrip-*, *reprehend-*, *Marcellin-* find no passage on whether a pope may be reproved. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P11a-1 | potestate-pape-11-a / n7 | Bernard, *De consid.* IV.7.23 | verified | 2026-10-08. *De consid.* IV.7.23, PL 182, 788A: *Consideres ante omnia sanctam Romanam Ecclesiam, cui Deo auctore praees, Ecclesiarum matrem esse, non dominam: te vero non dominum episcoporum, sed unum ex ipsis … De caetero oportere te esse considera formam justitiae …*, through *postremo deum Pharaonis*. This is the last section of book IV, so Wyclif's *in fine libri IV* is right. Loserth gives no foot. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P11a-2 | potestate-pape-11-a / n9 | *Monstruosa res* = *De consid.* II.7.15; the "monster" at the end of book 3 | verified | 2026-10-08. *Monstruosa res gradus summus, et animus infimus …* is II.7.15, PL 182, 750C, after *Et nunc audi canticum meum, et quidem minus suave, sed salutare*. | P11a-4 | potestate-pape-11-a / n11 | *Occumbebant … non data possessio* = *De consid.* III.1.1; Bernard *sed quatenus* (print *ac quamvis*) | verified (reading corrected) | 2026-10-08. *De consid.* III.1.1–2, PL 182, 758B–C: *Occumbebant strenuissimi bellatores, sed non succumbebant … At quatenus haec portio te contingit … dispensatio tibi super illum credita est, non data possessio … Possessionem et dominium cede huic … praesis ut prosis*. Bernard reads *At quatenus*, not *sed quatenus*. He also has *strenuissimi … sed non* where Wyclif has *fortissimi … et non*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P11a-5 | potestate-pape-11-a / n13 | "Et erit omnis terra possessio eius": Loserth Ps. CIII (verse illegible); not Vulgate Ps. 103:24 | verified | 2026-10-08. The words are Bernard's, III.1.1, PL 182, 758B: *Non tu ille de quo propheta: Et erit omnis terra possessio ejus*. Migne's margin gives *(Psal. CIII, 24)*. Loserth's foot 268.11 is legible: "Ps. CIII, 27". Neither verse has the words. Vg 103:24 is *impleta est terra possessione tua*; 103:27 is *Omnia a te expectant*. `bible.py --find` finds *possessio eius* nowhere in the Vulgate. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| P11b-1 | potestate-pape-11-b / n6 | Aristotle, "the white ceases, the musical remains" = *Phys.* 1.7 (190a) / *De gen.* 1.4 (319b) | untraced | 2026-10-12. Loserth has no foot (269.16 foot is only Rom. VIII, 39). I searched Aristotle's *Physics* I and V, *De gen. et corr.* I and *Metaphysics* V (English) for "white" with "musical" plus "cease/perish/remain/persist". There is no "the white ceases and the musical remains". The nearest loci: *Phys.* 1.7, 190a17–21 (the unmusical does not survive, the man does); *De gen.* 1.4, 319b25–31 (the musical man passes away, the man persists); *Phys.* 1.2, 185b (to be white and to be musical differ in definition, yet the same thing is both). Wyclif's pair is a scholastic stock combination of these. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |
| P11b-2 | potestate-pape-11-b / n8 | Bernard, *De consideratione* 2.8.15 (Loserth "l. c. pag. 751") | verified | 2026-10-12. *De consid.* II.8.15, PL 182, 751B–D (cap. VIII *De pontificiae dignitatis et potestatis excellentia*): *Age, indagemus adhuc diligentius qui sis, quam geras … pro tempore personam in Ecclesia Dei. Quis es? Sacerdos magnus, summus Pontifex. Tu princeps episcoporum … potestate Petrus, unctione Christus*. That makes thirteen titles, so "these thirteen" is right. Loserth 269 foot 30: "l. c. pag. 751", which matches the PL 182 column. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |
| P11b-3 | potestate-pape-11-b / n9 | Bernard reads *quidem*, *nec modo ovium*, *tu unus omnium pastor* | verified | 2026-10-12. PL 182, 751C–D: *Sunt quidem et alii coeli janitores … quanto et differentius … tibi universi crediti, uni unus. Nec modo ovium, sed et pastorum tu unus omnium pastor*. So *quidem* and *nec modo ovium* are confirmed. Bernard also reads *uni unus* where Wyclif has *unus uni*, and *sed et pastorum tu unus* where Wyclif has *sed pastorum cum unus*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |

| P11c-3 | potestate-pape-11-c / n8 | Sergius = the legend of Muhammad's monk teacher | verified | 2026-10-12. Wyclif's own usage confirms the reading. *De blasphemia* ch. 6, p. 84 (latinworks02): *tamquam alter Sergius, seductor Machometi, volunt eligere carni placencia de lege utraque et displicencia transaltare*. This is the same charge of picking from both laws. The editor's foot there reads "Sergius and Mahomet are often alluded to in Wyclif's writings. See De Apostasia, p. 67". *De civ. dom.* II, ch. 13 (latinworks05): *Sergium monachum Machometi declarari dydascalum*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |
| P11c-4 | potestate-pape-11-c / n12 | Hus, *Opera* 219 | verified | 2026-10-12. Loserth 274 foot 17: "Cf. Hus Opp. 219." It then quotes D. 99 *Primae sedis*. This was checked in English translation only. The Latin folio was not checked. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |

D. 99 c. 3, PL 187, 471B (*ex Concilio Africano*): *Primae sedis episcopus non appelletur princeps sacerdotum, vel summus sacerdos …*. D. 99 c. 5, 472B (Gregory to Eulogius): *Ecce in praefatione epistolae … universalem me Papam dicentes …*. Loserth 274 feet 22, 23: "Cap. III.", "Cap. V." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |
| P11d-1 | potestate-pape-11-d / n1 | FitzRalph, *Summa* 11.5 (end) | verified (locus corrected; reading found) | 2026-10-12. Wyclif himself cites the source at 275.1–3: *Ardmacanus in De Questionibus Armenorum libro XI in fine cap. V*. There is no Loserth foot. In the Paris 1512 print, the passage is not at the end of ch. 5. It stands early in **ch. 6** (*Item potestas soluendi a peccato …*, which begins at the foot of col. a), at scan p. 176, col. b: *ex isto capite sequi michi videtur quod quo ad potestatem ligandi et soluendi siue retinendi siue baptizandi aut consecrandi aut benedicendi aut breuiter quo ad quamcunque potestatem actus sacramentalis expressam a christo in euangelio quicunque sacerdos in ordinatione suam totam accepit aut nichil ipsius quoniam simul et indiuise ac coniuncte sunt date a christo et quod deus coniunxit homo non separet dicit christus sicut legitur mat. 19 et mar. 10*. So the print's *conclusio* stands for FitzRalph's **quo ad**, and his "mat. 19" confirms Matt. 19:6. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |
| P11d-2 | potestate-pape-11-d / n8 | Ps.-Dionysius, *EH* 5 (and ch. 4 on the myron) | verified | 2026-10-12. *EH* 5.1.5 in Eriugena's version, PL 122, 1097B: *Inde divina legislatio ierarchicorum ordinum sanctificationem, et divini unguenti consummationem, et sacram altaris purificationem, divinorum pontificum perfectivis virtutibus singulariter divisit*. This is exactly Wyclif's three (*sacerdotum consecracio, divini ungenti perfeccio et sacri altaris benediccio*, 277.1–3). The three orders and their operations are EH 5.1. Loserth 276 foot 28: "St. Dionysii Areop. De Eccles. Hierarch. (Ed. Lugd. 1572) pag. 151." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |
| P11d-3 | potestate-pape-11-d / n10 | D.40 c.12 *Multi sacerdotes* (ps.-Chrysostom) | verified | 2026-10-12. D. 40 c. 12, PL 187, 217B. The rubric is *Non est verus sacerdos omnis, qui nominatur sacerdos. Item Joannes Chrysostomus* [Migne: *auctor operis imperfecti in Matth. hom. 43 ad c. 23*]. Text: *Multi sacerdotes, et pauci sacerdotes; multi nomine, pauci opere*. Loserth 277 foot 21: "Cap. XII." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |
| P11d-4 | potestate-pape-11-d / n12 | *tractatus De Sacramentis* (probably Wyclif's own) | untraced | 2026-10-12. Loserth gives no foot at 278.5. The phrase "tractatu De Sacramentis" occurs nowhere else in the Society OCR (all volumes searched). In those volumes, Wyclif's bare *in tractatu De …* names his own works (*De Papa*, *De universalibus*, *De tempore*, *De rege*, *De incarnacione*, *De eukaristia*). For others' works he writes *in tractatu suo de …* (e.g. Grosseteste). So "Wyclif's own" is the likelier reading. Hugh, cited next, is named with *libro secundo De Sacramentis*. No Wyclif work of the title is known to the Society editions (Shirley's catalogue not checked). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |
| P11d-5 | potestate-pape-11-d / n13 | Hugh of St Victor, *De sacr.* 2.4 (Loserth: Mainz 1617, III, 449) | verified | 2026-10-12. Hugh, *De sacr.* II.4.1–2, PL 176, 433B–D (*Pars quarta, De indumentis sacris*): *idcirco singularis eis in sacro ministerio habitus attribuitur, ut per id quod foris apparet, quales intus esse debeant, ostendatur. Sacrarum autem vestium ritus partim a Veteri Testamento assumptus est, partim a Patribus novis adjectus* (so *a patribus* is right against the MSS' *a fratribus*). Also: *Tunica byssina … ποδήρης, id est talaris … Haec ob speciem candoris nomen Albae sortitur*. Hugh cites Lev. 8; the Exodus chapters are Wyclif's. Loserth 278 foot 10: "Hugo De Sacram. Opp. (ed. Mog. 1617) III, 449." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11a.md`) |
| P11e-1 | potestate-pape-11-e / n2 | source of Loserth's italic run 279.18–27 (Hugh, *De sacr.*?) | verified | 2026-10-12. The source is Hugh, *De sacr.* II.4.3 *De zona* (PL 176, 434C): *Haec vestis, id est tunica linea circa renes fortiter astringitur, ut castitas sacerdotis nullo incentivorum aestu dissolvatur, sicut scriptum est: Sint lumbi vestri praecincti. Vestis quippe candida munditiam, zona vero continentiam significat, ut illic non sordescat, hic non effluat vita spiritualis. Haec zona … quatuor coloribus distincta … bysso, purpura, hyacintho, cocco*. Wyclif has *conversacio* for *vita spiritualis*, and he adds *cogitacione vel opere* and "Exod. XXVII". Hugh's *fortiter astringitur* supports the n2 emendation *cinccionem*. Hugh's next words (*ad quatuor elementa … sive quatuor principales virtutes*) support the n5 emendation *elementorum*. The preceding first vestment (alba, 279.1–15) follows Hugh II.4.2. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11e-2 | potestate-pape-11-e / n7 | Arist. *EN* 1.10, 1100b21–22, "tetragonus sine vituperio" | verified | 2026-10-12. *EN* 1.10, Bekker 1100b20–22: ὅ γʼ ὡς ἀληθῶς ἀγαθὸς καὶ τετράγωνος ἄνευ ψόγου (milestone 20 falls just before τὰ κατʼ ἀρετήν, so the phrase is on 1100b21–22). Read in the PerseusDL Greek with Bekker milestones. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11e-3 | potestate-pape-11-e / n7 | same example, *De civ. dom.* I, 54, l. 26 | verified | 2026-10-12. *De civ. dom.* I, c. 7, p. 54, ll. 25–26 (latinworks04): *secundum Aristotelem sicut tetragous sive vitupero*. Poole's foot 26 reads "Tetragous. Can Wycliffe mean κατήγορος?", so he did not recognise the *Ethics*. This matches Loserth's foot 280.5: "Eodem exemplo utitur W. De Civili Dominio I, 54, l. 26." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11e-4 | potestate-pape-11-e / n8 | "aurum in camino" = Prov. 17:3 (wording nearer Ecclus. 2:5) | verified | 2026-10-12. Loserth's foot 280.7 reads "Prov. XVII, 3." Vulgate Prov. 17:3 reads *Sicut igne probatur argentum et aurum camino*. Ecclus. 2:5 reads *in igne probatur aurum … in camino humiliationis*. Neither has *tribulacionis*, and the note's account is correct. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11e-5 | potestate-pape-11-e / n15 | Hugh, *De sacramentis*, l. c. p. 460, "very loosely quoted" | verified | 2026-10-12. Hugh, *De sacr.* II.4.4 *De feminalibus lineis* (PL 176, 434D–435B): *Neque vero existimandum in illo priori populo viros discooperta … carne pudoris solitos incedere, ut intratum ad sancta tunc demum verenda tegere juberentur. Sed voluit sermo Dei … singulare operimentum … quibus est injuncta servandae castitatis quotidiana necessitas, sicut quotidie est offerendi concessa potestas … quos rursus expleta vice ad operam carnalis commercii relaxavit*. "Very loosely quoted" is fair. Loserth's "l. c. p. 460" refers to his earlier citation (foot 279.10): "Opp. (ed. Mog. 1617) III, 449". I did not see the Mainz page. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11f-1 | potestate-pape-11-f / n1 | source of italic runs 283.1–3 and 283.21–284.3 | verified | 2026-10-12. Both runs are from Hugh, *De sacr.* II.4.7 *De rationali* (PL 176, 435D–436B). For 283.1–3: *Sequitur deinde rationale sive logion, quod pectori pontificis superimponitur*. For 283.21–284.3: *ut in doctrina sua pontifex nec aliud quam veritas suggerit, efficere praesumat, neque id quod a veritate intus hauserit ratione et opere approbare et manifestare desistat. Notandum vero quod adinvicem concatenantur rationale et superhumerale, quia cohaerere et concordare debent, ratio et opera*. Hugh also supplies 284.11–16 (*non prius rationale quam superhumerale nominatur, quoniam in schola virtutum opera praecedunt … Quae coepit Jesus facere et docere … ne docere praesumas quod prius non didiceris*). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11f-2 | potestate-pape-11-f / n3 | Josephus, *Ant.* III, 9 (Frankfurt 1588, p. 38b) | verified | 2026-10-12. The content is Josephus, *Ant.* III.8.9 (Whiston): the right-shoulder sardonyx shone at the sacrifices, the twelve stones shone before victory, and "this breastplate, and this sardonyx, left off shining two hundred years before I composed this book". Loserth's foot 283.9 quotes the Latin: "lib. III, cap. IX (ed. Franc. 1588, p. 38b) … Desiit tamen … annis 200 ante quam haec commentaremur". The Frankfurt page was not seen. Wyclif's wording is nearer Comestor, *Hist. schol.* Exod. c. 64 (PL 198, 1185A–B): *Asserit autem Josephus hunc sardonicem et fulgorem … cessasse annis ducentis, antequam ipse scripsisset haec*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11f-3 | potestate-pape-11-f / n3 | modern form *Ant.* 3.215–218 | verified | 2026-10-12. Whiston III.8.9, read. I did not read a text with Niese section numbers, so the bracketed "3.215–218" cannot be confirmed. Use the Whiston form. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11f-4 | potestate-pape-11-f / n4 | "Rasa" = Rashi, via Lyra on Exod. 28:30 | verified | 2026-10-12. Lyra on Exod. 28:30 (Venice 1603 I, OCR): *per quorum fulgorem manifestabitur sacerdoti divinum beneplacitum, et hoc secundum Iosephum; secundum vero Rab. Sa[lomonem] nomen Domini tetragrammaton fuit inclusum modo predicto*. Rashi on Exod. 28:30 (Sefaria) reads: "an inscription of the Proper Name of God which was placed between the folds of the breast-plate". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11f-5 | potestate-pape-11-f / n7 | Exod. 25 ("XXV, 28") and 29:5 | verified (corrected) | 2026-10-12. Loserth's foot 284.12 reads "Exod. XXV, 28; XXIX, 5." Exod. 25:28 is about the bars of the table, which is irrelevant, and is presumably a misprint. Exod. 29:5 names the superhumeral first. Wyclif's claim is right for **Exod. 28:4**: *rationale et superhumerale, tunicam et lineam strictam …*, which names the rational first. Hugh II.4.7, whom Wyclif is following, says the reverse (*non prius rationale quam superhumerale nominatur*). The present note says Exodus contradicts Wyclif, which is wrong. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11f-6 | potestate-pape-11-f / n14 | cosmic reading of the vestments (Wisd. 18:24; Josephus *Ant.* 3.7.7) | verified (corrected) | 2026-10-12. Wyclif's direct source for 285.33–286.27 is Nicholas of Lyra on Exod. 28, at the end of the chapter (*Legitimum sempiternum*, 28:43). He follows it nearly word for word. Lyra gives the causes: *vestes pretiosas … ut haberentur in maiori reverentia a populo, et quia illud cedebat ad honorem divinum, cum essent medii inter Deum et populum*. The cosmic reading follows: *Tunica enim linea figurabat terram … Baltheus cingens illam tunicam figurabat Oceanum … Tunica hyacinthina ratione coloris figurabat elementum aeris … tonitrua. Balthaeus autem cingens illam tunicam figurabat elementum ignis, quia ambit aerem; superhumerale cum rationali sibi coniuncto figurabat celum sydereum … pulchritudinem stellarum. Duo autem lapides onychini … duo magna luminaria … Duodecim gemmae in rationali … duodecim signa zodiaci … rationes … Iob 38 Nunquid nosti ordinem caeli et pones rationes … Per mitram vero figurabatur celum empyreum, et per laminam auream … Deus praesidens omnibus creaturis … nomen domini tetragrammaton … Sap. 18 … nomen eius magnum*. Behind Lyra stand Comestor, *Hist. schol.* Exod. c. 66 *De typo vestium* (PL 198, 1186C–D), Josephus *Ant.* III.7.7 (Whiston, read) and Wisd. 18:24 (read). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11f-7 | potestate-pape-11-f / n15 | Peter Comestor, *Hist. schol.*, *Hist. lib. Machab.* c. 1 | verified | 2026-10-12. Comestor, *Hist. schol.*, chapter *De Alexandro* (PL 198, 1496D–1497A). It comes in the Persian and Greek prelude (*De Ocho*, *De Dario*, *De Alexandro*) between Esther and *Liber I Machabaeorum*. The TEI heads it "CAP. IV" under Esther, so the Maccabees heading appears to be lost in the PL/TEI, and Wyclif's "historia Machabeorum primo cap." is plausible for a manuscript division. The text reads: *Alexander intuens antistitem, pontificali stola insignem, et super cidarim laminam auream, in qua scriptum erat nomen Dei, descendit de equo … et nomen Dei adoravit*. Loserth's foot 286.29 reads "vid. Petr. Comest. l. c." (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11f-8 | potestate-pape-11-f / n15 | Alexander story from Josephus *Ant.* 11.8.5 | verified | 2026-10-12. Josephus, *Ant.* XI.8.5 (Whiston): Alexander, seeing "the golden plate whereon the name of God was engraved, … approached by himself, and adored that name, and first saluted the high priest", and he tells Parmenio "I did not adore him, but that God". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11g-1 | potestate-pape-11-g / n1 | Hugh, *De sacr.* II, p. 4, c. 9 ff. (the six vestments) | verified | 2026-10-12. Hugh, *De sacr.* II.4.9–13 (PL 176, 437A–438): c. 9 *De indumentis novi sacerdotii*, c. 10 stole (*jugum Domini … per anteriora descendens … per arma justitiae a dextris et a sinistris … stola cum zona … colligatur*), c. 11 chasuble (*charitatem significat … excellentiorem viam*), c. 12 maniple (*divino cultui mancipatis aliquoties negligentia subrepit … in sinistra manu*), c. 13 dalmatic. Wyclif's 287–289 follows these closely. Hugh does not count six vestments; that is Wyclif's own count. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11g-2 | potestate-pape-11-g / n7 | Gregory, *Hom. in Ev.* 30.2, *operatur enim magna, si est* (bears on print *magis*) | verified | 2026-10-12. Gregory, *Hom. in Ev.* 30.2, PL 76, 1221B: *Nunquam est Dei amor otiosus. Operatur etenim magna, si est; si vero operari renuit, amor non est.* Gerhoh (PL 193, 927A) quotes it as *Operatur enim magna, si est*. This is evidence for *magna* against the print's *magis*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11g-3 | potestate-pape-11-g / n8 | John 1:5 at 288.27 | editor | Loserth's foot 288.27 reads "27. Joh. I, 5." The line is *quod luceat lux vestra coram hominibus*, which is Matt. 5:16 (*Sic luceat lux vestra coram hominibus*), as the note already says. John 1:5 (*lux in tenebris lucet*) is at most an echo. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-11b.md`) |
| P11h-1 | potestate-pape-11-h / n7 | X 1.8 *De auctoritate et usu pallii*, c.5; Loserth: rather c. *Ex tuarum* (Wyclif has *Ex tenore*) | verified | Friedberg II, X 1.8 *De auctoritate et usu pallii* c. 5, Innocent III to the archbishop of Compostela, inc. *Ex tuarum tenore literarum accepimus* (read in the BD1141952 OCR, 2026-10-12). Loserth 291 foot 24: "I, 8 Cap. V; sed est cap. Ex tuarum." Wyclif's *Ex tenore* shortens *Ex tuarum tenore*, so it is the same chapter. Loserth is right. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |
| P11h-2 | potestate-pape-11-h / n11 | weathercock-as-preacher allegory, cf. Durandus, *Rationale* 1.1 | verified | Durandus, *Rationale* 1.1.22: "Gallus supra Ecclesiam positus praedicatores designat … Hi etiam, sicut et gallus contra ventum se vertunt, quando increpando et arguendo contra rebelles fortiter resistunt". The book 1 c. 1 table also has "22. Gallus in summitate Ecclesiae fixus quid notet". Read in the Naples 1859 OCR, 2026-10-12. Wyclif's preacher set "against all the winds" matches it. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |
| P11i-1 | potestate-pape-11-i / n4 | D.12 c.12 *Omnia talia* = Augustine, *Ep.* 55.19.35 | verified | Gratian D.12 c.12, PL 187, 66A (rubric "Augustinus … ad inquisitiones Januarii epist. CXIX, c. 19"). Augustine *Ep.* 55.19.35 (PL 33, 221: in *Epistola LV*, cap. XIX, §35). Wording read against both on 2026-10-12. Wyclif has *legalibus tamen **sacramentis***, which follows Gratian; Augustine (PL) has *sarcinis*. So Wyclif quotes through Gratian, as he says. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |
| P11i-2 | potestate-pape-11-i / n10 | the "decree" on Paul and Peter as fellows | verified | Decretum C.2 q.7, PL 187, ~651A, read 2026-10-12. c.33 (Jerome on Galatians): "Majores et minores non dignitate, sed vita … Paulus Petrum reprehendit, quod non auderet, nisi se non imparem sciret". c.37 (under Ambrose's name): "Aequales fuerunt meritis, licet gradu differentes, Petrus et Paulus". Both fit *omnes presbiteri debent esse socii … servus ex maiori humilitate fit alcior*. The identification is ours, by content; Wyclif does not name the canon. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |
| P11i-3 | potestate-pape-11-i / n16 | John turns rods to gold, pebbles to gems: Isidore, *De ortu et obitu patrum* 72; *Legenda aurea* | verified | Isidore, *De ortu et obitu patrum* c. 72 §128, PL 83, 151C: "Mutavit in aurum sylvestres frondium virgas littoreaque saxa in gemmas. Item gemmarum fragmina in propriam reformavit naturam". *Legenda aurea* c. 9 (*De sancto Johanne*), ed. Graesse: the rods and pebbles story at p. 58, and Isidore quoted by name at p. 62. Both read 2026-10-12. Wyclif's *littoria saxorum fragmenta* conflates Isidore's *saxa* and *gemmarum fragmina*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |
| P11i-4 | potestate-pape-11-i / n16 | cross-reference to *De ecclesia* p. 189 and p. 81 above | verified | Loserth 299 foot 2: "De Ecclesia pag. 189 and 81 supra." Both checked 2026-10-12. Our p. 81 (latinworks19): "cum silvestres frondes mutavit in aurum et fragmina saxorum in gemmas". *De ecclesia* c. 9 (latinworks10): "beatum Evangelistam convertisse frondes silvestres in aurum et litoria saxa in gemmas". That passage falls between the OCR's running heads 186 and 192, which agrees with p. 189. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |
| P11j-1 | potestate-pape-11-j / n12 | D.99 c.5 *Ecce* | verified | Gratian D.99 c.5 *Ecce*, PL 187, 472B–474A, read 2026-10-12 (rubric "Gregorius Eulogio Patriarchae Alexandrino, lib. VII, epist. 30"). Loserth 301 foot 5 "Cap. V" agrees. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |

The print's *unitatem* stands against both. Read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |
| P11j-4 | potestate-pape-11-j / n13 | D.99 c.4 *Nullus patriarcharum*, Pelagius II | verified | Gratian D.99 c.4 *Nullus patriarcharum*, PL 187, 472A: "Unde Pelagius II epist. omnibus episcopis illicite a Joanne Constantinopolitano convocatis". | P11j-5 | potestate-pape-11-j / n14 | D.99 c.3 *Primae sedis* | verified | Gratian D.99 c.3 *Primae sedis*, PL 187, 471B, read 2026-10-12. Loserth 301 foot 29 agrees. Wyclif's next sentence, *Universalis autem nec eciam romanus pontifex appelletur*, is not part of c.3: it is Gratian's own dictum (II Pars) leading into c.4, which Wyclif runs into the canon. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |
| P11j-6 | potestate-pape-11-j / n14 | the canon is from an African council (Carthage) | verified | Gratian's rubric reads "Item ex Concilio Africano, c. 6" (PL 187, 471B). The canon is Carthage III (397) c. 26 in the Hispana (PL 84, 192C: "Ut primae sedis episcopus non appelletur princeps sacerdotum aut summus sacerdos aut aliquid hujusmodi, sed tantum primae sedis episcopus"). The *Breviatio canonum* (PL 67, 954A) gives "Conc. Carthag., tit. 34". All read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |
| P11j-7 | potestate-pape-11-j / n16 | Bernard, Lincoln and Jerome on taking Christ's name | untraced | **Bernard** (probable): *De consideratione* 2.7.14–15, PL 182, 750C, read 2026-10-12: "Simia in tecto, rex fatuus in solio sedens … Monstruosa res gradus summus, et animus infimus: sedes prima, et vita ima". This is dignity without the matching life, and the ape image is the same as Wyclif's *symeam ad summum repere*. Wyclif quotes both lines as Bernard *libro ii° ad Eugenium* in *De officio regis*, ed. Pollard–Sayle, pp. 46–47 (latinworks18). **Lincoln (Grosseteste) and Jerome: untraced.** Places checked: Loserth's text and feet at p. 302 (no foot for this line; the foot "St. Bernardi De Consideracione III" in the latinworks19 OCR belongs to a later page in c. 12); Society OCR (*simia/symea, monstruosa*); PL for the Bernard phrases. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12a.md`) |
| P11k-1 | potestate-pape-11-k / n2 | Augustine on Peter's fall ("expedit multos incidisse…") = *De civ. Dei* 14.13 | verified | Augustine, *De civ. Dei* 14.13, PL 41, 422: *Et audeo dicere, superbis esse utile cadere in aliquod apertum manifestumque peccatum … Salubrius enim Petrus sibi displicuit quando flevit, quam sibi placuit quando praesumpsit*. Wyclif paraphrases (*expedit multos incidisse …*). His own *De ecclesia* p. 164, which Loserth cites here, quotes this very passage as "XIV°, De Civitate Dei, cap. XIII°" and calls the sin *expediens* (latinworks10). Read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12b.md`) |
| P11k-2 | potestate-pape-11-k / n2 | Loserth compares *De ecclesia* p. 164, *Serm.* II 152, *Op. evang.* III 198 | verified | Loserth's foot at 303.12 reads "Cf. De Ecclesia pag. 164, Serm. II, 152, Op. Evang. III, 198." *De ecclesia* p. 164 was read (see P11k-1) and matches. *Serm.* II 152 and *Op. evang.* III 198 rest on Loserth. Checked 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12b.md`) |
| P11k-3 | potestate-pape-11-k / n4 | "materia de necessitate futurorum" = Wyclif's own treatment of future contingents | untraced | The deduction that the damned give the blessed a joy they would otherwise lack was not found by phrase search across the Society OCR. Wyclif names "materia de necessitate futurorum" as a topic at *De dominio divino* I.14 (Poole 1890; *Sed quia introducitur materia de necessitate futurorum*, through cap. 19), and *Logica* III (latinworks16) uses the same phrase. Loserth, *De statu innocencie* intro. (latinworks17), counts a tract *De necessitate futurorum* among the lost works (Shirley no. 200) and points to *De dom. div.* I.14–19. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12b.md`) |
| P11k-4 | potestate-pape-11-k / n8 | Magister = Lombard, *Sent.* II d. 38–40, on intention (*simplicitas oculi*) | verified | Lombard, *Sent.* II d.40 c.1, PL 192, 747 (*An ex fine omnes actus pensari debeant*): acts are *simpliciter ac vere* good *qui bonam causam et intentionem … comitantur*, with *Affectus tuus operi tuo nomen imponit*. II d.38 (PL 192, ~744) treats the end of the will. The image of the single eye is not in d.38–41 (only an incidental *simplici oculo* at d.38 c.5), so it is Wyclif's own, from Matt. 6:22. Read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12b.md`) |
| P11k-5 | potestate-pape-11-k / n11 | *Non omnis* = C. 5 q. 5 c. 2, from Augustine Ep. 93 | verified | Gratian C.5 q.5 c.2, PL 187, 722A: rubric *Idem* [Augustine] *ad Vincentium Donatistam et Rogatistam, epist. XLVIII*, incipit *Non omnis, qui parcit, amicus est*; §2 *Et qui phreneticum ligat, et qui lethargicum excitat, ambobus molestus ambos amat*. Augustine, *Ep.* 93.2.4, PL 33, 323, has the same words. Read 2026-10-12. The canon *binds* the frenzied man. Wyclif's "deprived of his sword" is his own paraphrase. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12b.md`) |
| P11k-6 | potestate-pape-11-k / n12 | *De occidendis* = C. 23 q. 5 c. 8 (Loserth "Cap. VIII", 306.4), from Augustine Ep. 47 | verified | Gratian C.23 q.5 c.8, PL 187, 1218A: rubric *Augustinus ad Publicolam, epist. CLIV*, incipit *De occidendis hominibus*. It is Augustine, *Ep.* 47.5, PL 33, 186. The point Wyclif uses comes later in the canon: *Absit, ut ea, quae propter bonum ac licitum facimus … si quid per haec praeter nostram voluntatem cuiquam mali acciderit, nobis imputetur*. Read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12b.md`) |

| P11m-3 | potestate-pape-11-m / n5 | *Opus imperfectum* hom. 19 (p. 706 ff.) | verified | Ps.-Chrysostom, *Opus imperfectum* hom. 19 (on Matt. 7:15–16), PG 56, 739–40. The true Christian is known by confession agreeing with the scriptures, not by the name: *non nomen solum Christi Christianum facit, sed etiam veritas Christi … in nomine Christi multi ambulant, in veritate autem ejus pauci*. Wyclif's *Solum enim continuatos corpori Christi dicit esse christianos* is a summary (*dicit*), not a quotation; the words *continuatos corpori* do not occur. | P11m-7 | potestate-pape-11-m / n17 | Augustine reads *doluisse fertur … addidisse* | verified | PL 42, 39: *qui cum esset presbyter, doluisse fertur quod episcopus non potuit ordinari; et in Arianorum haeresim lapsus, propria quoque dogmata addidisse nonnulla, dicens …*. So *doluisse fertur … addidisse* is confirmed. Augustine's *nonnulla* goes with *dogmata*; the print's comma after *addidisse* moves it to *dicens*. | P11m-8 | potestate-pape-11-m / n17 | Augustine reads *quando quis voluerit* for the print's *cum quibus voluerit* | verified (claim corrected) | PL 42, 39: *nec statuta solemniter celebranda esse jejunia, sed cum quisque voluerit jejunandum, ne videatur esse sub lege*. Augustine has **cum quisque voluerit**, not *quando quis voluerit*. The print's *cum quibus voluerit* is a corruption of *cum quisque*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12b.md`) |
| P11m-9 | potestate-pape-11-m / n20 | D. 95 c. 5, Jerome, *Olim idem presbyter qui et episcopus* | verified (incipit corrected) | Gratian D.95 c.5, PL 187, 448C, rubric *Presbyter idem est qui episcopus, ac sola consuetudine praesunt episcopi presbyteris. Item Hieronymus ad caput I epistolae ad Titum*; incipit **Olim idem erat presbyter, qui et episcopus**. Loserth's foot at 316.14: "Decreti Prima Pars, Dist. XCV, cap. V." Read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-12b.md`) |
| P12a-1 | potestate-pape-12-a / n4 | D.95 c.5 *Olim* (Jerome); Jerome has *presbiterorum* for Wyclif's *sacerdotum* | verified | Gratian D.95 c.5, PL 187, 448C, rubric *Item Hieronymus ad caput I epistolae ad Titum*: *Olim idem erat presbyter, qui et episcopus, et antequam diaboli instinctu studia in religione fierent, et diceretur in populis: Ego sum Pauli, ego Apollo, ego autem Cephae, communi presbyterorum consilio ecclesiae gubernabantur*. The canon has *presbyterorum*. Wyclif's *sacerdotum* is his own: he writes *communi sacerdotum consilio* in his other citations of this canon too (latinworks19, line 548). Wyclif paraphrases the rest (*instigacione dyaboli* for *diaboli instinctu*). Loserth's foot at 317.24 reads "Decr. prima pars, dist. XCV, cap. V." Read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13a.md`) |
| P12a-2 | potestate-pape-12-a / n6 | Ps.-Chrysostom, *Opus imperf.* hom. 34: secular rule before the endowment | editor | Loserth's foot at 318.11 reads "Opp. tom. VI, 816." That is the opening column of hom. 34 (PG 56, 816–822, Matt. 20:1–16, the labourers in the vineyard). The whole of hom. 34 was read in the djvu text, and it does not say that secular lordship is prior to the clergy's endowment. The nearest passage is col. 818: darkness comes first *in saecularibus et laicis Christianis*, and now it has seized *sacerdotes positos in summo vertice spiritualium dignitatum*. The next homily (hom. 35, col. 830) sets secular primacy, which has *causa* and *utilitas*, against ecclesiastical primacy, which has neither. That is the passage Wyclif goes back to at 320.3 (P12a-4). Wyclif's homily numbers are not reliable: at 211.25 he calls hom. 35 "XXVIII", and Loserth corrects it. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13a.md`) |
| P12a-3 | potestate-pape-12-a / n10 | "II De civitate Dei XII": the ordinance of kings is from God | verified (corrected) | *De civ. Dei* II.12 (PL 41, ~57) is about Roman poets (*Quod Romani auferendo libertatem poetis …*), so Wyclif's "II.12" cannot be right. The claim fits V.21, PL 41, 167 (heading *Romanum regnum a Deo vero esse dispositum, a quo est omnis potestas*): *non tribuamus dandi regni atque imperii potestatem, nisi Deo vero … qui dedit Assyriis …*. Book IV's argument says the same (*cujus potestate ac judicio regna terrena constituuntur*). Read 2026-10-12. The correction to V.21 is an identification by sense, not a reading Wyclif gives. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13a.md`) |
| P12a-4 | potestate-pape-12-a / n12 | Chrysostom "ut supra": *neque racio neque utilitas* in taking ecclesiastical primacy | verified (corrected locus) | Ps.-Chrysostom, *Opus imperf.* hom. 35, PG 56, 830, read on the leaf image: *saecularem quidem primatum desiderare, etsi ratio non est, vel causa est: quia etsi justum non est, vel utile est; primatum autem ecclesiasticum concupiscere, neque ratio est, neque causa: quia neque justum est, neque utile. Quis enim sapiens ultro se subjicere festinat servituti, labori, dolori … ut det rationem pro omni Ecclesia*. Wyclif quotes the same sentence in full at 211.25–212 (as "Omelia XXVIII"), where Loserth's foot reads "Recte Homilia XXXV ex cap. XX." "Ut supra" points to 318.11 (hom. 34), but the saying is in hom. 35. Wyclif's *neque racio neque utilitas* compresses *neque ratio … neque causa … neque utile*. Read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13a.md`) |

| P12c-2 | potestate-pape-12-c / n1 | the chapter printed "II" (324.12, plate: print has II) | verified (corrected) | The chapter is XX.19 (heading *Quid apostolus Paulus Thessalonicensibus scripserit de manifestatione Antichristi*), PL 41, 685. | P12c-3 | potestate-pape-12-c / n5 | Augustine: the temple of God = the church; Antichrist with his body | verified | *De civ. Dei* XX.19.2, PL 41, 685–86: *Non enim templum alicujus idoli aut daemonis, templum Dei Apostolus diceret. Unde nonnulli, non ipsum principem, sed universum quadammodo corpus ejus … simul cum ipso suo principe hoc loco intelligi Antichristum volunt: rectiusque putant … in templum Dei sedeat, tanquam ipse sit templum Dei, quod est Ecclesia: sicut dicimus, Sedet in amicum, id est, velut amicus*. Augustine leaves open which temple is meant (*incertum est: utrum … templi … Salomone … an vero in Ecclesia*), and he reports the body reading as the view of *nonnulli*. Wyclif gives it as Augustine's. Loserth's "pag. 597" (325.29) does not fit his own "687" for the same chapter at 324.8. It is probably a misprint. Read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13a.md`) |
| P12c-4 | potestate-pape-12-c / n9 | Augustine reports Nero and the Roman-empire reading of *qui tenet*, and "approves" the second | verified | *De civ. Dei* XX.19.3, PL 41, 686. Of the Nero opinion Augustine says *multum mihi mira est haec opinantium tanta praesumptio*. Of the Roman-empire reading he says only *non absurde de ipso Romano imperio creditur dictum, tanquam dictum sit, Tantum qui modo imperat imperet, donec de medio fiat*. The third opinion (the faithful are to hold the faith until the hidden mystery of iniquity leaves the church; cf. 1 John 2:18–19) is the one Wyclif called *supradicta*. *Ego prorsus quid dixerit, me fateor ignorare* is verbatim. "Approves" overstates *non absurde*. Read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13a.md`) |
| P12c-5 | potestate-pape-12-c / n11 | Augustine "ch. 24": Antichrist from the Roman empire, which he will extinguish | untraced | Augustine nowhere says that Antichrist must be *de Romano imperio* and will extinguish it. Read: all of *De civ. Dei* (every passage with *Antichrist* near *Roman-*). XX.24 is on the Psalms. XX.19.3 has only *qui modo imperat imperet, donec de medio tollatur*. XX.23 (PL 41, 694–96) has the four kingdoms and the ten kings *in orbe Romano*. Augustine's *Epp.*, *Sermones* and *Enarrationes* (PL TEI) have no such passage. The nearest are Jerome, *Ep.* 121.11 ad Algasiam (PL 22, 1037: *non veniet Antichristus, nisi prius Romanum deleatur imperium*), Jerome *In Ier.* (PL 24, 839B), and Adso, *De Antichristo* (PL 40 App., 1131 ff., transmitted under Augustine's name). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13a.md`) |
| P12c-6 | potestate-pape-12-c / n3 | Augustine: *nulli dubium est … dixisse* | verified | *De civ. Dei* XX.19.2, PL 41, 685: *Nulli dubium est, eum de Antichristo ista dixisse; diemque judicii (hunc enim appellat diem Domini) non esse venturum, nisi ille prior venerit, quem refugam vocat*. Wyclif has *isto* for *ista*. Augustine's text of 2 Thess. 2:3 has *refuga* (cf. 12-c n2). Read 2026-10-12. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13a.md`) |

Same Sergius as P11c-3. The Bahira label is the usual one for the legend. Wyclif does not use it. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12d-2 | potestate-pape-12-d / n3 | "the church's judgement deceives and is deceived" (canonists) | verified | 2026-10-12. X 5.39.28 *A nobis* (Innocent III, "Idem" after c.27) (Friedberg II, Lib. V tit. 39): "iudicium Dei veritati, quae non fallit nec fallitur, semper innititur; iudicium autem ecclesiae nonnunquam opinionem sequitur, quam et fallere saepe contingit et falli". A papal text, so it fits Wyclif's *papa attestatur suam crebram decepcionem* ("saepe" = *crebram*). Wyclif does not cite it, so keep "probably". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |

PG 56, *Op. imperf.* hom. 51 (Migne numbers it 51 too), on Mt 24:51: "Quomodo sacerdos super omnem populum glorificatur, si benefecerit, non solum propter suam justitiam, sed propter omnium, quia causa est ipse justitiae universorum: ita et peccans super omnes punitur …". The wording matches Wyclif's. The columns (925–928) are Loserth's and were not legible in the OCR. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12e-4 | potestate-pape-12-e / n3 | D.40 c.6 *Si papa* (modern form ours) | verified | 2026-10-12. Gratian D.40 c.6 *Si papa* (PL 187, 214–215, TEI 21354). It is the sixth canon of Distinctio XL. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |

Same homily (Migne hom. 51), a few lines further on: "isti autem, quia fingunt se esse Christianos, cum sint Antichristi, id est, contra Christum. Item si quis videtur in Ecclesia esse, et agat contra Ecclesiam: et ille talis hypocrita dicitur". Wyclif's "ubi supra" is right. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12e-6 | potestate-pape-12-e / n3 | "Homily 44" (man worse than the devil) | verified (corrected) | 2026-10-12. Not Migne hom. 44. The passage is *Op. imperf.* **hom. 24** (Migne "Homilia xxiv, ex capite x", on Mt 10:17, "Cavete ab hominibus"): "homo malus pejor est quam ipse diabolus. Diabolus enim si videat justum, non est ausus ad eum accedere … sic diabolus etiam sine homine non valet aliquid contra sanctos." Searching the book for "pejor est diabolo" found no other hit. Wyclif's (or his scribe's) XLIV is probably XXIV. Loserth gives no apparatus variant. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12e-7 | potestate-pape-12-e / n6 | Grosseteste, *Ep.* 128 to Innocent IV, p. 432 | verified (Loserth's identification wrong) | 2026-10-12. Ep. 128 (Luard pp. 432–437) shares the Lucifer, *filius perditionis* and pastoral-defrauding themes. It lacks Wyclif's distinctive words. Those are in the Lyons memorandum (see P12e-8). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12e-8 | potestate-pape-12-e / n6 | the "sermon to the pope" = the Lyons memorandum of 1250 | verified | 2026-10-12. Grosseteste's memorandum read before Innocent IV and the cardinals at Lyons, 13 May 1250 (Brown, *Fasciculus* II, 250–257). p. 255: "Praesidentes huic sedi … cum communiter in hujusmodi obtemperatur, vera et perfecta advenit discessio, et in januis est revelatio Filii perditionis". pp. 252–253: "Causa, fons et origo hujus est haec CURIA" (Wyclif: *curia est causa multorum talium*). Later: souls "assimilatur … deificatur" (Wyclif: *assimilare Deo animas*). Wyclif's "sermo ad papam" is this text. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12e-9 | potestate-pape-12-e / n10 | Hus reuses the twofold-clergy passage (Opp. p. 226) | verified | 2026-10-12. Hus, *De ecclesia* ch. 15 (Schaff tr. pp. 159–160) has the whole passage: the two sects of clergy, the clergy of Christ resting in its head, the clergy of Antichrist "palliated", the clergy that "limps into two parts", the laity wavering, and the next paragraph (12-f's opening, 333.1 ff.). Loserth's "Opp. p. 226" was not checked against the 1558/1715 *Historia et monumenta*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12f-1 | potestate-pape-12-f / n3 | *virtus circa difficile* = Aristotle *EN* 2.3, 1105a9 | verified | 2026-10-12. *EN* 2.3, 1105a9 (Bekker line 9, before milestone 10): "περὶ δὲ τὸ χαλεπώτερον ἀεὶ καὶ τέχνη γίνεται καὶ ἀρετή". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12f-2 | potestate-pape-12-f / n9 | Augustine's harmonizing rule, *De consensu evangelistarum* | verified | 2026-10-12. Wyclif names no passage. Augustine states the rule in *De cons. ev.* II.19.47 (PL 34, 1100): "si quis praetermittat aliquid quod alius dicat, non esse contrarium; nec si alius alio modo aliquid dicat, dum eadem rerum sententiarumque veritas explicetur". He restates it at III.17 (PL 34, 1192): no evangelist contradicts another "si vel tacuit quod alius dixit, vel dixit quod alius tacuit". Augustine does not apply it to Acts 1:18 against Mt 27 in this work. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12f-3 | potestate-pape-12-f / n11 | *precium seminis* = bishops' revenue from fines on clerical unchastity | untraced | Searched all the Society OCR volumes for *precium seminis* (only this place), *culagium*, and fornication + money. No parallel. Wyclif does pair "effusio sanguinis vel seminis" as the two pollutions of a church: *Sermones* IV, p. 304, "de pollucione sanguinis vel seminis quibus ecclesie polluuntur"; latinworks10, "plus polluunt locum quam effusio sanguinis vel seminis". So the money could equally be fees for reconciling churches polluted by seed, or commuted penances and fines for sins of the flesh (not only the clergy's). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12f-4 | potestate-pape-12-f / n14 | *sors Domini* = the clergy as the Lord's lot, Jerome *Ep.* 52.5 | verified | 2026-10-12. Jerome, *Ep.* 52.5 (to Nepotian), PL 22, 531: "Si enim κλῆρος Graece, sors Latine appellatur: propterea vocantur Clerici, vel quia de sorte sunt Domini, vel quia ipse Dominus sors, id est, pars Clericorum est". He goes on to cite "Pars mea Dominus" (Ps. 15:5). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-13b.md`) |
| P12g-1 | potestate-pape-12-g / n2 | "God rewards adverbs" maxim, source | verified | 2026-10-12. The source is the *Glossa ordinaria* to the Decretals, X 5.22.1 (*Scripta fraternitatis*, De collusione detegenda; the chapter contains *qualitas*, the lemma). Rome 1582 ed., gloss: "ut dixit Io. quidam sapiens: Deus non est remunerator nominum, sed adverbiorum, hoc est dicere, non remunerat Deus bonum quod fit, sed quod bene fit". Wyclif cites the same gloss as "glossa Bernhardi" on "Extra De Collusione detegenda, capitulo primo in verbo qualitas" in *De civ. dom.* II c. 12 (ed. Loserth p. 139). He uses the maxim elsewhere too (*De ver. sacr. script.*; *Opus evangelicum*; latinworks25, 26), calling it what "theologi quam decretiste dicunt" and "wlgariter dicitur". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-14.md`) |
| P12g-2 | potestate-pape-12-g / n15 | Chrysostom: prelates are the stomach and cook of the church's body | verified (corrected) | 2026-10-12. The author is Ps.-Chrysostom, *Opus imperfectum in Matthaeum*, hom. 38 (on Mt 21:12), PG 56.839–840 (djvu text, under the "Homilia xxxviii ex capite xxi" heading before col. 841). The text: "Quemadmodum medicus … de stomacho ejus interrogat … ita si sacerdotium integrum fuerit, tota ecclesia floret … Cor autem et stomachus sacerdotes intelliguntur … Sicut enim stomachus accipiens cibum, coquit eum in seipso, et per totum corpus dispergit: sic et sacerdotes …". Wyclif paraphrases this ("cocus" stands for *coquit*). He cites the same figure, also attributed to "Crisostomum", in *De officio regis* (latinworks18, p. 65–66: "Sanato stomacho residuum corpus foret sanum") and in latinworks33 ("clerus in regnis sit stomachus"). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-14.md`) |

III De rescriptis (the next title is IV De consuetudine). Wyclif's paraphrase of the rule agrees with it, including *potior in praebenda*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-14.md`) |
| P12i-1 | potestate-pape-12-i / n13 | Decretum, *De consecratione* D.5 c.24 (our modern form) | verified | 2026-10-12. Gratian 21354, De cons. D.5 c.24 (PL 187.1862A), "Item Hieronymus. | P12j-1 | potestate-pape-12-j / n14 | "secundum Augustinum graciose donat omne debitum" = *Conf.* 1.4.4 (*reddis debita nulli debens, donas debita nihil perdens*) | verified | 2026-10-12. *Conf.* 1.4.4, PL 32.663 (TEI 7270): "Supererogatur tibi, ut debeas; et quis habet quidquam non tuum? Reddis debita nulli debens, donas debita nihil perdens." Wyclif's *cuilibet creature quodammodo obligatur* answers to *supererogatur tibi ut debeas*. This is a paraphrase, not a quotation. | P12k-1 | potestate-pape-12-k / n3 | *Robertus Gybbonensis* = Robert of Geneva (Clement VII, elected at Fondi, Sept. 1378), against Urban VI | verified | 2026-10-12. Loserth's index identifies him: "Robertus antipapa (Clemens VII.)" and "Clemens VII. antipapa, v. Robertus Gebennensis". Robert was elected at Fondi on 20 September 1378 (en.wikipedia "Antipope Clement VII", which gives that election date). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-14.md`) |
| P12k-2 | potestate-pape-12-k / n4 | "ut patet in materia de excommunicacione": Wyclif's treatment of excommunication elsewhere; no work named | untraced | Loserth gives no foot. His index has only "Wyclif De Excommunicacione 353" and "Schisma docet multas catholicas veritates, specialiter de excommunicacione 353", neither naming a work. I found no Wyclif work titled *De excommunicacione* in the Society OCR. Wyclif's long treatment of excommunication is *De civ. dom.* I cc. 37–40 (latinworks04, where "excommunic-" clusters), but nothing ties this phrase to it. *Materia* may just mean the topic in general. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-14.md`) |
| P12k-3 | potestate-pape-12-k / n13 | Augustine: the civil laws are "mixte multis nequiciis" | untraced | I found no Augustine text with *mixte multis nequiciis* or similar. A python window search of the whole Society OCR corpus (civil, imperial or human laws + Augustin + nequ-, iniquit-, mixt-, vici- or peccat-) returned only this passage. A PL Augustine search for *mixtae multis* gave nothing. The nearest thought is *De lib. arb.* 1.5.13 (PL 32.1228): "legem istam, quae populo regendo scribitur, recte ista permittere, et divinam providentiam vindicare". That is a parallel, not the source. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-14.md`) |
| P12l-1 | potestate-pape-12-l / n6 | Chrysostom, *Hom. in Ep. ad Hebr.* 2 (Latin version): "christianus debet sapere in penuria …", travellers to the royal city | verified | 2026-10-12. Chrysostom, *Hom. in Ep. ad Hebr.* 2, §§4–5, in Mutianus Scholasticus's Latin, PG 63.249–250 (column headers 247–248, 249–250, "MUTIANO SCHOL. INTERPRETE. — CAP. I. HOMIL. II."). The text: "Sane vero, si oportet altum sapere, in pauperie hoc oportet, non in divitiis facere. Quare? Quia si potes in paucis vitam tuam bene disponere, eo qui hoc non potest, multo major multoque melior haberis. 5. Dic enim mihi, si acceptis quibusdam ad regiam civitatem … nec jumentis … neque diversoriis, neque calceamentis …". Loserth's "Opp. tom. XII, 26" is the Montfaucon volume, which agrees. Wyclif paraphrases. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-14.md`) |
| P12l-2 | potestate-pape-12-l / n8 | *Inter querelas* = Decretum C.23 q.4 c.27 (Loserth prints "Causa XXII") | verified | 2026-10-12. Gratian 21354, C.23 q.4 c.27 *Inter querelas* (PL 187.1190A), Gregory to Januarius, "Pro injuria propria episcopo aliquem excommunicare non licet". I confirmed CAUSA XXIII, QUAESTIO IV and "C. XXVII" in the TEI, so Loserth's "Causa XXII" is a slip. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-14.md`) |
| P12m-1 | potestate-pape-12-m / n5 | Ch. 10 of this work recites a decree of Jerome, with Bernard (*De consideratione*) and Grosseteste | verified | 2026-10-12. Ch. 10 has all three. Jerome's decree is cited at p. 250 (chunk 10-h, "per decretum beati Jeronymi positum XXIV distinccione prima: Hec est fides, papa beatissime", = C.24 q.1 c.14). Lincoln (Grosseteste) is cited at pp. 257–258 (10-j, 10-k). Bernard's "librum ad papam Eugenium" (*De consideratione*) is cited at p. 265 (10-l). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-15.md`) |
| P12m-2 | potestate-pape-12-m / n6 | Gregory, *Reg. past.* 2.1 (shepherd and beasts) | verified | 2026-10-12. Gregory, *Reg. past.* 2.1, PL 77.25D (TEI 21435, "CAPUT PRIMUM [Al. | P12m-5 | potestate-pape-12-m / n11 | Gregory, *Reg. past.* 2.1 ("Oportet namque…") | verified | 2026-10-12. *Reg. past.* 2.1, PL 77.26D–27A: "Oportet namque ut metiri sollicite studeat quanta tenendae rectitudinis necessitate constringitur, sub cujus aestimatione populus grex vocatur. | P12n-1 | potestate-pape-12-n / n1 | The "letter to Cyril" = Ps.-Augustine, *Ep. ad Cyrillum de magnificentiis Hieronymi* | verified | 2026-10-12. Ps.-Augustine, *De magnificentiis S. Hieronymi* (*Ep. ad Cyrillum*), PL 22.288 (TEI 7127). After Jerome appears to him following Jerome's death, the writer says: "Testis enim est Deus … quod inde tantus in me exstitit caritatis fervor, quod plus alieno bono gaudeo, quam meo? plus affecto omnibus subesse, quam praeesse." This is Wyclif's "Testor Deum, quod a tempore illo pocius desideravi subesse quam preesse". (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-15.md`) |
| P12n-2 | potestate-pape-12-n / n1 | Augustine, *Ep.* 134 to Apringius (*non tam praeesse quam prodesse*) | verified (corrected) | 2026-10-12. *Ep.* 134.1, PL 33.511 (TEI 7300) does read "cujus utilitatibus servio, cui non tam praeesse quam prodesse desidero", as Loserth says. But Wyclif names the letter to Cyril, and the words he quotes are in Ps.-Augustine (P12n-1). Loserth's "Videtur esse" is superseded. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-15.md`) |
| P12n-3 | potestate-pape-12-n / n6 | *Decretum* D.40 c.6 *Si papa* | verified | 2026-10-12. | P12o-1 | potestate-pape-12-o / n1 | Higden, *Polychronicon* VIII, 268, the Franciscan-only papacy story | verified (corrected) | 2026-10-12. Higden, *Polychronicon* VIII, p. 268 (Rolls Series): "Nicholaus quartus, de ordine fratrum Minorum, post Honorium quartum, sedit papa annis quasi sex". MSS C and D add (p. 268 apparatus): "Hic declaravit regulam fratrum Minorum per constitutionem quandam, cujus sequaces Nicholaitae vocantur". Higden does not tell the story of the papacy restricted to Franciscans. Loserth's "Cf." covers only the Franciscan order; the restriction comes from the manuscripts' own margin. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-15.md`) |
| P12o-2 | potestate-pape-12-o / n1 | The Franciscan pope is Nicholas IV (1288–92) | verified | 2026-10-12. Higden VIII.268, as above. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-15.md`) |

It ends "propter ecclesiam, quam a Christo tuendam suscipiunt", which is Wyclif's *tueri*. In Isidore, PL 83.723B (TEI 8218), it is *Sent.* 3.51.4 ("CAPUT LI. Quod principes legibus teneantur"). | P12s-2 | potestate-pape-12-s / n4 | the twelve abuses recall Ps.-Cyprian, *De duodecim abusivis saeculi* | verified | PL 4, 869ff.: the treatise counts twelve *abusionis gradus*, from *Primus abusionis gradus est, si sine bonis operibus sapiens* to *Duodecimus … populus sine lege*. | P12s-3 | potestate-pape-12-s / n6–8, 14 | Gregory, *Reg. past.* II.6 (MSS *XVII*) | verified | PL 77, 34–38, II.6, headed *CAPUT VI [Al. XVII]*. Read there: *Petrus namque auctore Deo … a bene agente Cornelio* (36A); *Ne enim praesidentis animus ad elationem potestatis suae delectatione rapiatur … Ducem te constituerunt* (37A); Matt 24:48 and *Inter hypocritas enim jure deputatur* (37B–C); *nec praeesse se hominibus gaudeant, sed prodesse … pastores pecorum … terror vester ac tremor* (34C–D). The MSS' *XVII* is the older running count, so it is not an error. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-17.md`) |
