| P12s-5 | potestate-pape-12-s / n10 | Decretum D.99 c.3 *Primae sedis* (modern form ours) | verified | PL 187, 471B–473A. D.99 c.3 *Primae sedis* is Carthaginian, not Gregory, but its *II Pars* (Gratian) reads *Universalis autem nec etiam Romanus Pontifex appelletur*. c.4 *Nullus patriarcharum* is Pelagius II. Gregory's own text is c.5 *Ecce in praefatione*, to Eulogius (*universalem me Papam dicentes*). Wyclif names Gregory, so c.5 fits best and c.3 is the source of the wording. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-17.md`) |

| P12t-4 | potestate-pape-12-t / n7 | Ps.-Chrysostom, *Opus imperfectum*, hom. 27 | untraced | Loserth has no foot. Migne's hom. 27 (PG 56, on Matt 11:25ff.) has nothing on betraying Christ. | P13a-2 | potestate-pape-comp-a / n14 | Bernard against the pope needing lordship = *De consideratione* | verified | Ch. 5, 86–87 (chunk 05-c ¶1). The first objection is that the pope could not fulfil his office without kingdoms and lordships, and Wyclif answers with Bernard *libro IV ad Eugenium* (*Inter hec tu pastor procedis deauratus … Petrus hic est …*). This is *De consid.* IV.3.6, PL 182, 776A, verified under **P05c-1** (corrected from IV.2.3). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-18.md`) |
| P13a-3 | potestate-pape-comp-a / n16 | the three objections of the lord of Armagh = FitzRalph, *Summa in quaest. Armen.* | verified | Ch. 5, 96.21–99 (chunks 05-e ¶2–4 and 05-f): *Tres tamen evidencias innuit dominus Ardmacanus*. (1) Someone must rule as head, and that is Peter (96.21). (2) Much was said to Peter singly, Matt. 16:19 (97.6). (3) Paul had his licence to preach from Peter, Gal. 2:9 (98.27). The treatise names no book here. All three are in FitzRalph, *Summa* **VII.6–7** (scan p. 100; see above). This agrees with Wyclif's own "VII.6" at 95.8 (P05e-1; RESULTS-3 item 4 confirms VII.6 *distinguit in apostolis duplicem potestatem*). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-18.md`) |
| P13a-4 | potestate-pape-comp-a / n20 | Bernard, Augustine and Chrysostom: the primacy does not imply lordship | verified | Ch. 7, reply to the first argument (135–138, chunk 07-a): Bernard, *De consid.* II.6.10–13 and III.1.2 (**P07a-4, P07a-5, P07a-7, P07a-8**), and Augustine, *Sermo* 67.7 (**P07a-3**). The Chrysostom is in the reply to the second argument: hom. 5 on 1 Tim., 139.31 (chunk 07-b n5; **P07b-3**, read in English only; P07b-2). The treatise also cites Ambrose here (P07a-1, untraced). | P13a-8 | potestate-pape-comp-a / n24 | Lincoln = Grosseteste, on the danger to a prelate | verified | Ch. 7, 152 (chunk 07-f ¶1, n4): after *notaret papa ex preeminencia dignitatis periculum*, Wyclif quotes Grosseteste's letter to Hugh of Pateshull, ed. Luard p. 99, verified under **P07f-2** (and P07f-3). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-18.md`) |
| P13b-1 | potestate-pape-comp-b / n3 | Augustine's three conditions of Antichrist | verified | Ch. 8, 187–189 (chunks 08-f, 08-g): the testimonies on Antichrist's disciples are drawn from Augustine, *Sermo* 129 on John 5 (*De verbis Domini* 45 in Wyclif's numbering), §§3, 6, 8, verified under **P08f-2, P08f-3, P08g-1**. The second (*Secundum testimonium*, John 5:43) and the third (*Tercium testimonium*, John 5:44) were read. The first was not separately checked. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-18.md`) |
| P13b-2 | potestate-pape-comp-b / n4 | which three popes were slain | verified | Ch. 8, 190–191 (chunk 08-g). (1) Sabinian, struck in the head in a vision by Gregory, died: Higden V, 412 (**P08g-5**; Wyclif names the *Flores historiarum*). (2) Innocent IV, struck by the dead Grosseteste: Higden VIII, 240–42 (**P08g-6**). Wyclif adds that these saints incurred no irregularity *ex percussione letali summorum pontificum*, so *a beatis* means "by the saints". (3) Marcellinus, *exutus ab illo officio … martyr effectus est* under Diocletian: D.21 c.7 *Nunc autem* (**P08g-7**). This is the compendium's *tercius papatu deposito … a tyranno*. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-18.md`) |

| P13b-4 | potestate-pape-comp-b / n9 | the chronicle of a pope's subsidy petition from the English church | verified | Ch. 10, 223–224 (chunk 10-b ¶1, n2): *in predicta cronica que intitulatur Flores historiarum*, s.a. 1226, the nuncio Otto presents the pope's (Honorius III's) letter to the council at Westminster asking for a subsidy. *Flores historiarum*, ed. This is only c.2 *Sacrosancta* (Anacletus), PL 187, 123A–124A, verified under **P10c-1** (and P10c-2). c.1 *Omnes* is not cited. (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-18.md`) |
| P13b-6 | potestate-pape-comp-b / n14 | Jerome's decree = C.24 q.1 c.14 *Haec est fides* | verified | Ch. 10, 250 (chunk 10-h): *per decretum beati Jeronymi positum XXIV distinccione prima … Hec est fides, papa beatissime*. This is C.24 q.1 c.14, PL 187, 1269B, verified under **P10h-2**. The text is really Pelagius, *Libellus fidei* (**P10h-3**). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-18.md`) |
| P13b-7 | potestate-pape-comp-b / n15 | *floridis* = the *Flores historiarum* (see p. 223) | editor | Loserth's foot to 405.16: "Rectius: Florum Historiarum. Vide supra p. 223." But the sixth argument summarised is treatise ch. 10, 256–259 (chunks 10-j, 10-k). Lincoln's saying on the cardinals there comes from Grosseteste's own letter 36, ed. Luard p. 125 (**P10j-5**), with letter 35 (**P10k-3**), not from the *Flores*. And at 258.30–259.1 Wyclif says Grosseteste flattered the pope *verbis floridis*, then goes straight on to *tam necessarius articulus fidei*, which is the compendium's next clause. So *dictum floridis* most likely echoes *verbis floridis*, "the flowery saying" (for *floridum*). (see `chunks/potestate-pape/_verify/RESULTS-18.md`) |

| Iprol-1 | incarnacione-prol / n1 | *Tractatus De Anima* "tasted beforehand" = Wyclif's *De compositione hominis* (Beer 1884) | untraced | Neither Harris nor Beer makes the identification. Harris's preface (latinworks03, ll. 262–265) speaks of "the De Anima and other early philosophical treatises" as still unedited in 1886, two years after Beer's *De compositione hominis*. Beer's introduction (latinworks08, ll. 190–245, 655–700) describes the MSS (C has *De benedicta incarnatione* and *De composicione hominis* in the same codex) but never ties his text to the *De Anima* named here. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |

"Many men" is the *Scriptum super Sent.* III d.1 q.2 a.5 ad 2 (*dicerentur duo homines*) and ad 3 (*plures homines, et non unus homo diceretur*) (snp3001). So Wyclif's *sicut dictat sentencia sancti Thome* matches the Sentences commentary. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |

| I01a-9 | incarnacione-01-a / n17 | Augustine, *Contra Maximinum* II, *creatorem constituit* | verified (wrong attribution) | *non creatorem creavit, sed creaturam constituit* is not in *Contra Maximinum* II (7435.xml has no *creatorem creavit*, *non creatorem* or *creaturam constitu*). It is **Maximinus** speaking, *Collatio cum Maximino* 15.13, PL 42, 729 (7431.xml). The subject is the Son (*Filius … non creatorem creavit, sed creaturam constituit*), and the text reads ***creaturam***. The quotation before it, *Dicis Patrem infectum … facta sunt omnia*, is Augustine's, *Collatio* 14 (PL 42, 723), which he repeats in *Contra Maximinum* I.17 (PL 42, 756), so Wyclif's "libro primo" is right. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |

PL 33, 519–522. Aug. has *operatur et semina*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |

| I01b-4 | incarnacione-01-b / n7 | Augustine's rule *quid et secundum quam naturam* | verified | No Augustine text reads *secundum quam naturam* (PL grep: hits only in later authors). The Lombard cites this for the question whether Christ is a creature (*Sent.* III d.11 c.1, PL 192, 778–779). Cf. *Ep.* 187.8 *quid secundum quid dicatur intuendum*, which Wyclif quoted at 5.1. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| I01b-5 | incarnacione-01-b / n9 | *gigas gemine substancie* = Ambrose, *Intende qui regis Israel* | verified | Ambrose, hymn *Veni redemptor gentium* (= *Intende qui regis Israel*), PL 16, 1411 (6982.xml): *Geminae Gigas substantiae*. Augustine himself uses the line, *Contra sermonem Arianorum* 8.6, PL 42, 689: *idem ipse Christus, geminae gigas substantiae, secundum quid obediens, secundum quid aequalis Deo*. This fits Wyclif's "Augustine, Jerome … understood". 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| I01b-6 | incarnacione-01-b / n10, n16 | Jerome, *In Gal.* I end (Gal. 3:13); does Jerome read *vermem*? | verified (wrong work) | The passage is Jerome, ***In Ephesios*** I, on Eph. 2:10, PL 26, 471A (7186.xml): *Et quia semel ad nomen creaturae venimus … nos libere proclamamus, non esse periculum eum dicere creaturam, quem **vermem**, et hominem, et crucifixum, et maledictionem, tota spei nostrae fiducia profitemur*. It is not *In Gal.* (7187.xml has nothing like it). Jerome only paraphrases Prov. 8:22 (*dicit se creatam initium viarum Dei*), and the full Prov. 8:22–23 is Wyclif's. Jerome reads *vermem* (so A B C are right) and *fiducia* for Wyclif's *reverencia*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |

| I02b-2 | incarnacione-02-b / n2 | *De haer.* 55 (Apollinarists) | verified | *De haeresibus* 55, PL 42, 40 (7445.xml): *Apollinaristas Apollinaris instituit, qui de anima Christi a Catholica dissenserunt, dicentes, sicut Ariani, Deum Christum carnem sine anima suscepisse … mentem … defuisse animae Christi, sed pro hac ipsum Verbum in eo fuisse … Verbi aliquid in carnem fuisse conversum atque mutatum, non autem carnem de Mariae carne fuisse susceptam*. | I02b-4 | incarnacione-02-b / n9 | *Contra Maximinum* "I and III, c. 22" (A B C 23) | verified (locus corrected) | There is no book 3 of *Contra Maximinum*, which has two books (7435.xml). The matches are these. (a) *Collatio cum Maximino*, PL 42, 722 (7431.xml), Augustine speaking: *Legimus enim, Qui plantat et qui rigat unum sunt … sed ambo homines erant, ejusdem substantiae … Non dixit, Ut ipsi et nos unum: sed, Ut ipsi sint unum*. (b) *Contra Maximinum* II, **c. 22**.1, PL 42, 792: *Quoniam poposci ut proferres ubi legeris, Unum sunt, dictum esse de aliquibus rebus quae non essent unius ejusdemque substantiae … Si enim non diligerent invicem, natura unum essent, dilectione non essent: si autem unum natura non essent, unum dici dilectione non possent … non solum natura, quod jam erant; verum etiam perfectione charitatis atque justitiae, pro suae capacitate naturae, quantum in Dei regno esse potuerint: ut etiam ipsi summe unum sint in natura sua, quemadmodum Pater et Filius summe unum sunt, quamvis in excellentiore atque incomparabiliter meliore natura sua*. Wyclif's "book 1 and book 3, c. 22" therefore counts the *Collatio* as book 1 and *Contra Maximinum* I–II as books 2–3; I02c-5 points the same way. (c) The *Homines … per consorcium … omnia in omnibus* quotation before it is *Ep.* 238.13, PL 33, 1043, nearly word for word. Augustine's *potuerint* supports A B C *potuerunt*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-2.md`) |

| I02c-3 | incarnacione-02-c / n6 | *De Anima*: Aristotle's, or Wyclif's own treatise? | verified | The book is Wyclif's own *De Anima*, the treatise the prologue names as the introduction to this one (*Prelibato tractatu De Anima qui introductorius est*, latinworks03 l. 1503). Beer, ll. 1219–1220 and 2015–2018): *eadem persona corpus spiritus et connexio utriusque vel natura integra resultans ex ambobus*, and the person is *communis ad corpus ad animam et ad naturam … resultantem*. Wyclif's *non confundendo naturas* is closer to the second. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-2.md`) |
| I02c-5 | incarnacione-02-c / n17 | *Contra Maximinum* I c. 11, the synodal anathema *ex nihilo* | verified (wrong attribution) | *Si quis ex nihilo Filium dicit* is not in *Contra Maximinum* I (7435.xml, c. XI and the whole book). It is **Maximinus** speaking to Augustine in the *Collatio cum Maximino* 15.13, PL 42, 730 (7431.xml): *Quod quidem audi auctoritatem synodicae lectionis, quia in Arimino patres nostri inter caetera et hoc dixerunt: Si quis ex nihilo Filium dicit, et non ex Deo Patre, anathema sit*. So the synod is Ariminum (359), not Nicaea. Augustine answers in *Contra Maximinum* II.14.3, PL 42, 772: *nec ego Nicaenum, nec tu debes Ariminense … proferre concilium*. Wyclif turns the address round (*Audi, inquit, Maximiniane*) and reads *locucionis* for *lectionis*. His book 1 is the *Collatio*, as in I02b-4. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-2.md`) |

Verbi* c. 6 (all the manuscripts give 6) | verified (locus corrected) | Schmitt's c. 6 (II, 20–21) is on another question: whether the incarnation of the Son entails that of the Father and the Spirit. The doctrine Wyclif cites is c. 11, Schmitt II, 28.23–29.3: *ita in Christo una persona est plures naturae et plures naturae sunt una persona … Non enim est alius deus, alius homo in Christo, quamvis aliud sit deus, aliud homo*. With it goes c. 9, II, 24.19–21: *non sic assumpsit hominem, ut natura dei et hominis sit una et eadem, sed ut persona dei et hominis una eademque sit* (also PL 158, 10610.xml, which has no chapter heads after IV). Anselm's *alius/aliud* is exactly the distinction of n5's C note. Wyclif's "6" (all the MSS) must follow another chapter division; *De compositione hominis* c. 1 cites the same doctrine as *de incarnacione verbi 7° ca°*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-2.md`) |
| I02d-2 | incarnacione-02-d / n10 | Aristotle, *Ethics* "8.5": the friend as "another self" (NE IX.4 / IX.9) | verified (locus corrected) | Aristotle, *NE* IX.4 (Perseus, Rackham, Bekker 1166a). In 1166a7–8 a friend is one *who shares his friend's joys and sorrows*. In 1166a31–32 the good man feels towards his friend as towards himself, *for a friend is another self*. Both points of Wyclif's third supposition are in IX.4, not in VIII.5. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-2.md`) |
| I02d-3 | incarnacione-02-d / n12 | Augustine, *Tract. in Ioh.* 78 on John 14:28 | verified | Aug. *Tract. in Ioh.* 78.3, PL 35, 1837 (7323.xml): *quia naturae humanae gratulandum est, eo quod sic assumpta est a Verbo unigenito, ut immortalis constitueretur in coelo, atque ita fieret terra sublimis, ut incorruptibilis pulvis sederet ad dexteram Patris … hoc erat ire ad eum et recedere a nobis, mutare atque immortale facere quod mortale suscepit ex nobis, et levare in coelum … Quis non hinc gaudeat … atque id se speret futurum esse per Christum?* So Augustine's words end at *per Christum*, and the next sentence (*Nec dubito …*) is Wyclif's. Augustine has *immortalis* (A B C omit it) and *fieret* (A B C *in terra*). 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-2.md`) |

There, as here, the whole hope rests on Christ's lowliness and is set against the fear of calling him a creature. Wyclif takes the first answer. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-3.md`) |

| I03d-2 | incarnacione-03-d / n6, n9 | "Ome. 78" = *tract.* 78 (John 14:28); was a clause like *sic unus est Christus Deus et homo* lost at 41.24–26? | verified | *Tract. in Ioh.* 78.2–3, PL 35, 1836 (head: John 14:27–28). 78.2: *Neque enim homo Christus non dicitur Filius Dei, quod etiam sola caro ejus in sepulcro meruit appellari … cum credimus in Dei Filium qui sepultus est, profecto Filium Dei dicimus et carnem quae sola sepulta est … Quid igitur, haeretice … quid esset homo?* 78.3: *Agnoscamus geminam substantiam Christi … ne sit quaternitas, non Trinitas Deus. So a clause **was** lost at 41.24–26, by eye-skip from *caro* to *caro*. This is Wyclif's two parts: the same by union to the Word, varied as to form. "de Christo ca. 50" = q. 50 of the *tertia pars*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-4.md`) |
| I03d-5 | incarnacione-03-d / n19 | "ex dictis De Anima": Aristotle, or Wyclif's own treatise? | verified | Harris's index, among *other works of W.'s referred to*: *De Anima, 1, 7, 22, 44, 203, 230*, so he takes p. 44 as Wyclif's own treatise (as at I02c-3). Beer p. 41.1–9): *eadem forma substancialis, que mansit in corpore vivo, manet ad tempus in corpore mortuo; sed et hoc consonat fidei nostre de corpore Cristi, quod iacuit in sepulcro*. XXVII) says only that the Godhead stayed inseparable from soul and body in death and the hypostasis stayed one. It has no *totus*/*totum*. The distinction is III.7 (NPNF ch. VII): *He is then wholly* [ὅλος] *perfect God, but yet is not simply* [ὅλον] *God … wholly … to His subsistence*, the masculine going with the hypostasis and the neuter with the nature. The application to tomb and hell is the Lombard's, *Sent.* III d. 22 c. 3, PL 192, 804 (11468.xml): *Christus eodem tempore totus erat in sepulcro, totus in inferno, totus ubique … sed non totum*. | I04a-2 | incarnacione-04-a / n5 | Scotus, III d. 22 q. un. (*Ord.*/*Lect.*), quoted at 49.25–50.21, 50.23–51.3, 51.8–15 | verified | Scotus, *Ord.* III d. 22 q. un. (Wadding VII/1). (a) Humanity is an *entitatem absolutam realiter distinctam* destroyed in the triduum. (b) *Christus* has *non … vnum conceptum*, so *oportet rationem illius subiecti esse in se veram … alioquin … haec vera, maius Deo est*. (c) The reply to arg. 1: *nego consequentiam, sicut nec sequitur nullo homine existente, homo albus est homo albus, igitur homo albus est homo*. (d) The reply to arg. 3: *non placuit Doctoribus ut denominaretur ab illis … et forte non sine ratione … nec carnalis, quia … sonat in vitium … Exemplum de albedine … Et similiter de quantitate*. Wyclif's quotations are close paraphrase. *Caesar est homo* is from the same question. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-4.md`) |
| I04a-3 | incarnacione-04-a / n7 | Damascene "ca. 49" (Burgundio's numbering, book III): "Christ" as the name of the hypostasis | verified | Wyclif has the number from Scotus, *Ord.* III d. 22 (Wadding VII/1): *secundum Damascenum … 49. In the Greek division this is III.3 (NPNF: *by the word Christ we understand the name of the subsistence, not in the sense of one kind, but as signifying the existence of two natures*). That Burgundio's chapter is 49 was not checked. Scotus's *significativum* bears out A B C *significancium* (50.11) against the print's *singulatim*, and his *maius Deo* bears out the print against A B C *de eo*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-4.md`) |

| I04c-1 | incarnacione-04-c / n2 | Cowtonus = Robert Cowton OFM, the Doctor's "abbreviator" | untraced | Harris, note on 57.24 (latinworks03, ll. 19772–19784): *Who is this Colton or Cowton who abridged Duns Scotus? Robert Cowton OFM (Oxford, *Sentences* c. 1309–11) is the likely man: his Book III is described as an echo of Scotus's teaching, and an *abbreviatio* of his commentary (by Richard Snettisham) circulated. Q. 2 (438–440) has the same for the flesh. Q. 3, *Utrum Verbum unitum fuerit carni et animae duplici unione* (440–441), respondeo: *unio dicitur tribus modis … actio unientis … passio unibilis … relatio unitorum … Unica enim unione univit sibi Verbum carnem et animam*. Ad 3: *si teneam equum per frenum … non tamen duplici tentione teneo utrumque, sed unica*. Bonaventure nowhere says *de substancia fidei*. That is Wyclif's gloss. CCCCXCI and CCCCXCII; q. 13 follows). Sed contra: *tunc non fuisset verum illud dictum prophetae, Non dabis sanctum tuum videre corruptionem*. Resp.: *putrefactum non fuisset nec incineratum, sed deus sua ordinatione integrum custodisset … Eodem igitur miraculo quo tunc corpus Christi assumpsit cum anima sibi coniuncta, ipsum conservavit a corruptione ipsa anima separata*. Ev. cxix. 6*). **At 62.19–20 the source supports the print (O) *Quis, inquit, ita dormivit*** against A B C *quod, inquit, ideo (C cita) dormivit*: Augustine's *Quis ita* is interrogative, and A B C's *quod … ideo* is a misreading of *quis … ita*. | I04d-3 | incarnacione-04-d / n17 | Augustine and Hugh on the dead body as God (John 19) | verified | Wyclif names no places (Harris's margin: *Augustinus*). Augustine, *In Ioh.* 78.2, PL 35, 1836 (cf. I03d-2): *cum credimus in Dei Filium qui sepultus est, profecto Filium Dei dicimus et carnem quae sola sepulta est*. Hugh, *De sacr.* II.1.11, PL 176, 401A–B: *mortuus est etiam Deus, quia Deus homo fuit … et Christus persona in sepulcro jacuit, et secundum solam carnem … Absit! Sed vere mortuus est, et vere sepultus*. John 19:42, *ibi … posuerunt Iesum*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-5.md`) |
| I05a-1 | incarnacione-05-a / n2 | Chrysostom's teaching that no one is harmed except by himself = *Quod nemo laeditur nisi a seipso* | verified | The PG is not local, and no Latin text was found online. A medieval Latin version did circulate (Edinburgh MS, *de eo quod nemo leditur nisi a se ipso*, inc. *Scio quod crassioribus quibusque*; Paris incunable *Liber de eo quod nemo laeditur nisi a semetipso fuerit laesus*). Read in Stephens's NPNF I/9 translation (EWTN text). §1: *no one of those who are wronged is wronged by another, but experiences this injury at his own hands*. §17 restates it. Wyclif's *non potest sibi inexistere nisi oriatur principaliter a se ipsa* is this thesis. Harris's general index has *Chrysostom, St., 66* and no locus. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-6.md`) |
| I05a-2 | incarnacione-05-a / n9 | Augustine "Ome. 47": "Absit quod Christus pro triduo animam suam dimiserit…" = *Tract. in Ioh.* 47; Wyclif's summary? | verified | Aug. *Tract. in Ioh.* 47.10, PL 35, 1738 (7323.xml; John 10:17–18): *Fidelem latronis animam non deserebat, et deserebat suam? Absit: sed illius ut Dominus custodivit, suam vero inseparabiliter habuit*. Harris's Index II: *Ome. 47*, *Tract. in Jo. Ev. xlvii. 10*. The number is the received one. *Pro triduo* and *dimiserit* are Wyclif's. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-6.md`) |
| I05a-3 | incarnacione-05-a / n10 | Aristotle, *Physics* II.3 (195a): the absent pilot as cause of the shipwreck | verified | Arist. *Phys.* II.3, 195a11–14. Harris (note on 67.28) quotes the Greek (*τὴν ἀπουσίαν τοῦ κυβερνήτου τῆς τοῦ πλοίου ἀνατροπῆς, οὗ ἦν ἡ παρουσία αἰτία τῆς σωτηρίας*). The Latin was read in Thomas, *In Physic.* II l. 5 n. 7 (cpy012): *idem est causa contrariorum quandoque; sicut per suam praesentiam gubernator est causa salutis navis, per absentiam autem suam causa est submersionis eius*. Perseus returned 503. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-6.md`) |
| I05a-4 | incarnacione-05-a / n11 | Thomas "De Christo, art. 70" (O *50*) = *ST* III q. 50 aa. 2–3? | verified | *ST* III q. 50 a. 2 co. (sth4046): *id quod per gratiam Dei conceditur, nunquam absque culpa revocatur … Cum igitur in Christo nullum fuerit peccatum, impossibile fuit quod solveretur unio divinitatis a carne ipsius*. A. 3 co. extends it to the soul (*multo minus separatum est ab anima*). This is Wyclif's *racio*: no dimission without preceding demerit. Harris's note on 68.4 quotes the same sentence (*S. T. III … Migne, iv. 462*), but his Index II files p. 68 under *De Christo, 70*, the printed number. **The source supports O's *50* against the print's *70*.** Wyclif's "De Christo" numbers by question of the *tertia pars* (cf. I03d-4, "ca. 50" = q. 50). 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-6.md`) |
| I05b-1 | incarnacione-05-b / n11 | Leo, "in quodam sermone": *tanta fuit unicio Dei et hominis ut nec supplicio posset dirimi nec morte distingui* | verified | Leo, *Sermo* 68 (al. 66), *De passione Domini* 17, c. 1, PL 54, 373A (7631.xml): *cum in tantam unitatem Dei et hominis natura convenerit, ut nec supplicio potuerit dirimi, nec morte disjungi*. Wyclif has *tanta fuit unicio … posset … distingui* for Leo's *in tantam unitatem … potuerit … disjungi*. (*Sermo* 51.2, PL 54, 309C, *ut nec supplicio posset affici, nec morte dissolvi*, is a false lead: it describes Peter's mistaken belief.) Harris has no note here. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-6.md`) |
| I05c-1 | incarnacione-05-c / n5 | Augustine "Ome. 44" (O *48*) on John 10:36 = *Tract. in Ioh.* 48? | verified | Aug. *Tract. in Ioh.* 48.9, PL 35, 1745 (7323.xml; John 10:36): *Forte aliquis dicat: Si Pater eum sanctificavit, ergo aliquando non erat sanctus? Sic sanctificavit, quomodo genuit. Ut enim sanctus esset, gignendo ei dedit, quia sanctum eum genuit. Nam si quod sanctificatur, ante non erat sanctum; quomodo dicimus Deo Patri, Sanctificetur nomen tuum*. Harris's Index II pairs p. 73 *Ome. 44* with *Tract. in Jo. Ev. xlviii. 9*. **The source supports O's *48* against the print's *44*.** Foot p073: *3 48 O*. Wyclif's *sed si sic eum sanctificavit, quomodo genuit?* turns Augustine's statement into a question, and his *aliquando* stands for *ante*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-6.md`) |
| I05c-2 | incarnacione-05-c / n21 | "in materia De Adnichilacione" = Wyclif's own *De adnichilacione* | verified | Harris's index, *other works of W.'s referred to*: *De Adnichilacione, 76, 78*. The treatise is *De potencia productiva Dei ad extra* cc. 12–14 (*Summa de ente* II tr. 6), printed as the *fragmentum de annihilatione* by Dziewicki, *De ente* (1909), pp. 287ff. (latinworks11). P. 289: *deus non posset adnichilare substanciam aliquam, nisi adnichilet totam universitatem creatam*. P. 293: *si deus potest mundum adnichilare, tunc et quodlibet eius accidens … motu, tempore … que deus de potencia absoluta non potest corrumpere antequam naturaliter corrumpantur*, the same examples as 76.10–14 here. I did not find *vacuum vel defectus in Deo* in the fragment. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-6.md`) |
| I05d-1 | incarnacione-05-d / n11 | Anselm: the least absurdity (*inconveniens*) in God means impossibility = *Cur Deus homo* I.10 | verified (locus corrected) | The maxim is in the place Wyclif names, *De incarn. Verbi*, and on this very question: Schmitt c. 10, II, 26.3–5 (PL 158, 276C, Migne c. V): *Quoniam ergo quamlibet parvum inconveniens in deo est impossibile, non debuit alia dei persona incarnari quam filius. Illo enim incarnato nullum sequitur inconveniens*. The same maxim is in *CDH* I.10, Schmitt II, 67.4–5: *Sicut enim in deo quamlibet parvum inconveniens sequitur impossibilitas* (Schmitt's apparatus cross-refers the two). Harris, note on 78.23: *inconveniens should have been in the text as it is in the passage of Anselm referred to. De Inc. Verbi, V. c. 5*. Foot p078 n. 18: *inconue^s (i.e. inconveniens) O M*. **Anselm's word supports O M *inconveniens* against the print's *inconsequens*.** Wyclif's chapter number (6) is neither Schmitt's 10 nor Migne's 5. Schmitt c. 6 has *nullum inconveniens sequi* and *innumera inde nasci inconvenientia*, but not the maxim. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-7.md`) |
| I05d-2 | incarnacione-05-d / n12 | Anselm, *De incarn. Verbi* 6, 7, 9; *CDH* "6"; why neither Father nor Spirit was incarnate = *De incarn. Verbi* 10, *CDH* II.9 | verified | Schmitt *De incarn. Verbi* c. 9, II, 23.7–25.4: the incarnation of one person does not entail the others', *sed impossibile*. C. 10, II, 25.6–26.9: why the Son rather than the Spirit or the Father (two sons in the Trinity; the Father would be *nepos*). *CDH* II.9, II, 105: *in qua persona deus … hominem assumat*, which refers back to the letter *ad domnum papam Urbanum*. Wyclif's 6, 7, 9 fit neither Schmitt's division nor Migne's: in PL 158, Schmitt cc. 6–9 = c. IV and c. 10 = c. V. *CDH* "6" is unplaced. II.6 is on satisfaction by the God-man and does not fit. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-7.md`) |
| I05d-3 | incarnacione-05-d / n13 | "materia De Adnichilacione" = Wyclif's own (the *De ente* fragment, pp. 287 ff., per I05c-2) | verified | Reuses I05c-2. Harris's index: *De Adnichilacione, 76, 78*, which names this page. The work = *De potencia productiva Dei ad extra* cc. 12–14, ed. Dziewicki, *De ente* (1909), pp. 287ff. The conditional reading of the authorities was not looked for in the fragment. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-7.md`) |
| I05d-4 | incarnacione-05-d / n14 | Augustine "contra Maximinianum": God could have produced another Son (Maximinus speaking?) | untraced | Searched the *Collatio* (7431.xml) and *Contra Maximinum* I–II (7435.xml) for *alium filium*, *alterum filium*, *potuit generare*, *impotens*. Harris has no note and no Index II entry for p. 79. The nearest is Augustine's own answer, not Maximinus', at *Contra Maximinum* II.12.3, PL 42, 768: the Son did not beget a creator, *Neque enim non potuit, sed non oportuit. Immoderata enim esset divina generatio, si genitus Filius nepotem gigneret Patri*. That is the conditional mode of speech Wyclif describes, but it concerns the Son, not "God", so it is only perhaps his place. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-7.md`) |
| I05d-5 | incarnacione-05-d / n15 | Anselm, *CDH* II.5 | verified | Schmitt *CDH* II.5, II, 99.14–100.28, on why God's doing what he began is not done *cogente necessitate*: *deus nihil facit necessitate … quae scilicet necessitas non est aliud quam immutabilitas honestatis eius … et idcirco improprie dicitur necessitas*. This is Anselm speaking of God by an improper mode, the mode Wyclif means. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-7.md`) |
| I05d-6 | incarnacione-05-d / n16, n17 | quotation = *Enchiridion* 40 (c. 12), Wyclif's "3"; Augustine reads *et de Maria virgine sicut filius, insinuat* | verified | Aug. *Enchir.* 40 (CAPUT XL in PL), PL 40, 252 (7406.xml): *profecto modus iste quo natus est Christus de Spiritu sancto non sicut filius, et de Maria virgine sicut filius, insinuat nobis gratiam Dei, qua homo nullis praecedentibus meritis … ut idem ipse esset filius Dei qui filius hominis, et filius hominis qui filius Dei: ac sic in naturae humanae susceptione fieret quodam modo ipsa gratia illi homini naturalis, quae nullum peccatum posset admittere*. **Augustine's text supports O's *sicut filius insinuat* at 79.11** (foot p079 n. 4: *sicut filius insinuat O*). Wyclif has *non ut eius filius* for *non sicut filius* and leaves out *et filius hominis qui filius Dei*. Harris (note on 79.9) and his Index II (*Enchiridion 36*) give instead *Enchir.* 36, on the same grace: *Quid enim natura humana in homine Christo meruit*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-7.md`) |
| I05e-1 | incarnacione-05-e / n1 | *De haeresibus* modern numbers (Ebionites 10 … Photinians 45, Sabellians 41) | verified | *De haer.*, PL 42 (7445.xml), from the section heads: X Ebionaei (p. 27ff.), XXXIII Theodotiani, XLI Sabelliani (32), XLIV Pauliani (34; *nec eum aliquid amplius quam hominem putant*, which Wyclif quotes), XLV Photinus, XLIX Ariani (39), LV Apollinaristae, LVIII Metangismonitae (41), LX Proclianitae, LXI Patriciani, LXX Priscillianistae (44), LXXIII *divinitatem doluisse, cum figeretur caro ejus in cruce* (44). All of the note's modern numbers are right. Wyclif's 49 for Photinus is the Arians' number. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-7.md`) |
| I05e-2 | incarnacione-05-e / n3 | Philaster (Filastrius) is Augustine's source, not a heretic | verified | *De haer.* 45, PL 42, 34 (Photinus): *Philaster autem continuatim ponit ambos sub singulis et propriis numeris*. Also 41, PL 42, 32: *Philaster autem Brixianus episcopus*. Harris, note on 80.26: *W. has tripped here. Philaster is several times quoted by Augustine as the author of a catalogue of heresies, not as himself a heretic*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-7.md`) |
| I05e-3 | incarnacione-05-e / n6 | "materia De Universalibus" = Wyclif's *De universalibus* | verified | Harris's index: *De Universalibus, 81, 164*. | I05e-4 | incarnacione-05-e / n10 | Thersites (*Iliad* 2); Wyclif's source | untraced | Identification: Wyclif uses the name the same way in *De apostasia* c. 15, ed. Wyclif has it under Leo's name and with *conventio* from Bonaventure, *In III Sent.* d. 11 a. 2 q. 1 arg. 1 (Quaracchi III, 249): *Leo Papa: Nova et inaudita conventio: Deus, qui est et qui erat, fit creatura*. Quaracchi n. 2 makes the identification. Wadding's *30*, read on the plate, is probably a misprint for 50. If Scotus's 49 is III.3 (RESULTS-4), then 50 is III.4, which fits. Burgundio's numbering was not seen. | I07d-3 | incarnacione-07-d / n7 | the "10th genus" is the category of having | verified (locus corrected) | *Categories* 4, 1b25–2a4 (classics.mit.edu): the list ends *position, state* [ἔχειν, *habere*], *action, affection*, so "having" is eighth in Aristotle's own order. That order is standard but I did not read it in a text. *Categories* 15, 15b17ff.: the senses of "to have", from a habit or disposition to clothing and a ring. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| I07d-4 | incarnacione-07-d / n11 | *Dialogus ad Felicianum* (Vigilius, PL 42): the humanity an accident to the Word | verified | Vigilius, *Contra Felicianum* c. 9, PL 42, 1163 (7452.xml): the human birth is not common to Father and Son, *sed accidens speciale*, *illud proprium quod per accidens uni noscitur evenisse personae*. This is the only place in the work where *accidens* is used of the incarnation. Harris (note to 119.18): *W. gives the substance of Aug. ad Felic. cap. xii., but adds the logical technicalities*. In fact c. 12 (PL 42, 1167) is on the twofold birth and has no *accidens*. The nine genera, the substance and the coeval *passio* are Wyclif's. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| I07d-5 | incarnacione-07-d / n12 | *De Trin.* I.13.28 *talis erat illa susceptio* (Wyclif *unio*) | verified | Aug. *De Trin.* I.13.28, PL 42, 840 (7441.xml): *Ex forma enim servi crucifixus est, et tamen Dominus gloriae crucifixus est. Wyclif writes *unio* (A *unionis*) and *fuit*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| I07d-6 | incarnacione-07-d / n1 | the schools' maxim that actions belong to supposits | verified | The maxim derives from Arist. *Metaph.* I.1, 981a16–17: actions concern the individual (I did not read this passage). The exact form, with *passiones* attached as in Wyclif: FitzRalph, *De pauperie Salvatoris* (in Poole's *De dominio divino*, 1890, latinworks09 l. 21551), *secundum philosophos, acciones sunt suppositorum sive singularium, ita sunt similiter passiones*. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-10.md`) |
| I07e-1 | incarnacione-07-e / n4 | "Jerome" *passus est Dei Filius non putative sed vere* = Pelagius, *Libellus fidei* under Jerome's name | verified | Pelagius, *Libellus fidei* 5, PL 45, 1717 (7493.xml): *Passus est enim Dei Filius non putative, sed vere, omnia quae Scriptura testatur*. | I07e-2 | incarnacione-07-e / n5 | Augustine, *Enchiridion* 40, as Wyclif cites it | verified | Aug. *Enchir.* 40, PL 40, 252 (7406.xml, the same reading as I05d-6): not on the passion but on the unity of person, *ut idem ipse esset filius Dei qui filius hominis, et filius hominis qui filius Dei*. | I08b-3 | incarnacione-08-b / n4, n5 | Augustine, *In Ioh. tract.* 19.15: does it read *Filio Dei* and *habet hominem*? | verified | Aug. *Tract. in Ioh.* 19.15, PL 35, 1552–1553 (7323.xml): *Sed ipse est filius hominis, qui et Filius Dei. Haerendo enim ad unitatem personae filius hominis Filio Dei, facta est una persona … Filius Dei, quod est Verbum Dei, habet hominem, tanquam anima corpus. Sicut anima habens corpus, non facit duas personas, sed unum hominem; sic Verbum habens hominem … Quid est homo? Anima rationalis habens corpus. Quid est Christus? Verbum Dei habens hominem*. (1) The source **supports A B C's *Filio Dei*** at 127.23–24 against the print's *Filius Dei*. It has one *filius hominis* followed by *Filio Dei*, so the rejection of A B C's first *filio* stands. (2) The source **reads *habet hominem***, not *animam*, at 127.27. Harris says the same in his Notes (to 127.27): *Since the text was printed I have found that hominem is the word in Aug.* Augustine has *tanquam anima corpus*, and Wyclif adds *habens*. Wyclif's "Ome. 19" is the received number (A B C "9"). 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-11.md`) |
| I08b-4 | incarnacione-08-b / n6 | Hugh, *De sacr.* II.1.11, "rational soul having a body" | verified | Hugh, *De sacr.* II.1.11 (*Quod Christus separata anima a carne et persona fuit et Deus et homo*), PL 176, 401B–411A (11082.xml). At 409B: *Anima quippe in quantum est spiritus rationalis, ex se et per se habet esse personam, et quando corpus ei sociatur non tantum ad personam componitur, quantum in personam apponitur*. This is Wyclif's *sentencia*, that every man is a soul or spirit having a body. Hugh does not use the formula "rational soul having a body" (it is Augustine's, *In Ioh.* 19.15), so the note's quotation overstates. 2026-10-22 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-11.md`) |

| I08e-7 | incarnacione-08-e / n15 | "forms are given according to the disposition of matter", the Avicennian maxim | untraced | The exact wording *formae dantur secundum dispositionem materiae* is not found. The doctrine is Avicenna's *dator formarum*: lower agents only dispose matter, and the giver of forms gives the form once matter is fully disposed. The Stanford excerpt of the Latin Avicenna has *quando disponitur dispositione completa ad recipiendum formam*. Urbano (Loconsole 2023, 71) has *agentia … solum disponunt materiam ad susceptionem forme et forma postea inducitur a decima intelligentia, quam Avicenna appellat datorem formarum*. The maxim in this form is the schools'. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-12.md`) |
| I08e-8 | incarnacione-08-e / n19 | five and six as circular numbers: Boethius, *Inst. arith.* II | verified | Boethius, *De inst. arith.* II.30 (*De circularibus vel sphaericis numeris*), PL 63, 1137B–C (7746.xml): *numerus ille cyclicus vel sphaericus appellatur, ut sunt multiplicationes quae a quinario vel senario proficiscuntur. Nam quinquies quinque, qui fit 25, ab 5 progressus, in eosdem 5 desinit … Quinquies enim 25 fiunt 125*, and *idcirco cyclici vel sphaerici vocantur, quod sicut sphaera vel circulus, in proprii semper principii reversione formantur*. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-12.md`) |
| I09a-1 | incarnacione-09-a / n6 | Anselm, *De incarn. Verbi* "c. 7" ("Sicut in Deo una natura…"); modern chapter may be c. 11 | verified (locus corrected) | See the section above. Schmitt c. 11, II, 28.23–29.1, with the objection at 28.14–22. PL 158, 278B–C (10610.xml), Migne *CAPUT VI [al. IV]*. The text agrees with Wyclif's quotation (Anselm *et tamen non tres dii*, Wyclif *et non tamen tres dii*). Wyclif's "7" runs one above Migne's division here and at I05d-1. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-12.md`) |
| I09a-2 | incarnacione-09-a / n6 | inner quotation follows *Quicunque* vv. 15–16 | verified | *Quicumque*, PL 88, 585B (8323.xml): *Ita Deus Pater, Deus Filius, Deus Spiritus sanctus; et tamen non tres dii, sed unus Deus* (vv. 15–16). Anselm (Schmitt II, 28.25–26) has *pater est deus, et filius deus, et spiritus sanctus deus, et tamen non tres dii, sed unus est deus*. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-12.md`) |
| I09a-3 | incarnacione-09-a / n8 | *aliud et aliud, non alius et alius*: Gregory Nazianzen, *Ep.* 101, and Latin fathers | verified | Greg. Naz., *Ep.* 101, read in the Latin quoted by Rusticus, *Contra Acephalos*, PL 67, 1226C (7875.xml): *Non alius vero et alius: absit … E converso quam se in Trinitate habet: illic enim alius et alius … non aliud vero et aliud*. Rusticus's own formula is at 1178B: *aliud et aliud, non alius vero et alius: absit*. The Latin form Wyclif quotes is Anselm's, c. 11 (Schmitt II, 29.1): *Non enim est alius deus, alius homo in Christo, quamvis aliud sit deus, aliud homo*. I did not check the Augustine and Jerome that Wyclif names. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-12.md`) |
| I09a-4 | incarnacione-09-a / n10 | Anselm, *De incarn. Verbi* 1, on universals | verified | Anselm, *De inc. Verbi* c. 1, Schmitt II, 9.20–10.11 (PL 158, 265A–C, Migne c. II). After the *dialecticae haeretici, qui non nisi flatum vocis putant universales esse substantias* comes *Qui enim nondum intelligit quomodo plures homines in specie sint unus homo: qualiter in illa secretissima et altissima natura comprehendet quomodo plures personae … sint unus deus?* At 10.9–11 (PL 265C) the same holds for the incarnation: *quomodo … intelliget hominem assumptum esse a Verbo, non personam*. This is Wyclif's "the means to the mystery of the Trinity [and] of the incarnation". Wyclif's "1" = Schmitt's 1 (as at I02b-5). 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-12.md`) |
| I09a-5 | incarnacione-09-a / n11 | Aristotle, *De anima* II.4, 415b13 | verified | Aristotle, *De anima* II.4, 415b8–14 (Smith): the soul is *the essence of the whole living body … in the case of living things, their being is to live, and of their being and their living the soul in them is the cause or source* (Latin *vivere viventibus est esse*, 415b13). 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-12.md`) |
| I09a-6 | incarnacione-09-a / n12 | Anselm, *De incarn.* "7": universal form the same as its supposits | verified | Anselm c. 11 (Wyclif's 7), Schmitt II, 29.3–4 (PL 158, 278C): *cum profertur ,homo', natura tantum quae communis est omnibus hominibus, significatur*. With c. 1, *plures homines in specie sint unus homo*, this is the ground Wyclif claims. The proposition *quelibet universalis forma est idem cum omnibus et singulis eius suppositis* is not Anselm's wording. Harris's Note on 145.19: *W.'s formula is 'quelibet universalis forma est idem cum singulis ejus suppositis'*. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-12.md`) |
| I09a-7 | incarnacione-09-a / n16 | Nestorians' argument, beginning of Anselm's "c. 7" | verified | Schmitt c. 11 opens with it, II, 28.14–22 (PL 158, 278B, Migne c. VI): *Dicunt enim quidam: Quomodo dicimus in Christo non esse duas personas, sicut duas naturas? … et homo assumptus persona est, quia omnis homo individuus esse persona cognoscitur … ita duae in illo videntur esse personae*. Wyclif's *in principio ca° 7* is exact. Anselm says *quidam*, and the name "Nestorians" is Wyclif's. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-12.md`) |
| I09b-1 | incarnacione-09-b / n2 | Trinity works the union, the Word alone assumes: Lombard, *Sent.* III d. 1 | verified | Lombard, *Sent.* III d. 1 c. 4 (Migne's numbering), PL 192, 758–759 (11468.xml): *Filius, non Pater vel Spiritus sanctus, carnem assumpsit. | I09b-2 | incarnacione-09-b / n12 | God Lord from time: Augustine, *De Trin.* V.16.17 | verified | Aug. *De Trin.* V.16.17, PL 42, 921–922 (7441.xml): *Quod de Deo ex tempore dicitur, relative dicitur, non accidentaliter … si dominus non dicitur, nisi cum habere incipit servum, etiam ista appellatio relativa ex tempore est Deo … Ecce Dominum esse non sempiternum habet*. | I09c-1 | incarnacione-09-c / n1 | "in materia De Trinitate": the topic, or Wyclif's own *De Trinitate* | verified | Topic, not the treatise. Harris's subject Index lists p. 150 under *Trinity*. No Wyclif *De Trinitate* appears in Harris's references. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09c-2 | incarnacione-09-c / n5 | Anselm, *De incarn. Verbi* (Wyclif "7"): the nature of the species is each one of men | verified | Anselm, c. 7 in Wyclif's numbering (Schmitt c. 11; PL 158, c. VI), 278B–D (10610.xml). The text has *Sicut enim in Deo una natura est plures personae* and then *personam designamus quae cum natura collectionem habet proprietatum, quibus homo communis sit singulus* (278D). Wyclif's *singulus hominum* is Anselm's *homo communis sit singulus*. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09c-3 | incarnacione-09-c / n6 | *Quicumque* v. 37 and Augustine's frequent use of the soul-flesh analogy | verified | As I08e-3 (RESULTS-12). *Quicumque*, PL 88, 586B: *Nam sicut anima rationabilis et caro unus est homo, ita Deus et homo unus est Christus*. Aug. *In Ioh.* tr. 78.3, PL 35, 1836. *Ep.* 137.3.11, PL 33, 520. Also *Ench.* 11.36, as the Lombard recites it in *Sent.* III d. 6 (PL 192, 768): *quemadmodum una est persona quilibet homo, anima scilicet rationalis et caro, ita sit Christus una persona, Verbum et homo*. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09c-4 | incarnacione-09-c / n8 | Augustine, Jerome and Anselm say "Verbum hominem assumpsit" | verified | Augustine, *Ench.* 11.36, PL 40, 250: *gratia suscepit hominem tanta unitate personae*. *De Trin.* XIII.18.23, PL 42, 1032: *Cur Filius Dei hominem suscepit*. The Lombard, *Sent.* III d. 5, PL 192, 767, says Augustine *in pluribus … locis hujusmodi utitur locutionibus: Ille homo a Verbo est assumptus*. Anselm, PL 158, 275C: *Deus non sic assumpsit hominem*, and c. VI, 278B: *postquam hominem assumpsit*. For Jerome the phrase is found only in works under his name in PL 26 that are not his: *Breviarium in Psalmos*, 877C (*hominem assumptum esse ad dexteram Dei Patris*), and *Comm. in Job*, 691B. It was not found in genuine Jerome. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09c-5 | incarnacione-09-c / n9 | Anselm names the singular humanity "Iesus Christus" | verified | Anselm, c. VI, PL 158, 278D–279B. The text has *vel proprio nomine Jesum, personam designamus*, then *in assumpto vero homine, vel in nomine Jesu, intelligitur cum natura … collectio proprietatum*, and *Verbum, et illum assumptum hominem, id est Jesum*. Anselm names the singular assumed man *Jesus*; the equation of this with Wyclif's singular *humanitas* is Wyclif's own. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09c-6 | incarnacione-09-c / n10 | *homo* signifies *quale quid*: Aristotle *Cat.* 5, 3b13–21; Anselm *De grammatico* | verified | Aristotle, *Cat.* 5, 3b13–21 (Edghill): a secondary substance is *a class with a certain qualification* and genus and species *signify substance qualitatively differentiated* (Boethius has *qualem quandam substantiam*). Anselm, *De grammatico* c. XIX, PL 158, 578B–C: *homo significat ut unum substantiam et qualitates, quibus constat homo … principaliter significativum substantiae … et non qualitas, sed quale*. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09c-7 | incarnacione-09-c / n13 | Aristotle's argument "homo tunc esset albedo" | verified | Aristotle, *Metaph.* VII.6, 1031a19–28 (Ross). For accidental unities the thing and its essence are not the same: *if they are the same, the essence of man and that of white man are also the same*. Wyclif's argument has the same form: *humanitas* = *esse hominem*, so if humanity were white, being man would be being white. His conclusion *homo esset albedo* is not Aristotle's wording, and no closer place was found (*Cat.* 5 and *Metaph.* VII.4 also checked). Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09c-8 | incarnacione-09-c / n14 | Augustine, *Tract. in Ioh.* 19, "Maria humanitatem genuit…" | verified (locus corrected; paraphrase) | Not in *Tract.* 19 (PL 35, 1543–1552), where neither *humanitas* nor the cross occurs. No PL text has *humanitatem genuit*. The source is *Tract.* 119.1, PL 35, 1950: *non divinitatis, sed infirmitatis matrem … nunc vero quod Maria pepererat, pendebat in cruce*. It is prepared at *Tract.* 8.9, PL 35, 1455: *quia genuisti infirmitatem meam, tunc te cognoscam, cum ipsa infirmitas pendebit in cruce*. Wyclif's "Ome. 19" is most likely 119 with the C dropped; he cites *Ome. 119* elsewhere in this work (latinworks03 l. 6295). Augustine's *infirmitas* becomes Wyclif's *humanitas*. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09c-9 | incarnacione-09-c / n14 | Wyclif's "Enchiridion 29" = *Ench.* 12.38; *paulo ante* = 10.35 | verified (locus corrected) | *Ench.* 12.38 (Migne c. XXXVIII), PL 40, 251 (7406.xml): *cum illam creaturam quam Virgo concepit et peperit, quamvis ad solam personam Filii pertinentem, tota Trinitas fecerit; neque enim separabilia sunt opera Trinitatis*. The *paulo ante* is a few lines earlier in the same 12.38: *utraque substantia, divina scilicet atque humana, Filius est unicus Dei Patris omnipotentis*, which Wyclif echoes word for word (*divinam scilicet et humanam*). Ench. 10.35 does not have it. Wyclif's "29" is his own numbering and does not match Migne's. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09d-1 | incarnacione-09-d / n6 | Anselm's "clericus in fide devius" is Roscelin; A B C *Nestorius* a reader's gloss | verified | Anselm, *De inc. Verbi* c. I, PL 158, 262A: *praesumpta est a quodam clerico in Francia talis assertio: Si in Deo … tres personae sunt una tantum res … sicut tres angeli*. *Ep.* II.41 to Fulco (Schmitt *Ep.* 136), PL 158, 1192D: *Roscelinus clericus dicit in Deo tres personas esse tres res ab invicem separatas, sicut sunt tres angeli*. Harris's Note on 155.19 identifies Roscelin, and his Intro (latinworks03 l. 656–662) calls the A B *Nestorius* *a strange historical ignorance*. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09d-2 | incarnacione-09-d / n12 | Augustine, *De praed. sanct.* 15.31, as recited by the Lombard, *Sent.* III d. 6 | verified (distinction corrected) | *De praed. sanct.* 15.31, PL 44, 982: Rom 1:4, *Praedestinatus est ergo Jesus … Praedestinata est ista naturae humanae tanta et tam celsa et summa subvectio*. The Lombard recites it at *Sent.* III **d. 7**, PL 192, 774 (*Quod videtur August. notasse in lib. ad Prosperum et Hilar.*), not d. 6. Scotus and Bonaventure treat Christ's predestination under d. 7 as well. *Sent.* III d. 6 (PL 192, 768) recites instead *Ench.* 11.36, *ex quo homo esse coepit, non aliud coepit esse homo quam Dei Filius*. That passage fits Wyclif's *naturalis*, as does *Ench.* 10.35, *unicus Dei Filius, non gratia, sed natura*. Wyclif's "Dist. 6ª" fits the Enchiridion recital. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |

Tom. V. App.* (Index II: *Sermo 233*). Ps.-Aug. Also at PL 56, 587A (*Libellus Augustini de fide catholica contra omnes haereses*, Leo appendix). Wyclif's title comes through the Lombard, *Sent.* III d. 5, PL 192, 767: *Aug., in Expositione Symboli, sub anathemate tradit, dicens: Si quis dixerit …*. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-13.md`) |
| I09d-4 | incarnacione-09-d / n18 | *natura prope nichil* echoes Augustine's *prope nihil* (prime matter) | verified | Aug. *Conf.* XII.6.6, PL 32, 828: *quiddam inter formatum et nihil, nec formatum nec nihil, informe prope nihil*. | I10a-1 | incarnacione-10-a / n6 | Scotus, *In III Sent.* d. 1 q. 1: the threefold dependence/negation (Wyclif "Questione articuli 3ⁱ") | verified | Scotus, *Ordinatio* III d. 1 q. 1, in the third article (Lychetus marks *Tertius articulus* before it), n. 9, Wadding VII.1 pp. 16–17: *distinguendum est inter dependentiam actualem, potentialem, & aptitudinalem: & hoc vocando aptitudinalem, quae semper quantum est de se esset in actu … Et potentialem voco absolute illam, ubi nulla est impossibilitas ex repugnantia … respectu potentiae activae supernaturalis*. Personhood in a creature is the negation of actual and aptitudinal dependence; the negation of the third cannot be in any creature with respect to the Word. The Wadding scholion names the three *actualis, aptitudinalis, & obedientialis*. Wyclif's "Questione articuli 3ⁱ" = q. 1, art. 3. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-14.md`) |

| I10d-1 | incarnacione-10-d / n3 | Augustine, "aliud Deus aliud homo sed non alius, quia non alia persona" | untraced | The wording *aliud Deus aliud homo sed non alius, quia non alia persona* is not in Augustine's *Ench.*, *De Trin.*, *Tract. in Ioh.* or *Ep.*, or in the Lombard. Aristotle sets this *according to a part* case apart from change *by accident* (224a21); Wyclif's *per accidens* blurs the two. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-15.md`) |

It has *latria*, the two kinds of *dulia* (one *soli humanitati Christi exhibenda*), and *pietas* as the worship due to God. Augustine's testimony follows in c. 2 (*Ego dominicam carnem … ideo adoro, quod a divinitate suscepta et deitati unita est*; then *En. in Ps.* 98). Lombard's d. 8 is on whether the divine nature is born of the Virgin. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-16.md`) |

| I11a-1 | incarnacione-11-a / n1 | Scotus, *Ord.* III d. 1 q. 1: two respects of an accident to its subject; the five arguments | verified | Scotus, *Ord.* III d. 1 q. 1. The two respects are in the first article, n. 3, Wadding VII.1 p. 6: *Accidens enim ad substantiam … duplicem habet habitudinem, scilicet informantis ad informatum … Aliam habet, ut posterioris naturaliter ad prius, a quo dependet … Huic simillima est ista habitudo, quae est dependentia naturae humanae ad personam divinam*. Wyclif's quotation is a paraphrase. His 2° (the divine person can terminate any dependence) is the second article, n. 4ff., p. 7. His 3° (singularity before personality) and 4° (an accident existing of itself in the Eucharist) are the first two of three reasons that Scotus reports in the third article, n. 7, p. 10 (*Ista rationes non ostendunt propositum*). Scotus rejects them. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-16.md`) |

VI): *personam designamus quae cum natura collectionem habet proprietatum, quibus homo communis fit singulus … eadem est Verbi, et assumpti hominis proprietatum collectio*. What individuates Jesus is the collection of properties he shares with the Word through the union. Anselm does not say in so many words that the hypostatic union individuates. Re-read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-16.md`) |

| I12d-3 | incarnacione-12-d / n2 | "ut patet in methaphisica" (214.9) | untraced | Harris gives no foot-note at 214.9 (latinworks03 l. 18050), and Index II has only *De Substantia* for p. 214. The only parallel is Wyclif, *De compositione hominis* c. 2 (latinworks08 l. 2528, Beer p. 23.1–4): *significant principaliter quale quid, id est qualitatem substancialem, ut album solam qualitatem significat, sicut homo humanitatem … ut patet in Predicamentis*. Wyclif rejects these at 158.21–23 (*similitudo non est probabilis*). These are the arguments current "today" that anything can be identified with anything. Wyclif's *ut arguitur* (215.30) fits them, though the step to *quilibet terminus … significaret quidlibet* is his own. Read 2026-10-23. (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-18.md`) |

Arg. 2 there has *Jesus est Petrus*. The chunk note had it backwards: this is the "many men" position, not the "single man" one. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-19.md`) |
| I13b-1 | incarnacione-13-b / n1 | Scotus, *Ord.* III d. 1 q. 3: neither many men nor one man | verified | Scotus, *Ord.* III d. 1 q. 3 (*Utrum una persona possit assumere plures naturas*), Wadding VII.1, 44–45. Arg. 1 is *aut esset unus homo, aut plures*. In ad 1 the answer *dicitur, quod nec esset unus homo, nec plures* is one Scotus reports. Lychetus assigns it to Richard of Middleton. Scotus rejects it (*Sed contra, vnum et multa … immediate diuidentia ens*), then rejects Thomas's "one man", then answers *tertio modo* from the analogy of one spirator and two spirantes. Lychetus records that one exposition read Scotus himself as "neither one nor many". So Wyclif's attribution follows that reading. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-19.md`) |
| I13b-2 | incarnacione-13-b / n2 | Aristotle, *Cat.* 5, 2b5–6: if the first substances are destroyed, nothing else remains | verified | Aristotle, *Cat.* 5, end of the argument at 2a34–2b6 (classics.mit.edu): *if these last did not exist, it would be impossible for anything else to exist*. The Bekker line was not read in a numbered text. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-19.md`) |
| I13c-1 | incarnacione-13-c / n2 | *peccatum naturale* = Aristotle's fault of nature, *Phys.* II.8 | verified | Aristotle, *Phys.* II.8 (classics.mit.edu): *monstrosities will be failures in the purposive effort*. Wyclif does not cite Aristotle, so this is the note's gloss, and it fits. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-19.md`) |

This is a possibility granted only under the condition of a will. Anselm's words are *si vellet*, not *si Deus voluerit*. Thomas, *Scriptum* III d. 1 q. 2 a. 3 co., uses the same device on this same question: *non dicimus Deum posse nisi sub conditione, scilicet si vellet*. Harris's Notes on 229.14 explain *si voluerit* but give no Anselm locus. *De inc. Verbi* (10610.xml) has no such phrase. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-19.md`) |

| I13d-1 | incarnacione-13-d / n5 | "Questione 7 et duabus sequentibus" = *Scriptum* III d. 1 q. 2 a. 3–5 (serial); "omnis necessitas et impossibilitas Deo subicitur" | verified | Thomas, *Scriptum* III d. 1 q. 2 a. 3 (*Utrum pater potuit carnem assumere, et etiam spiritus sanctus*), snp3001. Counted serially it is article 7, because d. 1 q. 1 has four articles. *3ii* = of book III. The quotation is s. c. 1: *sicut dicit Anselmus, omnis necessitas et impossibilitas Deo est subjecta … Ergo patrem incarnari non est impossibile*. Wyclif's 2nd argument (*aeque distant … in infinitum*) is s. c. 2, his 3rd (*posse assumere carnem est dignitatis in filio*) s. c. 3. A. 4 is the same nature assumed by three, and its s. c. 1–2 (image of the Trinity; powers of the soul) are Wyclif's 229.14–29. A. 5 is one person assuming two natures. Anselm's source is *CDH* II.17 [al. 18], PL 158, 421C: *Omnis quippe necessitas, et impossibilitas, ejus subjacet voluntati*. 2026-10-23 (see `chunks/incarnacione/_verify/RESULTS-19.md`) |

No member is lost. The "other things" are set against the Trinity, which the sentence before says is to be believed rather than expounded. Wyclif's *Cum … credatur* joins two sentences. | Kprola-8 | eucharistia-prol-a / n16 | Hugh of St Victor, presumably *De sacramentis* II.8 | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 3 bt p073.29–p074.20: "Hugonis, IIº De Sacramentis, Parte VIIIª in cuius IXº capitulo". I read PL 176, 468 (Pars octava, cap. IX "Qualis intelligenda est mutatio panis"): "conversio... non secundum unionem sed secundum transitionem credenda est". (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprola-9 | eucharistia-prol-a / n19 | III Decretals = X 3.41.6 *Cum Marthe*, three opinions on the water in the chalice | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 4 bt p103.10–p104.12: "decretalis libro III De Celebracione missarum capitulo Cum Marthe... recitando multas opiniones stolidas non diffinit. Prima sentencia... Secunda... tercia". These are the three opinions on the water, and the passage quotes "Potest... dici, quod aqua non transit in sanguinem". I read Friedberg X 3.41.6 *Quum Marthae*, including "potest dici, quod aqua non transit in sanguinem". The same decretal is also at 29.14. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprola-10 | eucharistia-prol-a / n21 | the order of knowing = Aristotle *Post. An.* II.1 | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 4 bt p109.27–p110.5: "Sicut enim questio 'quid est' presupponit questionem 'si est'... ut patet IIº Posteriorum in principio". Loserth foot 109.30: "Aristoteles, Analyticorum Posteriorum II, 1". I read Post. An. II.1 (classics.mit.edu, Mure): the four questions (that, why, whether it is, what it is). The *De ecclesia* claim is unchecked and unneeded. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-1 | eucharistia-prol-b / n1 | Scotus, Henry (of Ghent?) and FitzRalph hold *Ego Berengarius* not abrogated (ch. 5) | verified | 2026-11-07, ch. 5 read at bt p115–119. The claim is wrong: Scotus, Henry and FitzRalph are touched as opponents or witnesses. The threefold proof that *Ego Beringarius* is not abrogated (p117.9–25: "primo... secundo... tercio") is Wyclif's own, and the subject of *docet* is the chapter. Scotus at 115.12: "super quartum in principio distinccione X". Loserth foot: "Scoti Reportata, lib. IV, dist. X, q. I, schol. I, Opp. tom. XI, 1, pag. 632". Henry is explicitly "Henricus Gaunt quodlibeto IXº, questione IXª" (116.20). Loserth foot: "Heinricus de Gandavo, Quodlibeta, Paris. 1580". FitzRalph at 119.8: "dominus Armachanus libro IX capitulo . . .", chapter left blank. Scotus and Henry loci not opened (Loserth's, as quoted). (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-2 | eucharistia-prol-b / n1 | *Ego Berengarius* = *De cons.* D. 2 c. 42 | verified | 2026-11-07. Same Friedberg reading as Kprola-3 (De cons. D. 2 c. 42). Ch. 5 bt p117.3–4: "De Consecracione, distinccione IIª, capitulo: Ego Beringarius". (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-3 | eucharistia-prol-b / n4 | Lombard *Sent.* IV; Bonaventure *In IV Sent.* | verified | 2026-11-07. Lombard: ch. 5 bt p119.29–p120.11 quotes "Magister Sentenciarum IVº, distinccione Xª in principio: Sunt item alii precedencium insaniam transcendentes...". I read PL 192, 859 (Sent. IV d. 10 c. 1, "Sunt et alii praecedentium insaniam transcendentes"). Loserth foot 119.31: "Cf. Petrum Lombardum dist. X". Bonaventure is named at 120.14 ("recitant ut heresim posteriores doctores, scribentes super Lumbardi sentencias et specialiter Thomas et Bonaventura", i.e. on IV d. 10) and at 135.3. Loserth gives no locus, and Bonaventure's In IV Sent. d. 10 was not opened. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-4 | eucharistia-prol-b / n5 | Anselm: an accident cannot be without a subject | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 5 bt p130.3–p131.29. Anselm *De veritate* c. 13 (Wyclif "capitulo ultimo"; PL 158, 484D–485A, read): "Existente namque corpore, colorem ejus necesse est esse; et pereunte corpore, colorem ejus manere impossibile est". This is said by the DISC., which Wyclif himself notes ("in persona discipuli"). The *De corpore Christi* text (130.4–19) is Loserth's "Sancti Anselmi Epistolarum Lib. IV, Ep. CVII". Not found in local PL 158–159 by phrase. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-5 | eucharistia-prol-b / n5 | Augustine on quantity | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 5 bt p133.1–11: "ut patet Vᵗᵒ de Trinitate IXº et XIIIº: Sicut ergo non dicimus tres essencias, ita non dicimus tres magnitudines...". I read PL 42, 918 (De Trin. V.10.11): "Sicut ergo non dicimus tres essentias; ita non dicimus tres magnitudines... In rebus enim quae participatione magnitudinis magnae sunt". Loserth: "De Trin. V, 8... ib. V, 10". (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-6 | eucharistia-prol-b / n7 | Thomas *ST* III q. 75 a. 2 (three-membered argument, porism, "heretical") | verified | 2026-11-07. Wrong locus. Ch. 5 bt p137.10–p138.4 quotes "sanctus Thomas super IVº, distinccione XIª, articulo primo: Hec... posicio... est incompetens, inpossibilis et heretica". Loserth foot: "S. Thomae, Sup. Quart. Sent., dist. XI, q. II [sic], art. I... Cf. et eiusdem Summa [III], Quaest. LXXV, Art. II". I read corpusthomisticum snp4011, In IV Sent. d. 11 q. 1 a. 1 qc. 1 co.: "incompetens est huic sacramento, et impossibilis, et haeretica", "impossibile est aliquid esse nunc cum prius non fuerit". The "trimembre argumentum" (142.27) is the threefold incompetence. The "porisma" (146.6–9) is the impossibility premiss. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-7 | eucharistia-prol-b / n9 | Innocent III's decretal = *Firmiter* (Lateran IV c. 1; X 1.1.1) | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 5 bt p141.11–29 ("ecclesia tempore Innocencii tercii loquens de transsubstanciacione... concordare decretalem Innocencii et decretum"). I read Friedberg X 1.1.1 *Firmiter* ("Innocentius III. in concilio generali, cap. 1": "transsubstantiatis pane in corpus"). (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-8 | eucharistia-prol-b / n10 | Gregory on the paschal lamb = *Hom. in Evang.* 22 | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 6 bt p164.8–20: "Exod XIIº... in esu agni paschalis ista 14ᶜⁱᵐ observentur, ut exponit Gregorius Super Evangelia Omelia XII: Quis... sit sanguis agni, non iam audiendo sed bibendo didicistis". Loserth: "S. Gregorii In Evangel. lib. II, Hom. XXII". I read PL 76, ~1178 (Hom. XXII): "non jam audiendo, sed bibendo didicistis". (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-9 | eucharistia-prol-b / n14 | Rabanus | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 6 bt p187.5–11: "Rabanus libro De Naturis rerum, libro Vº, capitulo XI... Aliud est sacramentum, aliud virtus sacramenti...". Loserth: "Rab. Maurus, De Universo lib. V, cap. XI". I read PL 111, 136A (De universo V.11 "De sacramentis divinis"), verbatim. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-10 | eucharistia-prol-b / n18 | Augustine, "the bread is the body of Christ" | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 7 bt p227.10–17: "patet per beatum Augustinum, Sermone LXIº pro feria IIª Pasche, et ponitur in De consecracione, distinccione IIª:... Non... omnis panis sed accipiens benediccionem Christi fit corpus Christi". Loserth: "S. Augustini Sermo CCXXXIV... Decret. Tert. Pars, dist. II, cap. LXI". I read Friedberg De cons. D. 2 c. 61 ("Non omnis panis, sed accipiens benedictionem Christi"). Augustine's sermon itself was not read; the Gratian text is the one quoted. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolb-11 | eucharistia-prol-b / n22 | a miracle of Ambrose | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 8 bt p244.1–3: "ut narratur de beato Ambrosio ipsum fuisse pro eodem tempore in locis distantibus et officium ecclesiasticum celebrasse"; the reply is at 245.25–246.14. Loserth foot: "Cf. Vitam S. Ambrosii a Paulino... cap. 49". I read PL 14, 44A–B: c. 49 has an appearance to holy men on the day of his death, not a celebrating in two places. The story that fits is Gregory of Tours, *De miraculis S. Martini* I.5, which I read at PL 71, 918C–919A: Ambrose, asleep at the altar in Milan, attends Martin's funeral. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |

I read corpusthomisticum snp2002, In II Sent. d. 2 q. 1 a. 1 ad 4: "numerus discretus vicissitudinis non continuae". (2) bt p250.28–251.10 "super IIº Sentenciarum, distinccione IIIª, questione XXVª"; Loserth: "S. Thomae Super dist. II [sic], lib. II Sent., Quaest. II... tom. VIII, pag. 40". (2) was not opened because the web page was truncated. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |

Ch. 9 bt p272.17–19: "decretalis Innocencii tercii ad cuius edicionem fuerunt mille CCC et XV prelati", so the number is Wyclif's and recurs in the chapter. *Firmiter*, X 1.1.1, was read in Friedberg (as Kprolb-7). | Kprolc-2 | eucharistia-prol-c / n2 | the decree of Nicholas = *Ego Berengarius*, *De cons.* D. 2 c. 42 | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 9 bt p279.11–15: "Nicholai IIⁱ testimonium et sinodus... (ut patet De Consecracione, distinccione IIª, Ego Beringarius)". Friedberg D. 2 c. 42 as Kprola-3. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolc-3 | eucharistia-prol-c / n3 | Augustine against the Donatists | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 9 bt p280.17–282.6: the *fundamentum Augustini contra Donatistas*, that the church is not to be burdened beyond scripture, and "nulli creature credendum est nisi de quanto fundaverit se in scriptura (ut dicit Augustinus)". Loserth foot 282.4: "S. Augustini Contra Faustum XIII, 5... De Baptismo contra Donatistas II, 3, Opp. IX, 98". I read PL 43 De bapt. II.3.4: "Quis autem nesciat sanctam Scripturam canonicam... omnibus posterioribus episcoporum litteris ita praeponi". The baptism-not-iterated argument at 281.19–24 has no Loserth locus and is not traced further. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolc-4 | eucharistia-prol-c / n4 | Lanfranc's three evidences (*De corpore et sanguine Domini*?) | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 9 bt p283.18–p284.2: "Lanfrancus abbas Beccensis in libello suo contra Beringarium De Sacramento Altaris... evidenciam triplicem". (1) manna, (2) foretelling the future vs narrating the past, (3) decrees. Loserth foot 283.20: "Lanfranci Opp. ed. Giles II, 163". I read PL 150, 440C (De corpore et sanguine Domini c. 22): "Quis enim nesciat manna... parva buccella panis... praestantiores exstitisse? Rursus quis non intelligat divinius fuisse praenuntiare futura quam narrare praeterita... idiota". (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| Kprolc-5 | eucharistia-prol-c / n7 | Damascene = *De fide orthodoxa* IV.13 | verified | 2026-11-07. Ch. 9 bt p310.7–16: "Damascenum. Scribit enim iste Grecus IIIº Sentenciarum suarum, capitulo...: Quemadmodum consuetudo est homini aqua lavari et oleo ungi...". Loserth foot: "Non sunt Sentenciarum libri, sed De Fide Orthodoxa lib. IV... cap. XIII", with the Latin quoted. I read the English (NPNF II.9, ccel.org, Exp. fid. | K01a-2 | eucharistia-01-a / n1 | the three-membered distinction from Lombard *Sent.* IV d. 8 | verified | 2026-11-07. PL 192, 856–857 (Sent. IV d. 8 c. 4), read: "Sunt ergo hic tria distinguenda: unum, quod tantum est sacramentum; alterum, quod est sacramentum et res; et tertium, quod est res et non sacramentum". This matches Wyclif 11.3–10 (nudum sacramentum / sacramentum et rem / rem sacramenti, the union with the mystical body = Lombard's "mystica ejus caro"). (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| K01a-3 | eucharistia-01-a / n5 | 12.14–17 = *Lauda Sion* (Daniel, *Thes. hymnol.* II, 98) | verified | 2026-11-07. Loserth foot 12.14–17: "E sequentia S. Thomae de Aquino: Lauda Sion Salvatorem. Cf. Daniel, Thes. hymnol. II, 98". I read the text of the sequence (Latin text via Wikipedia/EWTN): "Fracto demum sacramento, ne vacilles, sed memento, tantum esse sub fragmento, quantum toto tegitur". Wyclif reads "Non vacilles". (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
| K01a-4 | eucharistia-01-a / n5 | 13.15–17, 13.22–24 and 15.5–7 also *Lauda Sion* | verified | 2026-11-07. Same text of the sequence. 13.15–17 "Quod non capis, quod non vides, animosa firmat fides, praeter rerum ordinem". 13.21–24 "Nulla rei fit scissura, signi tantum fit fractura, qua nec status nec statura signati minuitur". 15.5–7 "Sub diversis speciebus, signis tantum, et non rebus, latent res eximiae". All three are stanzas of *Lauda Sion*. (see `chunks/eucharistia/_verify/RESULTS-1.md`) |
